# INVARIANTS.md — Behavioral & Schema Invariants

Load this file when the change touches: routing/scroll-shell, dark mode, RLS, `set_logs` schema, rest timer, Web Push and the Edge Functions, messaging/notifications triggers and realtime, offline mutations and what the device stores, auth and sign-out, the service worker, an update or a deploy, coach session review, stats persistence, the role-aware `UserMenu`, or anything to do with **what comes next / when a session was trained** (the queue model, `performed_on`/`performed_at`, day strips). Otherwise stay in `CLAUDE.md`.

## Shell, routing, viewport

- **HashRouter intentional** (GitHub Pages). URLs contain `/#/`. Vite `base` is `/sl_app/`. Pages are `React.lazy`-loaded in `routes.jsx`.
- **Bare `/sl_app/` (no hash) redirects to the role home — and waits for a role it does not know yet.** iOS PWAs cold-launch at the manifest `start_url` (`/sl_app/`), not at the last-visited hash route, so an authenticated re-launch lands at HashRouter pathname `/`. The `RedirectToRoleHome` index route inside `AppShell` (in [routes.jsx](../src/routes.jsx)) bounces to `/${role}` — without it, the wildcard catches `/` and renders `NotFound`. Unauthenticated users are intercepted earlier by `ProtectedRoute → /login`, so whoever reaches the route is signed in: a `role` of `null` there is a role that has not been read yet, and the route renders a spinner (with `authOffline.roleWait` when the device is offline) until `useAuth` has one. It must never send that user to `/login`: `LoginPage` shows its form to anyone without a role, and an installed app opened without a network would end on a sign-in form that cannot work offline. `src/routes.test.jsx` holds the wait, `src/routes.offline.test.jsx` the whole cold start on the real provider.
- **Outer page must not scroll.** `html, body, #root` are `overflow: hidden; position: fixed; inset: 0`. `AppShell` is `h-full flex-col` (NOT `h-dvh` — iOS Safari disagrees and spawns a second scrollbar). Only `main` scrolls. Don't touch — this keeps the mobile keyboard from pushing the sticky `BottomNav` mid-page. The inner wrapper inside `main` is `h-full flex flex-col` (not `min-h-full`) so flex-1 + min-h-0 children — currently only Messages thread/composer — get a hard height budget; pages with naturally tall content still scroll via `main` because it has `overflow-y-auto`.
- **iOS Safari auto-zooms focused `<input>` / `<textarea>` / `<select>` < 16px.** Every text-entry form element is 16px. Don't drop below. File pickers, radios, checkboxes, sliders unaffected.
- **A number a person types is `DecimalInput`, read by `parseDecimalInput` — never `<input type="number">`, never `Number()` or `parseFloat()` on the text of a field.** A number field decides what "82,5" means by the language of the browser: 825 in a Chromium whose UI is English, `""` in WebKit, and `""` was stored as null over a logged weight. [`lib/number.js`](../src/lib/number.js) reads a comma or a point as the one separator and refuses everything else (`{ ok: false, reason }`); its `value` is null for an empty field and for nothing else, so a write guarded by `ok` cannot put null, or another number, in place of what was typed. `Number('82,5')` is `NaN` and `parseFloat('82,5')` is 82. The caller holds the text and parses when it commits; `decimalText` seeds the field and is never applied to the text being typed (rewriting "82.0" to "82" under the cursor makes the next key give 825). Tests: `lib/number.test.js`, `ui/DecimalInput.test.jsx`.
- **Anything pressed is 44px: a pill that is a button takes `sl-pill-btn`, a control that must stay small takes `sl-hit`.** Both are in `@layer components` of `src/index.css` and held to 44px by `src/test/hitTargets.test.js`. `sl-hit` draws its area with `::after`: it cannot share an element with an `after:` utility, an ancestor's `overflow: hidden` cuts it, and two of them nearer than 44px overlap.
- **A menu that hangs off a control inside a box with `overflow: hidden` is drawn by `PortalMenu`, never inside the box.** [`coach/PortalMenu.jsx`](../src/components/coach/PortalMenu.jsx) renders a portal on `<body>` with a fixed position read from the trigger's box and the viewport in the click handler (`anchorOf`), below the trigger, or above it when the room below is short and there is more above (`placeMenu`). The caller gives the menu's height, counted from 44px items, so nothing is measured once it is drawn and no effect sets state. A scroll (capture, any scroller) or a resize closes it, Escape closes it and gives the focus back to the trigger, and its first button takes the focus when it opens. The Program Sheet's lists are `pk-group`s, rounded and clipped: an absolutely positioned menu inside one was cut at its edge, and on the last, empty phase "Reorder phases" and "Delete phase" (the only way to delete a phase) could not be pressed. Tests: `coach/PortalMenu.test.jsx`, `coach/ProgramSheet.test.jsx` ("menus are drawn outside the card").
- **A sticky element of `main` uses `top: 0`, not the safe-area inset.** A sticky box is held inside the content box of its scroller, and `main` already pads its top by `env(safe-area-inset-top)`: 0 is below the notch, the inset would be counted twice. `ui/OfflineBanner` relies on it; `ui/OfflineBanner.sticky.test.jsx` fails when `main` stops being the scroller that carries the inset, or when an element is put between `main` and the banner.
- **A page's back button calls `useBackOr(fallback)`, never `navigate(-1)` on its own.** `navigate(-1)` is `history.back()`: on the first entry of the tab — a push notification, a pasted link, the installed app started on a deep link — it leaves the app in a browser and does nothing in an installed PWA. [`hooks/useBackOr`](../src/hooks/useBackOr.js) goes one step back when the app has a page behind, and otherwise to `fallback` with `replace`. It reads `history.state.idx`, the number the router gives its entries, not `location.key`: a redirect on arrival (`<Navigate replace>`, the return from login) keeps idx 0 under a new key. A test of a screen that asserts `navigate(-1)` under a single-entry `MemoryRouter` is asserting the fallback branch. The coach's deep views keep their explicit destinations (ARCHITECTURE, "Back-button routing in coach deep views"). The athlete's three deep pages — `student/SessionView` (both of its top bars: the session and the page that says it could not be loaded), `student/MyGoals` and `student/StudentProfile` — use `useBackOr('/student')`.
- **Tailwind 4, CSS-based config.** `@theme` + `@custom-variant` in `src/index.css`. No `tailwind.config.js`. The Parc tokens reach Tailwind through `@theme inline` in the same file (`bg-ground`, `text-ink-2`, `rounded-group`, `min-h-target`…), each utility reading the variable of `src/styles/parc-tokens.css`; `src/test/parcTokens.test.js` holds that file to `design/parc/tokens.json`. `src/styles/bundle.css` (the `pk-*` classes) is imported into the components layer, so a utility beside a `pk-*` class wins over it.
- **The `.dark` remaps of `src/index.css` render nothing any more.** No `dark` class is set since the Parc foundation; the remaps, `@custom-variant dark` and the old `ink-0…950` scale stay until the clean-up step of the redesign (`design/parc/HANDOFF.md`) and are not extended. A screen not yet moved to Parc draws with the light Tailwind utilities it was written with.
- **Text on an accent or danger fill is `sl-on-fill` — not `text-white`, and not `text-ink-900` or `text-gray-900`.** White is 3.12:1 on `--color-accent` and 3.35:1 on `--color-danger`. The two dark text classes look right in the source and are not: `.dark .text-ink-900` and `.dark .text-gray-900` turn them into `ink-0`, cream, 2.94:1 on accent. `.sl-on-fill` (`src/index.css`, `@layer components`) is `var(--color-ink-900) !important`, which a layer makes stronger than the unlayered `!important` of the remaps; an inline `color: 'var(--color-ink-900)'` is the other spelling that holds. `src/test/contrast.test.js` recomputes the class against both fills, and `ui/ConfirmDialog.test.jsx` fails when the confirm button carries any other text colour.
- **Muted text is the token `--color-ink-400`, and its dark value lives in the `.dark` rule — never in a `.dark .text-ink-400` remap.** The token is read by the class, by `.sl-label`, by the `gray-400` remap, by inline styles and by chart fills; a class remap would move the first and leave the rest on `#807a6e`, which is under 4.5:1 on every dark surface. `src/test/contrast.test.js` fails on such a remap, and on any `.dark` rule that paints an ink background the token does not reach 4.5:1 on. A step darker than `ink-400` written inline (`var(--color-ink-500)` and beyond) is not remapped by anything and is not a text colour: `auth/LoginPage.test.jsx` holds the login page to that.
- **An id inside a component that can be mounted twice comes from `useId`.** `ui/Dialog` is mounted once per dialog of a page and renders its title whether it is open or not, so a literal id there is a duplicate id and `aria-labelledby` names the dialog after the first title in the document. `ui/Dialog.test.jsx` mounts two and three dialogs and fails on a shared id.
- **The app is LIGHT-ONLY (the Parc design system, `design/parc/`) and there is no theme toggle.** [index.html](../index.html) ships no theme class and `color-scheme: light`; `ThemeProvider` takes away a `dark` class something would have left and reports `theme: 'light'` — `toggleTheme`/`setTheme` are deliberate no-ops so a stale caller cannot flip it, and a `sl_app_theme` left in localStorage from the old toggle is ignored. Reason: one theme is also the only one whose strip can match, since an installed iOS PWA colours its status bar from the manifest's single static `theme_color`. New UI is written with the Parc tokens and the `pk-*` classes, never with a `dark:` variant.
- **The iOS status-bar / Dynamic Island strip is coloured by the MANIFEST, and nothing else.** An installed iOS web app ignores `theme-color` metas entirely — those only govern in-browser tabs — so `theme_color` in [vite.config.js](../vite.config.js) is `#FFFFFF`, Parc's `surface`, and `background_color` (the splash) is `#E3E6E4`, its `ground`. The metas of index.html and `ThemeProvider` say `#FFFFFF` too.
- **`manifest.webmanifest` must stay OUT of the service-worker precache.** [src/sw.js](../src/sw.js) filters it out of `__WB_MANIFEST`. While it was precached, Safari asked the worker for the manifest and got the stale copy back, so a `theme_color` change never reached iOS *even after removing and re-adding the app* — which made the strip colour look unfixable for several rounds. Nothing offline depends on it: the manifest matters at install time and iOS keeps its own copy after that.
- **Do not add `apple-mobile-web-app-status-bar-style: black-translucent`.** It is the only thing that makes the strip track the theme toggle, and it was tried on-device — but it leaves the initial containing block one status bar short of the screen, so the shell ends early and a dead strip appears along the bottom that lifts `BottomNav` off the edge. Neither `100dvh`, nor `inset: 0`, nor dropping the legacy `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` pairing sized that away. The strip colour is not worth a broken layout.
- **Still true for Safari tabs and Android:** `ThemeProvider` rewrites every `theme-color` entry via `paintStatusBar` (the stored theme may disagree with `prefers-color-scheme`), [index.html](../index.html) keeps a **media-less** `theme-color` last as a fallback for UAs that ignore `media`, and `html` itself is painted — not just `body` — since with `viewport-fit=cover` the safe-area strips fall outside the AppShell's painted box.

## React / hooks discipline

- **Rules of Hooks:** never call `useMemo` (or any hook) after an early return like `if (isLoading) return <Spinner/>`.
- **`useWeek.js` owns every week AND session mutation** — `useCreateWeek`, `useUpdateWeek`, `useDeleteWeek`, `useReorderWeeks`, and `useCreateSession` / `useUpdateSession` / `useArchiveSession` / `useDeleteSession`. Not `useSession.js` (which owns the session *tree* read and its slots), and not `useProgram.js` (which owned `useCreateWeek` until it was moved back here).
- **Any write that changes a week, a session or an exercise slot must invalidate `['program']`.** The Program Sheet (the Programming tab) renders the whole block — week labels, session titles, weekday pills and per-session exercise counts — from `useProgram`'s `['program']` entry. `['week', id]` is now read by almost nothing, so invalidating only that is a silent no-op: the write reaches the DB and the sheet keeps rendering the stale value until its staleTime expires. This bit `useUpdateSession` (a day move wrote through but the pill did not change), `useArchiveSession`, `useDeleteSession`, `useAddSlot` and `useDeleteSlot` — all now drop `['program']` too. Add it to any new session/slot mutation.
- **Any write that moves a session in or out of a calendar-week slot must invalidate `['coach-dashboard-programs']`** — the Athletes-roster week strips come from their own summary query, so they don't refresh off `['program']` or `['week']`. Use the exported `invalidateCoachDashboard(qc)` from [useProgram.js](../src/hooks/useProgram.js) rather than repeating the key.
- **Never fan a list of session ids back into `.in('session_id', […])`.** The GET URL grows ~37 bytes per id and hard-fails once a coach accumulates a few hundred sessions. Filter through the embedded join instead (`session:sessions!inner(week:weeks!inner(program:programs!inner(…)))` + `.eq('session.week.program.…')`), as the coach's confirmation reads of `useSessionConfirmation.js` (`coachConfirmations`, behind `useReviewInbox`, `useConfirmationTotals` and `useConfirmationHistory`), `useWeekConfirmedSessionIds` and `useStudentProgressStats` all now do.
- **Counting rows means `{ count: 'exact', head: true }`, never `data.length`.** PostgREST silently truncates an unbounded response at `db-max-rows` (1000) and returns no error, so a length-based count reads as a plausible wrong number. If the rows themselves are needed, read them with `fetchAllRows` (next bullet).
- **A read whose result grows with an athlete's history goes through `fetchAllRows`, never through `.limit(<a big number>)`.** PostgREST answers one request with at most `db-max-rows` rows (1000 in production) and reports nothing when it cuts; a client limit can only lower that ceiling. [`fetchAllRows(buildQuery, { pageSize, column, ascending })`](../src/lib/supabasePaging.js) asks again until a page comes back short. It pages by KEYSET — `order=<column>.asc`, then `<column>=gt.<last key>` — because an offset shifts when a row is inserted between two requests and a value does not; rows are de-duplicated on the key. Four rules follow. `buildQuery` returns a NEW query on every call, with its filters and without `.order()`, `.limit()` or `.range()`. The select must include the key column (`id` by default) — a row without it throws on the first page, not on the day a second page is needed. The key must be unique among the rows the query matches (`id`; `logged_on` within one athlete's `bodyweight_logs`; `session_id` in `session_confirmations`). And the result comes back in KEY order, which for a uuid is no order: a caller that needs dates sorts the rows itself (`useStudentProgressStats` sorts confirmations newest first, `readDoneSetHistory` sorts sets oldest first). A full page is never taken for the end, and a full page that brings nothing new throws instead of stopping. Today's callers: `reviewInboxQueryOptions` in `useSessionConfirmation.js` (the coach's inbox), `useConversations` in `useMessages.js` (every message of the account, on a database without `my_conversations()`), `useStudentProgressStats` (done sets, swaps, confirmations), `readDoneSetHistory` in `lib/doneSetHistory.js` (the done sets and swaps that `useStudentRecords` and `useLastPerformance` derive from), `fetchBodyweightSeries` in `useBodyweight.js` (the bodyweight series behind `useStudentRecords`), `useStudentLifetimeStats` (volume rows), `useStudentHistoricalSessions` (confirmations). `src/lib/supabasePaging.test.js` reads every source file and fails on a `.limit(n)` with `n` above `PAGE_SIZE`. `src/lib/supabasePaging.test.js` reads every source file and fails on a `.limit(n)` with `n` above `PAGE_SIZE`; its `WAITING` list is empty.
- **`PAGE_SIZE` (1000) must not exceed the project's API "Max rows" setting.** The loop ends on a page shorter than the page size, so a server that caps lower makes every page look like the last one and the truncation is back, silently, for every caller. If Max rows is ever lowered in the dashboard, lower `PAGE_SIZE` in `src/lib/supabasePaging.js` in the same change. Raising Max rows needs nothing.
- **A coach's list that grows with the history of every athlete is read in full only where the coach must act on all of it; the rest is a page at a time, when it is shown.** The Sessions feed's inbox — the confirmed sessions neither reviewed nor archived — is read in full by `fetchAllRows` (`reviewInboxQueryOptions` in [`useSessionConfirmation.js`](../src/hooks/useSessionConfirmation.js)), and sorted newest first by the client. "All" and the archived drawer are `useConfirmationHistory`: a `useInfiniteQuery` over `.range()`, `HISTORY_PAGE_SIZE` (30) per page, ordered `confirmed_at` then `id` descending so the order is total, read only while its view is open (`enabled`), the athlete filter and the archived flag sent to the server. An offset moves when a confirmation arrives between two pages: the flattened pages are de-duplicated on `id`. The empty states and "Show N archived" come from `useConfirmationTotals` — two head counts (`count: 'exact', head: true`), never a length of rows. One request for every confirmation, newest first, is what dropped an old session that was still waiting for its review out of the inbox, the chip and the tab's number once the coach's athletes had confirmed a thousand sessions since (review item L18).
- **An optional read function of the database is asked first, and its absence — `PGRST202`, and nothing else — falls back to a paged read with the same answer.** `useReviewCounts` calls `coach_review_counts()` and, without it, counts the inbox with `reviewCountsByStudent` through `queryClient.fetchQuery(reviewInboxQueryOptions(coachId))`, so the feed then finds the inbox read; `useConversations` calls `my_conversations()` and, without it, reads every message of the account with `fetchAllRows` and folds them. Any other error fails the read: a refused function is never an empty count or an empty list. The function and the fallback must give the same numbers — a change to one is a change to the other (`supabase/tests/coach-list-rpcs.test.js` holds the database side, `src/hooks/useSessionConfirmation.test.jsx` and `src/hooks/useMessages.test.jsx` the client side).
- **A hook test that reads a growing table mocks Supabase with `fakePostgrest`, not with a resolved value.** A `mockResolvedValue` hands the whole fixture back whatever the request says, which is how three hooks passed their tests while cutting at 1000 rows in production. [`fakePostgrest(tables)`](../src/test/fakePostgrest.js) serves tables by name, sorts on `.order()`, applies `.gt()` / `.lt()`, and never returns more than `maxRows` (1000) rows whatever `.limit()` it is sent; every other filter is recorded on `db.requests`, not applied, so the fixture is what the WHERE clause lets through and the test asserts the filters. A fixture of 1001 rows is then enough to prove a read pages.
- **Test auth is mocked, never provided:** `useAuth`'s context is module-private, so wrapping a component in your own `AuthContext.Provider` silently provides a value nothing reads and the component renders signed-out. Always `vi.mock('../../hooks/useAuth', ...)` instead. `src/test/utils.jsx` exports only `createTestQueryClient`; supply Router/Theme wrappers per test as needed. `matchMedia` polyfill lives in `src/test/setup.js`.
- **Who is signed in comes from the device first; `auth.getSession()` only confirms it.** `AuthProvider` ([`hooks/useAuth.jsx`](../src/hooks/useAuth.jsx)) takes its first `user` from the session supabase-js stores (`readStoredUser` in [`lib/storedSession.js`](../src/lib/storedSession.js): `localStorage[supabase.auth.storageKey]`, read synchronously) and its first `profile` from [`lib/profileCache.js`](../src/lib/profileCache.js) (`sl_profile_cache`: `{ id, role, full_name }` of ONE account, the last row read or written; handed out only for that id and only with a role the app has). With both, `isLoading` is `false` from the first render and nothing is waited for — so what a page starts at once cannot count on supabase-js holding the session's token yet: the Realtime channels wait for it ("A channel joins with the session's token", under "Messaging / notifications"). Do not go back to starting from `getSession()`: with an expired access token — an hour after the last refresh, so at most launches of an installed app — auth-js first attempts a refresh, for about 25 s without a network, twice at start-up (its own initialisation, then the call), and then answers `{ session: null, error }` although the session is still stored. The rules for what auth-js says afterwards:
  - **Only `SIGNED_OUT` signs the user out** (and a `getSession()` that answers "no session" *without* an error). A `getSession()` that answers with an error, or rejects, is "could not tell": the provider reads the stored session again (`asStored`) — auth-js keeps it when a refresh got no answer (`AuthRetryableFetchError`) and removes it itself, with a `SIGNED_OUT`, when the server refused. An event that carries no session and is not `SIGNED_OUT` (the `INITIAL_SESSION` of such a launch) says nothing about who is signed in.
  - **An event about the account that is already signed in changes nothing**: same `user` object, no request. auth-js emits `SIGNED_IN` on every return to the tab and `TOKEN_REFRESHED` every hour; a new `user` object re-renders every consumer of `useAuth`. `USER_UPDATED` is the exception (a new e-mail address). Consequence: `user` is the object of the first sighting — read `user.id` and `user.email` from it, nothing that moves with a token.
  - **A profile that is known is never replaced by a failed read**, and the profile is read once per account and launch (`ensureProfile`), again only after a failure: when the network returns (`online`), at the next auth event about the account, and every `ROLE_RETRY_MS` while no role is known at all. Offline it is not attempted. An answer that arrives after the account changed is dropped. `updateProfile` writes the cache too.
  - **`isLoading` means "not known yet who is signed in, or in which role"**, and it never waits for a request that cannot be made: a signed-in account whose role this device has never read goes through with `role: null` when the device is offline (the index route waits, a deep link renders — `RoleGate` lets an unknown role pass).
  - **The cached profile is a copy for the screen.** Anyone can edit their own `localStorage`; nothing is allowed because of it, and no client check may stand in for a policy.
  - **`lib/storedSession.js` follows the library**, and `src/lib/storedSession.test.js` holds it to the real client (stubbed transport): the storage key and the shape of what is stored, "no session" after a refresh that got no answer with the session left in place, the session removed after a refused refresh, and `auth.signOut()` leaving the device signed in when the logout request gets no answer. `src/hooks/useAuth.authjs.test.jsx` runs the provider itself on that client, with time faked: a cold load on a good token (one profile request with a network, no request without), two minutes offline on an expired token, the network back, a refused refresh, the three kinds of sign-out — and the offline one at four moments, one inside each of auth-js's refresh attempts, each followed by the network alone, by a sign-in that fails and by another account's sign-in. An upgrade of `@supabase/supabase-js` that fails either file changes one of the assumptions above.
- **A sign-out always ends signed out, and it is the only thing besides another account that discards what the device holds for an account.** `signOut` of `useAuth`: asks the registered guard first (`guardSignOut` — [`components/auth/SignOutGuard.jsx`](../src/components/auth/SignOutGuard.jsx), mounted once under `AuthProvider` in `App.jsx`), then releases the device's push subscription while the session is still there (`releasePushSubscription` — "Web Push" below; best-effort, four seconds at most), then asks the server (`supabase.auth.signOut()`) unless the device is offline, reads the returned error, and when the server could not be told removes the stored session itself (`forgetStoredSession`). auth-js is not told of that removal and tells no other tab: a second tab of the app learns it from the browser's `storage` event (`isStoredSessionRemoval` in [`lib/storedSession.js`](../src/lib/storedSession.js), listened to by the provider, which then reads the device again) and shows nobody as well — for that tab a session that went away, not a sign-out of its own, so it keeps what it holds for the account. The event of a removal is not proof of one by the time it arrives (that tab may have signed in again): only `readStoredUser()` says, and a session that is written is never taken from the event — who signs in is auth-js's to say (`SIGNED_IN`). `auth.signOut()` removes the stored session only once the server has answered the logout request — with `scope: 'local'` as well, in auth-js 2.103 — so ignoring its error showed the login page on a device that was still signed in at the next launch. Each completed sign-out increments `signOuts` in the context, which is how `ClearOnUserChange` tells it from a session that went away by itself (next section). After a sign-out made here only a sign-in signs the device in again (`signedOutHere`): the flag is cleared by the `SIGNED_IN` event and by nothing else — not by `signIn` itself, so an attempt with a wrong password changes nothing — and until then any other event that carries a session is not heard, the session it stored is removed again, and a late answer of the start-up `getSession()` is dropped. The reason is the refresh of an expired token: without a network auth-js attempts it for about 25 s at a time, one attempt after the other (its initialisation, the provider's `getSession()`, the listener's first event, then its 30-second timer), so an offline sign-out nearly always finds one on its way. When the network returns before that attempt gives up, it comes back with a session, stores it and says `TOKEN_REFRESHED` — and `INITIAL_SESSION` as well, carrying the same session, when it was the attempt behind the listener's first event. The same attempt can land after ANOTHER account has signed in, and auth-js has then stored the session of the account that left over theirs: a `TOKEN_REFRESHED` about another account than the one that is signed in removes the stored session, signs that account out (`signOuts` does not move: it is not a sign-out of theirs, and what the device holds for them stays) and puts the device back under the rule above — they sign in again. A change of account is always a `SIGNED_IN` first, so a refresh about somebody else is never anything but that late attempt. Online, the sign-out is auth-js's default `scope: 'global'`, as in the app that is deployed: it ends the session on every device of the account. The other devices learn it at their next refresh (`SIGNED_OUT`, which is not a sign-out of theirs), so they keep their cache and their unsynced writes for the next sign-in of the same account; `scope: 'local'` would leave them signed in — a product choice nobody has made, to be asked before it is changed. `signOut` waits for `auth.signOut()` for as long as auth-js takes — a second on a network that answers, over a minute on one that reports online and answers nothing while a token is being refreshed. Do not race that call against a timer and fall back to `forgetStoredSession()`: it cannot be taken back, and when it finally completes it removes whatever session the device stores by then — the next account's (tried on the real client: a logout request that hangs, another account signs in, the request gets its answer, the device holds no session). The tap gets its feedback from the control: `signOut` returns a promise that settles when the sign-out is over, or was declined at the guard, and a busy state follows that promise — neither `ui/UserMenu` nor `student/StudentProfile` shows one today. Every "Sign out" of the app must call `signOut` of the context and nothing else: that is what puts it behind the guard, which opens a question (`authOffline.signOut…`) while the client holds a write of the queue that has not settled (`isQueuedWrite`) or a draft that is ahead of the server (`hasUnsyncedDraft`).

## Language of what is printed

- **A date, a time, a number, a day name and a plural are printed through [`lib/i18n/format.js`](../src/lib/i18n/format.js), with `lang` from `useI18n`.** The app has its own language switch; `toLocaleDateString()` with no locale, and `Intl.*` built with `undefined`, print in the language of the device. `LOCALES` is the one table from a language to a locale — `format.test.js` holds its keys to `LANGUAGES` — so no file builds its own (`const LOCALE = { en: 'en-US', … }`, `lang === 'fr' ? 'fr-FR' : …`).
- **A bare calendar date is formatted from the string, not from `new Date(string)`.** `scheduled_date` and `performed_on` are `YYYY-MM-DD`; `new Date('2026-10-13')` is midnight UTC, the 12th for everyone west of Greenwich. `toDate` — used by every formatter of the module — reads a bare date as that day where the device is, and a timestamp as the instant it says. The formatters return `''` for a missing or invalid value, where `Intl.DateTimeFormat.format` throws.
- **No formatter is kept between calls.** An `Intl.DateTimeFormat` keeps the time zone the device had when it was built. `format.test.js` switches the zone of the process mid-run and fails on a cached one. The day-name tables are the exception: they are built from a week in UTC and printed in UTC.
- **A plural is `pluralKey(lang, n, …)`, never `n === 1`.** French counts 0 and 1.5 as singular ("0 séance"); the rule belongs to the language. `pluralKey(lang, n, 'student.training.sessions')` picks `…sessionsOne` or `…sessionsMany` — the pair the dictionaries carry — from `Intl.PluralRules`; every category but `one` takes the plural form. A pair that is not named that way is passed as `{ one, other }`.
- **Day names come from `dayNames(lang, width)` — Monday first, index `day_number - 1`.** `'narrow'`, `'short'` and `'long'` replace `DAY_LABELS`, `DAY_FULL` and `DAY_FULL_LONG` of `lib/day.js`, which are English. The short form drops the full stop of a French abbreviation ("lun", where Intl says "lun."); `dayName(lang, n, width)` is `''` for a value that is not 1 to 7.
- **An exercise's type is drawn by `ui/TypeBadge` from `lib/exerciseTypes.js`.** A ternary on `'pull'` draws every other type as a push. The list is not the database's enum and may be behind it: a type it does not have is drawn neutral under its own id. It may never be ahead — `lib/exerciseTypes.test.js` fails on an id that `exercise_type` in `supabase/schema.sql` lacks.
- **These keys are not seen by `lib/i18n/usage.test.js`**, which reads literal `t('a.b')` calls: a key built by `pluralKey`, a `labelKey` of `EXERCISE_TYPES`, a toast's `message` or `action.label`. `lib/exerciseTypes.test.js` checks its own; a counted string and a toast are checked by the test of the screen that prints them.

Not yet through `format.js`: the locale tables of `formatMessageStamp` (`hooks/useMessages`) and `formatNotificationStamp` (`hooks/useNotifications`). The day tables `DAY_LABELS` / `DAY_FULL` / `DAY_FULL_LONG` of `lib/day.js` are read by no screen since the Parc redesign, which names days through `dayName` / `dayNames`.

## `set_logs` — per-set targets, outcomes, RPE

- **Per-set targets live on `set_logs`, not `exercise_slots`.** Each set_log row stores both prescription (`target_reps`, `target_duration_seconds`, `target_weight_kg`, `target_rest_seconds`) and student actuals (`done`, `failed`, `skipped`, `rpe`, `actual_reps`, `actual_weight_kg`, `logged_at`). `set_logs.weight_kg` is the athlete's load from before the actuals: nothing writes it, and no select of `set_logs` names it — `src/lib/setLogsSelects.test.js` reads every select on `set_logs` in `src/` (chained on `.from('set_logs')` or embedded as `set_logs(…)`, constants resolved) and fails on one that does, so the column can be dropped without breaking a read. The legacy `exercise_slots.{reps, weight_kg, duration_seconds, rest_seconds}` columns are kept as deprecated mirrors of set 1 — slated for removal in a follow-up migration. `useAddSlot` materializes one set_log per planned set; `useUpdateSlot` fans uniform target edits to the logs that are still pending; `useUpdateSetTarget` writes to a single pending log; `useResetSlotToUniform` re-syncs every pending log to set 1. `isSlotUniform(slot)` and `formatSlotPrescription(slot)` in `lib/volume.js` decide compact vs. per-set rendering. On the athlete's session screen, the rest is shown once on the panel of the set in progress when every prescribed set has the same — also when their reps or loads differ — and on each row (« …, rest 2:00 », `showRest` of `SessionSetRow`) when the rests differ (`sharedRest` in [sessionModel.js](../src/components/student/sessionModel.js)); a set without a prescribed rest, and an extra set, say nothing. The headline grouping of `summarizeSlotPrescription` leaves rest out, so without the rows Dips rested 3:00 after sets 1 to 3 and 2:00 after 4 to 6 showed no rest anywhere (N5).
- **Students log off-plan actuals on `set_logs.actual_reps` / `actual_weight_kg`; the coach-owned `target_*` columns stay immutable to students.** When a student can't follow a set as written, the set's sheet ([SetSheet.jsx](../src/components/student/SetSheet.jsx), opened by a tap on its row) holds two fields prefilled from the prescribed target — on a swapped exercise from the substitute's last performance, on an extra set from the set before it (next bullets); [`useLogActual`](../src/hooks/useSetLogs.js) (offline-safe, `MUTATION_KEYS.logActual`) UPDATEs only the `actual_*` columns. On a set of the exercise as the coach wrote it, the client **normalizes a value equal to its target back to `null`**, so a populated `actual_*` there denotes a genuine deviation — what `formatActual(log)` renders, falling back to the target for the un-overridden dimension. Two kinds of set store **both values as entered**, because their targets are not what was done: a set of a **swapped** exercise (`slot_deviations.kind = 'swap'` with a substitute — the pinned `target_*` are the ORIGINAL exercise's, and `lib/doneSetHistory` nulls them, so the substitute's records and "Last time" read the logged actuals and nothing else; an empty load is stored as `0`, because `null` would read as the original exercise's load in `formatActual` and Stats) and an **extra set** (`is_student_added`, no targets). On those two `hasLoggedActual(log)` means "values were logged", not "off plan": the coach's Off-plan band in [SessionReview.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionReview.jsx) lists them. `useSetActions` gets the swap from `SessionView` as `swapped` (`deviationOf` in [sessionModel.js](../src/components/student/sessionModel.js)), computed with the test of `lib/doneSetHistory` (`kind === 'swap'` and a `substitute_exercise_id`), so what the row stores and what the history reads cannot disagree. The coach sees these in an "Off-plan" band per slot in [SessionReview.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionReview.jsx) (no new coach RLS — the existing FOR ALL policy already covers them). The shared-row coach/student column split (coach owns `target_*`, student owns actuals) was previously client-discipline only; it is now **hardened by the `pin_set_log_targets_for_student` BEFORE UPDATE trigger**, which reverts any student attempt to mutate `target_*` — and what the set IS (next bullet). Coach writes resolve a different `auth.uid()` than the slot's student, so `useUpdateSlot` / `useUpdateSetTarget` pass through untouched. `set-logs` is already in `PERSISTED_QUERY_ROOTS`, so the actuals write has offline parity for free. The two inputs are `DecimalInput` fields read by `parseDecimalInput` — the reps a whole number, the weight with a comma or a point, both inside what their columns hold (`int`, `numeric(6,2)`: `ACTUAL_REPS_RULES` / `ACTUAL_WEIGHT_RULES` in [useSetActions.js](../src/components/student/useSetActions.js)). **Text the editor cannot read is refused, never read as an empty field**: Save is disabled, an inline alert says why (`uiSharedHelpers.integerInvalid` / `decimalInvalid`), no `useLogActual` write is sent and the editor stays open on the text. Only a field left empty is null — "as prescribed" on a set of the exercise as the coach wrote it, no load (`0`) on a swapped exercise or an extra set — so garbage read as empty erased the actual a set held, or stored 0 kg. `patchForActual` writes `?? null`: the guard is the editor's, before the normalisation against the targets. Tests: `src/components/student/SetSheet.test.jsx`.
- **The athlete owns what they DID on a set; what the set IS belongs to the coach, and the athlete's own UPDATE cannot change it.** `pin_set_log_targets_for_student` (migration `2026_10_11_06_pin_student_writes.sql`, review P14) puts seven columns back to `OLD` when the writer is the athlete of the set: the four `target_*`, `is_student_added`, `exercise_slot_id` and `set_number`. `is_student_added` is what the `Students delete own set logs` policy trusts — flipped to `true`, a prescribed set could be deleted and inserted again with other targets; a set given another `set_number` or another `exercise_slot_id` leaves its place in `UNIQUE(exercise_slot_id, set_number)` free for a forged one. Whose set it is, is asked of `OLD.exercise_slot_id`, never of `NEW`: the slot is one of the columns held. **Silent**, like the pin on the targets: the UPDATE answers "1 row" and the columns keep their values — never read a row count as "written", read the row. A new column that says what a set is (a prescription column, a link to another row) joins the list in that function; a new column for what the athlete did does not. The coach is not the athlete of the set and passes through — `reconcileSetLogCount` in `src/hooks/useSession.js` renumbers the athlete's extra sets (park at `set_number + 100000`, then place) — and so does a statement without a user id. No athlete-side hook may send one of the seven columns in an `update`: it would be dropped without an error. `supabase/tests/pins.test.js` holds it.
- **Structural deviations: students can swap/skip an exercise, skip a set, or add an extra set — none of which touch the coach's prescription.** Exercise-level swap/skip live in the `slot_deviations` table (one row per slot, `UNIQUE(exercise_slot_id)`, `kind ∈ {swap,skip}`, `substitute_exercise_id` FK to `exercise_library`, held to the library of the program's coach by `trg_check_deviation_substitute_in_coach_library` — **library-only, no free-text**, so swaps stay known exercises for stats). RLS clones `slot_comments` (student owns the row; INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE ask the three questions of every athlete write — own row, active program, session neither archived nor reviewed; coach reads); offline via [`useSaveSlotDeviation`](../src/hooks/useSlotDeviations.js) (upsert/delete on the natural key, `'slot-deviations'` is in `PERSISTED_QUERY_ROOTS`). Per-set deviations are two booleans on `set_logs`: `skipped` (intentionally dropped — DB CHECK `set_logs_skipped_not_resolved` keeps it mutually exclusive with done/failed; written by [`useSetSkipped`](../src/hooks/useSetLogs.js), offline) and `is_student_added` (an extra set with NULL targets). **Adding/removing an extra set is ONLINE-ONLY** ([`useAddStudentSet`](../src/hooks/useSetLogs.js)/`useRemoveStudentSet`) — a brand-new-row INSERT can't be safely queued offline (the offline lane only UPDATE/UPSERT/DELETEs existing rows, and two offline adds would collide on `UNIQUE(exercise_slot_id, set_number)`); the UI gates it on `useOnlineStatus`, like `VideoUploadButton`. A new deviation notifies the coach via the `notify_coach_on_slot_deviation` trigger (`kind='session_deviation'`, in-app + best-effort Web Push, AFTER INSERT only so edits don't re-notify). Student UI: [SlotDeviationBar.jsx](../src/components/student/SlotDeviationBar.jsx) (swap/skip) + per-set controls in the set's sheet ([SetSheet.jsx](../src/components/student/SetSheet.jsx), writes in [useSetActions.js](../src/components/student/useSetActions.js)); coach UI: « Replaces X » / « Skipped by the athlete » under the exercise, skipped and added sets as rows, in [ReviewExercise.jsx](../src/components/coach/ReviewExercise.jsx) ([reviewModel.js](../src/components/coach/reviewModel.js) `setRows`).
- **What a tap on a set or an exercise loses has a way back, and skipping an exercise asks first.** Three places in the athlete's session (review P68, L12):
  - **Removing an extra set** (« Remove the set » in the set's sheet, `student/SetSheet`, through `removeExtra` of `student/useSetActions`) is followed by a `parcSession.toastRemoved` toast with Undo. `useRemoveStudentSet` answers `{ logId, removed }` — the row as the DELETE returned it (`.select()`), or `null` when the policy filtered the DELETE (a session closed since the page read it: no error, no row, and then no toast). Undo is `useAddStudentSet` with `restore: removed`: an INSERT with the set's own `id`, `done`, `failed`, `rpe`, `logged_at`, `failed_at`, `actual_reps` and `actual_weight_kg`, no target. A `23505` on its number (taken meanwhile by « Add a set ») reads the slot's sets and inserts after the last; its `id` already there is the set back already. The insert is online-only: without a network the Undo inserts nothing and says so (`parcSession.undoNeedsNetwork`). A video of the set is not brought back (its `set_log_videos` row went with the set). **An INSERT trigger on `set_logs` that rewrites an athlete's insert must keep these columns, or the Undo brings back an empty extra set.**
  - **Skipping a set** clears its outcome, its dates, its RPE and its actual values (`patchForSkipped(true)`), so it is followed by a `parcSession.toastSkipped` toast whose Undo writes them back, as read at the tap (`outcomeOf`): `setSkipped(false)`, then `toggleDone` with `at` = the old `logged_at` (or `setFailed` with the old `failed_at`), `setRpe`, `logActual` — queued writes on the row's FIFO scope, so they land in that order, each row they leave passes the CHECKs, and the Undo works offline. After a skip made in the set's sheet, the sheet's « Bring the set back » is the same way back and closes the toast (`takeBackSkip`, once); on a set skipped before the sheet was opened it only drops the flag. `patchForSkipped(false)` stays `{ skipped: false }`: a write queued by an older build carries it. Tests: `student/SetSheet.test.jsx`, `student/SessionView.replay.test.jsx` (the database ends with the set as it was).
  - **Skipping an exercise** (`SlotDeviationBar`) opens a `ConfirmDialog` — "Skip {name}?", the coach will be notified, the sets are hidden until the skip is undone (`sessionTouchUndo.skipExercise*`) — and `useSaveSlotDeviation` is called only on its answer: the `slot_deviations` INSERT notifies the coach, bell and push, the moment it is written. Undo deletes the row, and `retract_slot_deviation_notifications` takes back the unread notification; a push already delivered stays delivered.

  An Undo toast is pushed from the promise of `mutateAsync` or at the tap, never from a per-call `onSuccess`: `useRemoveStudentSet`'s `onSettled` reads the sets again before the mutation settles, the row has unmounted by then, and a per-call callback of an unmounted observer is never called. The toast's `onClick` runs on a row that may be gone; it calls the `mutate` of hooks the row held, which still run.
- **Relative strength (×BW) is opt-in per exercise and PR selection never folds in bodyweight.** `exercise_library.load_mode` is **NULLABLE** (`full` = logged weight IS the total load; `added` = logged weight sits on top of bodyweight, so system load = bodyweight + added; **NULL = unclassified**, behaves exactly like before — added/logged-load e1RM, no ×BW). NULLABLE (not `DEFAULT 'full'`) is deliberate: a default would silently label the weighted-calisthenics majority and show a *wrong* low ×BW on deploy. [`buildRecords`](../src/lib/records.js) leaves the PR headline + SORT on the bodyweight-independent added/logged-load e1RM (so a heavier student can't manufacture or reshuffle a PR, and the est-1RM number is unchanged from before this feature); `relStrength` (the ×BW pill) is computed SEPARATELY as the **MAXIMUM** over all qualifying sets of (system-load e1RM ÷ bodyweight at that set), via a `bodyweightAt(dateIso)` resolver from the `bodyweight_logs` series (`useStudentRecords`; coach flow resolves `profile_id` from the students row — the "Coaches read their students' bodyweight" RLS policy permits it). Tracking it as a true max — not deriving it from the added-load PR set, which needn't be the strongest RELATIVE single — is what a review caught: the PR set and the peak-×BW set can differ, and a bodyweight-only set on an `added` movement (system load = bodyweight) still counts. Unknown bodyweight → `relStrength=null`, pill omitted (never `0×`/NaN). `useLogBodyweight` MUST invalidate `['student-records']` (the series lives inside that query's `queryFn`, not its key) or a freshly-logged weight never unlocks the pill. **`useLastPerformance` and tonnage/volume (`lib/volume.js`, `useStudentProgressStats`) stay load_mode-blind by design** — ×BW and tonnage can tell slightly different stories for the same added-load lift; do NOT "fix" one to match the other. The natural next column is a `bw_fraction` for partial-bodyweight movements (push-up ≈0.64) — deferred; `text`+`CHECK` leaves room.
- **Student program authoring is an approval-gate model, ONLINE-ONLY for v1 (Phase 3.4).** `programs.created_by` (NULL for coach rows) + `status` (`draft`/`approved`, DEFAULT `approved` so the coach flow is untouched) + a `CHECK (NOT (status='draft' AND is_active))` (a draft can never be the active block). A student authors a draft's structure (weeks/sessions/exercise_slots + slot-level scalars) via ADDITIVE RLS gated on the SECURITY-DEFINER-STABLE `program_is_own_draft`/`week_is_own_draft`/`session_is_own_draft` helpers — **descendant policies MUST reach `programs` through these definers, never an inline subquery, or Postgres raises "infinite recursion in policy"** (the same firewall as the `*_for_slot`/`*_for_session` family). Students **never write `set_logs`** while drafting, and since migration `2026_10_16_20_drafts_coach_readonly.sql` (review P53) nobody else writes the tree of a draft either — see the bullet "**The tree of a draft is its author's.**" below. [`approve_program`](../supabase/schema.sql) (coach-only SECURITY DEFINER; self-authorizes with `auth.uid() IS NULL OR v_coach_id IS DISTINCT FROM auth.uid()`, where `v_coach_id` is `students.coach_id`) flips `status='approved'` FIRST — `UPDATE … WHERE id = p_program_id AND status = 'draft'`, and `{ approved: false }` when that finds no row —, then clears the four session columns, drops the sets without a target (not `is_student_added`, no `target_reps` and no `target_duration_seconds`, not `set_log_is_logged`, no video) and materializes the missing sets from the slot scalars, so `pin_set_log_targets_for_student` needs no draft exception. It answers `{ approved: true, set_logs_created: n }` as before, `n` counting the sets written again. The `pin_program_authoring_columns` BEFORE UPDATE trigger makes `created_by` **and `student_id`** immutable for everyone (pinning `student_id` blocks a draft author from re-pointing their draft to another student — tenant isolation; the security review caught this) and blocks the author (`auth.uid() = created_by`) from moving `status`/`approved_at` — only the coach can approve — and from changing `submitted_at` once it is set (next bullets). Verified live: a student can build their own draft and cannot self-approve or draft for another student; a non-coach `approve_program` is rejected. **Authoring is OFFLINE-CAPABLE (3.4d):** the draft is edited in an optimistic `['draft-tree', programId]` cache with **client-minted `crypto.randomUUID()` ids**, and every edit dispatches ONE keyed `saveDraftTree` mutation carrying the WHOLE flat snapshot to [`save_draft_tree(p_tree jsonb)`](../supabase/migrations/2026_07_24_save_draft_tree.sql). That RPC is **SECURITY INVOKER** (rides this same own-draft RLS + the exercise-in-coach-library WITH CHECK + the pin trigger — zero new trust surface), UPSERTs the program row (never deletes it, so `created_by`/`student_id`/`approved_at`/the one-draft index survive) and **DELETEs children by `program_id` then re-INSERTs from the jsonb** — a declarative full-replace that is **idempotent + reorder-proof** ONLY because a draft carries no `set_logs`/`slot_comments`/`slot_deviations`, so the cascade is inert (re-running a snapshot converges; stacked snapshots replay FIFO to the same end state; the `weeks(program_id,week_number)`/`sessions(week_id,sort_order)` uniques never transiently collide). The submit edge is a SEPARATE `UPDATE ... WHERE submitted_at IS NULL` so `notify_coach_on_program_submit` (AFTER UPDATE) fires exactly once even when create+submit happen in one offline session (an AFTER UPDATE trigger never fires on the INSERT branch). The five per-edit hooks are plain optimistic-cache editors (no `useMutation`); only `useSaveDraftTree`/`useDiscardDraft` are keyed (dedicated `draft-tree` FIFO scope, isolated from the set-log `offline-writes` lane). Persisted roots gain `my-draft`/`draft-tree`/`exercise-library`. Approval-race (`draft_not_editable`)/two-device (`23505`)/RLS (`42501`) → the server's version wins: the tree's unsynced marker is dropped and both draft caches are invalidated; `exercise_unavailable` and any other refusal → the tree is kept, with a toast; no answer → the tree is kept and sent again. **Between an edit and the answer of the database the cache is the only copy of the edit, and three rules protect it — the bullets "A draft tree that holds edits the database does not have…" under "Offline-safe student writes".** The save's onError/onSuccess/`skipErrorToast` live in the mutation DEFAULTS (`lib/offlineMutations.js`), not the hook, so a hydrated post-reload resume behaves identically (not silent). The XOR `CHECK` on `exercise_slots` (exactly one of reps/duration) is enforced at THREE layers — `SlotRow.commit` clamps reps ≥ 1, `buildSnapshot` re-coerces, and the RPC coerces again — because a both-null slot would abort the whole-tree replay. Verified live under RLS: idempotent re-run stays 1/1/1, coach notified once, non-owner write RLS-blocked. **The submit→review loop (3.4c):** the student sets `submitted_at` (own-draft UPDATE) → the `notify_coach_on_program_submit` AFTER UPDATE trigger fires ONLY on the `submitted_at` NULL→set transition of a `draft` (so `approve_program`, which sets `submitted_at=NULL`, never re-fires it) and notifies the coach (`program_submitted`, bell + best-effort push). The coach then either `approve_program` (→ `program_approved`, lands **approved + inactive**; the coach Sets active separately — the copy must NOT claim auto-activation) or [`send_back_program`](../supabase/migrations/2026_07_22_program_submit_notify.sql) (coach-only SECURITY DEFINER, self-authorizes on `students.coach_id = auth.uid()`; clears `submitted_at` so the student can revise and re-submit, which re-fires the notify trigger; → `program_sent_back`). Both client hooks (`useApproveProgram`/`useSendBackProgram`) are online-only and invalidate on `vars.studentId`, so callers MUST pass it.
- **The tree of a draft is its author's: the coach approves a draft, sends it back, renames it, trashes it and deletes it — and changes nothing in it.** `block_draft_tree_write` (one trigger name on `weeks`, `sessions`, `exercise_slots` and `set_logs`, one `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` function per table: `block_draft_week_write`, `block_draft_session_write`, `block_draft_slot_write`, `block_draft_set_log_write`; migration `2026_10_16_20_drafts_coach_readonly.sql`, review P53) refuses with `P0001` `draft_read_only` a write that carries a user id other than `programs.created_by` on a row of a program whose `status` is `draft` — in the trash or not; an `UPDATE` is judged on its parent as it was and as it would be, so nothing moves into or out of a draft. The coach's policies on the four tables are `FOR ALL` and never look at `status`: without the trigger a coach's edit was accepted and then lost at the athlete's next `save_draft_tree` (which deletes the phases and writes them again from the phone), and the rows of a higher "Sets" — written by `reconcileSetLogCount` with the targets of a last row a draft does not have — outlived the approval without reps or load. What goes through: a request without a user id (SQL editor, migration, service role); the author, as the policies decide; a row whose parent is gone — the cascade of a delete, which is how the coach deletes a draft. The row of `programs` has no such trigger. The name sorts before every other `BEFORE` trigger of the four tables, so the refusal answers before any guard or pin. An `INSERT` is judged by its triggers before row-level security: a stranger's insert into a draft is answered `draft_read_only`, not `42501`. A program made a draft again by hand (`created_by` NULL) is written by nobody who signs in until `approve_program`. A `SECURITY DEFINER` function called by the coach is the coach: one that writes the tree of a draft for them marks it approved first, as `approve_program` does — written the other way round, `approve_program` is refused its own sets. `supabase/tests/drafts-readonly.test.js` holds it; the page `docs/runbook/migrations/20-drafts-coach-readonly.md` has the check. On the client the coach's editor and sheet offer no write on a draft ("An athlete's draft is read-only for the coach until it is approved" under "Coach session review", "The sheet of an athlete's draft offers no write" under "Coach Athletes-roster layout").
- **A session of a DRAFT carries no `reviewed_at`, no `archived_at`, no `performed_at` and no `scheduled_date` — whoever writes it.** `trg_pin_draft_session_columns` (`pin_draft_session_columns()`, `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` on `sessions`, migration `2026_10_11_06_pin_student_writes.sql`, review P13) sets the four to NULL while `programs.status = 'draft'`. `Students author own draft sessions` is `FOR ALL`: the author writes every column of the sessions of their draft, and a draft sent in with `reviewed_at` set gave, once approved, sessions that never reached the coach's "to review" list and refused feedback as already given. **The rule is keyed on the status of the program, never on who writes**: `sessions.performed_at` is written by `sync_session_performed_at`, a SECURITY DEFINER trigger that runs inside the athlete's own request — on an approved program the athlete may well have authored. A pin "for the author" or "for the athlete" undoes that write, and confirmed sessions lose their training date (`supabase/tests/pins.test.js`, "once approved, a program its athlete wrote is trained and reviewed like any other", fails on exactly that). Consequences: nobody archives or dates a session of a draft — both are done after approval; the coach's write on a draft is refused before the pin is reached (`block_draft_tree_write`, previous bullet), the author's, the owner's and the service role's are accepted and not kept; a session moved into a draft loses the four; a new session column that records what happened to a session after approval joins the list in that function. `approve_program` clears the four on the sessions of the program it approves, for the drafts written before the trigger existed. Silent, like the other pins. **The function is `STABLE`, and a later `CREATE OR REPLACE` must keep the keyword**: the policy judges the program through `week_is_own_draft` (`STABLE`), as the statement saw it when it started, and a `VOLATILE` trigger function reads it as it is by the time the row is reached — so a stamp that waits for a row lock while the coach approves was "own draft" for the policy and "approved" for the pin, and landed on the approved program. An author reaches that with the API alone, a few connections stamping at once. No single-connection test sees it; `supabase/tests/pins.test.js`, "a stamp on its way while the coach approves …", holds it on three. KNOWN residual gaps: (1) the trigger judges a session when it is written — a program flipped from `approved` back to `draft` (no path of the app does it; a coach calling the API by hand could) keeps its stamped sessions until each is next written; (2) the price of `STABLE` — a week created earlier in the SAME statement as its session (a data-modifying CTE) is not seen. The policies do not see it either, so the author is refused (`42501`) — and the coach, whose week is refused first (`P0001` `draft_read_only`); only a caller row-level security does not apply to (the owner, the service role) gets a stamped session into a draft that way. `save_draft_tree` writes the weeks, then the sessions, in two statements — keep it so.
- **A submission is made once.** For the author of a draft, `pin_program_authoring_columns` keeps `submitted_at` as it is once it is not NULL (migration 06, review B4): `notify_coach_on_program_submit` fires on every NULL → set edge, and an author who could clear the column and set it again notified the coach, bell and push, as often as they liked. The way back is the coach's — `send_back_program`, after which the author submits again and the coach is told again, once — and `approve_program` clears it with the approval; neither is the author. A "withdraw my submission" feature for the athlete is therefore an RPC with its own rule, not an UPDATE of the column. KNOWN residual gap: an author can still discard a draft, write a new one and submit it — one notification per draft.
- **Adopting a swap into the program is a coach-only, forward-only, SECURITY DEFINER RPC (Phase 3.1).** [`adopt_swap(p_slot_id, p_substitute_id, p_dry_run)`](../supabase/migrations/2026_07_18_adopt_swap.sql) is the ONLY write path (coaches have SELECT-only on `slot_deviations` and the multi-slot rewrite must be atomic). Because a definer bypasses RLS it **self-authorizes**: it refuses a caller without a user id and asserts `coach_profile_for_session(session)` is not distinct from `auth.uid()` and that `p_substitute_id` is in the *caller-coach's own* `exercise_library` (verified live: a non-coach caller gets `not authorized`). **Forward-only via a REAL ordinal bound**, NOT the optional "confirmed" flag (a student can fully train a session without ever tapping Confirm, so "unconfirmed" is not "future"). It flips `exercise_id` X→Y only on slots that are: same program, `exercise_id=X`, session not archived, a DIFFERENT session than the reviewed one, **strictly later in program order** (`week_number`/`sort_order`), **have no logged `set_logs`** (`set_log_is_logged`: done, failed, skipped, an RPE or an actual) and no set with a video, no `slot_comments` row, no `session_confirmations` row, and no `slot_deviations` row of their own. That structurally excludes the reviewed slot AND any already-trained past session, so history is never rewritten. The dialog's "past sessions keep their history" promise is truthful because dry-run and apply share this exact predicate. `set_logs.target_*` (X's numbers) are **kept as a seed** the coach retunes — never repointed. `p_dry_run=true` returns `{applied: count}` for the confirm dialog's blast radius and writes nothing; the coach passes the substitute id they SAW (race-safe vs a student undo); double-submit is a graceful 0-count no-op. Notifies the student (`kind='swap_adopted'`, bell + best-effort push). Client: [`useAdoptSwap`](../src/hooks/useAdoptSwap.js) is **online-only** (atomic server rewrite can't queue offline). This is the first Phase 3 "collaborate" increment; full student program-authoring (`programs.created_by`/`status`, student-write RLS, offline INSERT) stays deferred. `adopt_skip` shares that predicate word for word, so what its dry run counts is what its DELETE removes, and none of it is held by a delete guard: the function never raises on a slot the athlete has touched, it leaves it out.
- **A set_log has three outcome states: pending, done, failed.** `done` and `failed` are mutually exclusive (DB CHECK `set_logs_done_xor_failed`). `useToggleSetDone` and `useSetFailed` each clear the opposite flag in the same patch, so a change between outcomes never trips the constraint. A failed set may not carry an `rpe` value either (DB CHECK `set_logs_no_rpe_when_failed`); `useSetFailed` nulls `rpe` in the same patch so a student who rated a set then later marked it failed doesn't leave an orphan rating. UI ([SessionView.jsx](../src/components/student/SessionView.jsx), [SessionExercise.jsx](../src/components/student/SessionExercise.jsx), the Parc design): **a set is done by the ActionBar's « Validate set n »** — the current set, the first set with no outcome of the exercise shown (`currentLogOf` in [sessionModel.js](../src/components/student/sessionModel.js)) — or by a rating or the Save of its values given in its sheet. **A tap never makes a set failed**: a tap on a row opens the set's sheet. **Failed is a hold**: a press of `LONG_PRESS_MS` (500 ms) on a row marks a pending or a done set failed (`useLongPress` of `components/parc`, then `markFailed` of [useSetActions.js](../src/components/student/useSetActions.js)) and pushes a toast « Set n marked failed. » whose Undo puts back what the set was (done at the moment it was done, with its rating; or pending, its rest ended). A finger that travels more than `LONG_PRESS_SLOP_PX` (10 px), a `pointercancel` or a release before the delay holds nothing — the row is `touch-action: pan-y` — and the click that ends a hold that fired is not taken for a tap. The context menu of a row is the hold for whoever cannot hold (WCAG 2.1.1): a right click, the menu key or Shift+F10, and Android, which opens it on a long press — it marks the set failed and shows no menu, once per press. A row is `select-none` with no touch callout, and the sentence « Press and hold a set to mark it failed. » under the list describes it (`aria-describedby`). A failed set is cleared in its sheet (« Not failed after all »). Tests: `student/SessionExercise.test.jsx` (fake timers and pointer events), `parc/SetRow.test.jsx`. The done→failed step is one write — `patchForFailed(true)` clears `done` in the same UPDATE, so the DB CHECK never sees an intermediate both-true row. A failed set offers no rating. The gesture that marks a set done — « Validate set n », or the Save of the values of a pending set — opens the RPE sheet ([RpeSheet.jsx](../src/components/student/RpeSheet.jsx)); marking a set failed opens nothing. It is the gesture that opens it, not `log.done` turning true — see "The rest timer and the RPE sheet follow the athlete's gestures" under Rest timer. Stats in [useStudentProgressStats.js](../src/hooks/useStudentProgressStats.js) only count true `done`. **The exercise shown**: the screen opens on the first exercise with a set to do (`firstSlotToShow`); once a gesture is made on it, it stays (`pin` in `SessionView`: a set failed by a long press does not take its exercise off the screen, with the set its toast brings back); after the RPE sheet closes it moves on (`nextSlotToShow`: the partner of a superset that has a set to do — the two are done in turn —, else the same exercise while it has one, else the next exercise with one, wrapping round); a finished exercise offers « Go to {name} » in the ActionBar, and a chip of the switcher shows any exercise. « Confirm session » is a text button in the page while a set is left, and the ActionBar's main action once every set is dealt with — one at a time. The confirm sheet counts the sets dealt with (`isLogResolved`: `skipped OR failed OR (done AND rpe != null) OR its slot has a skip deviation`, plus `done` without a rating) — hand-inlining the disjunction is how skipped sets once capped the count below the total for every deviated session.
- **An RPE is a number from 1 to 10 in steps of 0.5; the athlete is offered 6 to 10.** `set_logs.rpe` is `numeric(3,1)` since migration `2026_10_16_21_rpe_half_steps_day_check.sql` (review L34): `set_logs_rpe_check` holds the range and `set_logs_rpe_half_step` (`rpe * 2 = trunc(rpe * 2)`) the half steps; `numeric(3,1)` rounds a value to one decimal before either rule is asked (7.04 is stored as 7.0, 7.26 as 7.3 and refused). The API hands it over as a JSON number — `8.0`, which JavaScript reads as `8`, and `7.5` — so a client that writes whole numbers reads them back unchanged. SQL that builds text from one prints `8.0` (`trim_scale(rpe)` prints `8`), and a function that holds one in a variable declares it `numeric`: an `integer` rounds 7.5 to 8 without a word. A set with an RPE of 7.5 is logged like any rated set (`set_log_is_logged`). The rollback of migration 21 rounds a half step up. [RpeChoices.jsx](../src/components/student/RpeChoices.jsx), in the RPE sheet and the set's sheet, offers `RPE_VALUES` of [lib/rpe.js](../src/lib/rpe.js) (6, 6.5 … 10, nine 52 px buttons in rows of five) and gives `onChange` the number; `setRpe.mutate({ logId, rpe: 7.5, done? })` writes it as it is. A rating that is not offered — 1 to 5 from before half steps, or sent by an older build — is drawn first in the tenth cell, pressed, so it stays on screen and can be taken back. A rating is printed with `formatRpe(value, lang)` ("7.5", "7,5"), in the grid and on the set's pill. Compare ratings as numbers (`Number(value)`), never as text.
- **An RPE or an Actual given on a PENDING set marks it done, in the same write.** The athlete who rates a set, or writes down what it was, has done it: written alone, the rating left the set pending, and a session rated set by set was confirmed with no set done — no volume, no record, no "Last time", every set "not done" for the coach. On a pending set (neither done nor failed) [useSetActions.js](../src/components/student/useSetActions.js) sends `{ logId, rpe, done: true }` to `useSetRpe` and `{ logId, actualReps, actualWeightKg, done: true }` to `useLogActual`; `patchForRpe` / `patchForActual` in [`lib/offlineMutations.js`](../src/lib/offlineMutations.js) then build ONE patch with the value and `patchForDone(true, at)`, for the optimistic row and for the request alike, and a refused write takes back the rating and the tick together. Never on a done set (it would date the set again and can move `performed_on`), never on a failed one (`patchForDone` would turn it into a done set), never for a rating taken back (`null`) or an Actual cleared: there the payload is the value alone, as it was, which is also what a write queued by an older build carries. Only `done === true` is the marker. No new mutation key: the set-log writes are still five, one row each. Tests: `src/components/student/SetSheet.test.jsx`, `SessionView.replay.test.jsx` (one queued write, which lands done, rated and dated), `src/hooks/useSetLogs.tapTime.test.jsx`, `src/hooks/useSetLogs.optimistic.test.jsx`, `src/lib/offlineMutations.test.js`.
- **An extra set is done only once it says what was done.** It is inserted with no targets and nothing else (`useAddStudentSet`): values written at the insert would make an untouched extra set a logged set, which locks the coach's editor and refuses the exercise's removal. Every gesture that would mark an extra set done while it has no reps (above 0) and no load (`0` = bodyweight, no load added) — « Validate set n », an RPE — opens its sheet instead (`markDone` / `rate` answer `'values'`), and Save stays disabled, with `sessionRpeActualsExtras.extraNeedsRepsAndLoad` under it, until the inputs hold both; on a pending set that Save writes them and marks the set done (previous bullet). On an exercise prescribed in seconds an extra set has no reps to give — `set_logs` has no column for an actual duration — so only the load is asked for (`extraNeedsLoad`); `SessionView` passes the unit as `durationBased` (`isDurationBased` of `sessionModel`), read like the slot summary reads it (the first set with a target, else the slot's own columns). A done extra set keeps its values: « Back to the target » is not offered there. A done extra set without values written by an older build stays done.
- **The Actual editor of a swapped exercise and of an extra set opens on seeds, worked out by `editorSeeds` in [sessionModel.js](../src/components/student/sessionModel.js).** A swapped exercise: the substitute's last performance (`lastPerformance[substitute_exercise_id]`), set by set in set order, the last of its sets for any set beyond them; without one, the set before it in this session as it was done, and the prescribed reps for the first (the same scheme, as Stats reads it) — never the original exercise's load. An extra set: the set before it as it was done — its actual values, else its targets, and `0` kg after a set prescribed at bodyweight; an extra set after an extra set carries them on. A skipped set passes nothing on. What the set already holds always comes first. The sets of the exercise as the coach wrote it open on their own targets. The editor is the set's sheet ([SetSheet.jsx](../src/components/student/SetSheet.jsx)); `SessionView` gives `useSetActions` the context of the exercise shown (`exerciseContextOf`: `swapped`, the seeds, `durationBased`) wherever a set of it is written — the sheet, « Validate set n », the RPE sheet. Tests: `src/components/student/sessionModel.test.js`, `src/components/student/SessionView.sheets.test.jsx` (the real page and hooks).
- **A set the athlete has logged is read-only for the coach: never deleted, and its targets never rewritten.** "Logged" is ONE predicate, `isLoggedSet` in [lib/loggedSets.js](../src/lib/loggedSets.js): `done`, `failed`, `skipped`, or a non-null `rpe`, `actual_reps` or `actual_weight_kg`. It is the predicate of the `block_*_delete_with_logged_sets` triggers, and the file also holds its two query forms (`LOGGED_SET_OR_FILTER`, `onlyPendingSets`) so that no hook writes its own. A set that carries a `set_log_videos` row is protected the same way (`isProtectedSet`). In [useSession.js](../src/hooks/useSession.js) the three paths that delete rows — a lower set count (`useUpdateSlot` → `reconcileSetLogCount`), `useRemoveSet` and `useDeleteSlot` — call `readSlotLogs` and throw `loggedSetsError()` **before their first write**: `useUpdateSlot` writes `exercise_slots.sets` ahead of the delete, so a refusal that came after it would leave a slot whose count no longer matches its rows. `reconcileSetLogCount` reads again right before the delete, because the athlete can tick a set in between. The three target writes go through `onlyPendingSets`, and a write that matches no row is not an error: the database keeps a logged row's targets without raising, and the client makes the same choice. Two things to keep when editing this: **the error's message must contain "logged set"** — that substring is how `lib/mutationErrors.js` picks `errors.hasLoggedTraining`, for the client's refusal and for the database's alike — and **`useSession`'s embedded `set_logs` must select `LOGGED_SET_COLUMNS`**, because the editor renders its locks from that projection: a flag missing there is a logged set shown as editable.

## Rest timer

- **Rest timer is an app-wide singleton with one rendering site.** [hooks/useRestTimer.js](../src/hooks/useRestTimer.js) holds one `{ logId, endsAt }` in module scope, keeps a copy of it in `localStorage` (next bullet) and exposes it via `useSyncExternalStore`. Validating a new set calls `startRestTimer(logId, seconds)` which **replaces** the previous timer. Remaining seconds are derived from `endsAt - Date.now()` (timestamp-based, not a tick counter), and the hook listens to `visibilitychange` + `focus` so the timer stays correct after the tab is backgrounded or the phone is locked. `clearRestTimer(logId)` is a no-op unless the active timer matches — undoing a stale set never kills the current rest. `extendRestTimer(logId, seconds)` ("+30 s") moves the end of the running rest of that set later, and does nothing to the rest of another set, to a rest that has ended, or when there is none: a tap that lands as the rest ends must not start another one. The visible timer lives in **one** place: the ActionBar of `SessionView` (`RestControls`, the Parc `RestCountdown`), at the left of the main action, so it survives a change of exercise and stays under the thumb. While a rest runs it shows the countdown (`Rest` above `m:ss` in condensed blue digits) and « +30 s » (`extendRestTimer(logId, 30)`); there is no Skip: a rest ends by itself, or the next set validated replaces it. Once it is over, nothing is drawn — `RestTimerHost` says that it ended. `useSetActions` only writes to the singleton, from its gesture handlers (next bullet); it never reads or renders remaining time. (`RestTimerBanner.jsx` is no longer rendered; `RestTimerHost` still imports `HIDE_AFTER_EXPIRY_MS` and `formatMMSS` from it until the clean-up step of the redesign.)
- **The countdown is never in a live region; a hidden status region says when a rest starts and when it ends.** A live region whose text or label changes every second is read aloud every second by VoiceOver and TalkBack, and "Rest done" is lost in it (P44). The ActionBar's countdown is a `role="timer"`, whose `aria-live` is `off` by ARIA: its label (« Rest, 1:30 left ») is read when it is reached, never as it changes; its digits are `aria-hidden`. [RestTimerHost.jsx](../src/components/student/RestTimerHost.jsx) renders a `role="status"` element, `sr-only`, mounted with the shell and never unmounted while it lives — a region added together with its text is not announced. It holds `sessionRestControls.restStarted` ("Rest 1:30") from the start of a rest, the length read once per rest (keyed on `logId:endsAt`, so "+30 s" says the new length and the text never moves while the rest runs), `student.session.restDone` from `endsAt` to `HIDE_AFTER_EXPIRY_MS` after it, and nothing otherwise — no rest, a rest taken back or skipped, the flash over. Tests: `student/RestTimerHost.test.jsx`, `parc/ActionBar.test.jsx`, `student/SessionView.test.jsx` (the rest in the ActionBar).
- **The running rest is also in `localStorage`: written by the store on every change of the timer, read once when the module loads.** iOS kills a PWA in the background — filming a set with the Camera app is enough — and module memory goes with it. [hooks/useRestTimer.js](../src/hooks/useRestTimer.js) writes `{ logId, endsAt }` under the key `sl_rest_timer` from `commit`, the one function through which `startRestTimer`, `clearRestTimer`, `extendRestTimer` and `resetRestTimer` change the timer, and removes the key when there is no timer. **Whatever changes `endsAt` goes through `commit`**, never through `state = …`: Skip is `clearRestTimer` and "+30 s" is `extendRestTimer`, and either written beside `commit` would leave the old end time in storage — the next launch would bring back a rest the athlete had ended, or end one early. `emit` — the 1 s tick and the `visibilitychange`/`focus` refresh — goes around `commit` on purpose and writes nothing. `readStored` runs once, at module load, and takes back only a non-empty string `logId` with a finite `endsAt` strictly after `Date.now()`; a rest that ended while the app was gone, and any value the module did not write, is removed and the app starts with no timer (a "Rest done" for a rest of ten minutes ago is noise). Do not move that read into `subscribe` or `getSnapshot`: a lazy read hands a test the timer another test left. Nothing is removed at expiry while the app is open; the next load discards it. Every access is inside a `try`: storage that refuses (private mode, quota) leaves the timer running in memory. The key is private to this file and holds a set id and a time; signing out does not remove it, and it stops being read when the rest ends. Two things follow from a restored rest: the effects hook treats it as a rest that just started (wake lock, beep at `endsAt`) on whichever page of the shell the app opens on, and [hooks/useRestTimerPush.js](../src/hooks/useRestTimerPush.js) schedules a push again for the seconds that are left, once the stored cache is restored (`useIsRestoring`: the set's session is found in it). `schedule_rest_push` closes the row scheduled before the kill, so one push arrives for one rest; on a database without migration 16 the fallback insert leaves that row pending, and two pushes with the same tag can arrive.
- **The rest timer and the RPE sheet follow the athlete's gestures, never a change of the row.** [useSetActions.js](../src/components/student/useSetActions.js) holds the writes of one set and what follows them, and its callers are gestures: « Validate set n » (`markDone`: the write and `startRestTimer`, then the RPE sheet — or, on an extra set that has no values yet, nothing written and its sheet opened), the long press (`markFailed`: the write, `startRestTimer` when the set was pending), the sheet's « Not failed after all » (`clearFailed`: the write, `clearRestTimer`) and « Skip the set » (`skip` clears the rest as well). A rating (`rate`) or a Save of the values (`saveActual`) given on a pending set marks it done, so it is that set's gesture too: it starts the rest once, and the Save opens the RPE sheet. Between done and failed nothing moves: that rest is already running. There is no effect on `log.done` / `log.failed`, and there must not be one: the row changes for other reasons than a gesture — a queued write that lands, a read that comes back without it, a write that is taken back — and an effect turns each of those into a rest restarted, a sheet reopened after the athlete closed it and a rest push scheduled, once per set when a workout logged offline is replayed. The consequence to keep in mind: a tick the database turns down leaves its rest running (the row goes back to pending by a rollback, which is not a gesture). The hook is called per set by the component that holds the gesture (the row, the ActionBar's button, the sheets, each mounted with `key` = the set's id), and the set's id is the scope of its writes, so the writes of one set land in the order they were made whichever component made them. Tests: `student/useSetActions.test.jsx` (each gesture next to its "the row changes by itself" counterpart), `student/SetSheet.test.jsx`, and `student/SessionView.replay.test.jsx`, which plays gestures offline, a read without them and the replay, on the real hooks and cache. The Undo of a skip (`takeBackSkip`) starts no rest: the rest the skip cleared was the rest of a set done earlier.
