- **The coach's two lists have a read function each, which names its caller and answers what the app's fold answers.** `public.coach_review_counts()` gives `(student_id, to_review)`: one row per athlete of `auth.uid()` (`students.coach_id`) who has confirmations of sessions neither reviewed nor archived, in a block out of the trash — `student_id` is `students.id`, never the profile id that `session_confirmations.student_id` holds — and no row for an athlete with none. `public.my_conversations()` gives one row per account the caller has exchanged a message with, the newest conversation first: `counterpart_id`, `full_name` (NULL when the caller may not read that profile — a former coach after `transfer_student` — or when it is empty), `last_body`, `last_at`, `unread`, then `last_id`, `last_sender_id`, `last_recipient_id`, `last_session_id`, `last_read_at`, so that the newest message comes back in the seven `MESSAGE_COLUMNS` of `src/lib/messageCache.js`; two messages at the same time are ordered by `id`. Migration `2026_10_16_26_coach_list_rpcs.sql`, `optional`: both are `STABLE` and `SECURITY INVOKER`, in the allow-list of the `FUNCTION PRIVILEGES` block and in `RPCS` of `supabase/tests/function-acl.test.js`; a request without a user id gets no row and no error. They are the fold of `reviewCountsByStudent` (`src/lib/coachRoster.js`) over the coach's inbox, and the fold of `useConversations` (`src/hooks/useMessages.js`), taken over every row instead of the newest 1000 the API answers: `supabase/tests/coach-list-rpcs.test.js` holds each to a copy of its fold, past 1000 rows, and for a coach who is somebody's athlete. A change of what a list means changes the function, the hook and that test together. A client calls them only with a way without them — on `PGRST202`, a project that skipped the migration, it reads the rows itself — and no migration may require them. Columns are added at the end of a list, never renamed: a `RETURNS TABLE` changes only by a drop and a create (`supabase/migrations/README.md`, "A migration that adds a read function for a list the coach sees").
- **`schema.sql` and the migrations must describe the same database, and a test proves it.** `supabase/tests/parity.test.js` loads the frozen production baseline (`supabase/tests/fixtures/prod_baseline_2026_09_30.sql`), applies every `required` and `optional` migration of `supabase/migrations/manifest.json` in order, and compares the catalog with `schema.sql` object by object (`supabase/tests/catalog.js`: functions with their settings and privileges, policies, triggers, columns, constraints, indexes, table privileges, default privileges, enums, publications, comments, seeded rows). A change made to only one of the two turns it red. A table that a SQL file fills is listed in `REFERENCE_DATA` of `catalog.js`, so that its rows are compared one by one: the test refuses rows in any other table, where it could only compare how many there are. The baseline is `schema.sql` as the live review of 2026-09-30 compared it with production; its SHA-256 is pinned, and it is never edited to make the test pass. `contract` migrations are left out of `schema.sql` until they have been applied, so that `schema.sql` keeps describing production.
- **Every migration signs the ledger, and refuses to run twice or out of order.** `app_private.app_migrations (name, applied_at)` holds one row per applied migration, inserted by the migration inside its own transaction and removed by its rollback file. A migration raises `SLM01` when its name is already there and `SLM02` when the migration it requires is not; a rollback raises `SLM03` when there is nothing to roll back and `SLM04` when a later migration still needs what it would remove. A migration that brings a rule raises `SLM05` when rows that are already in the database break it and would keep the hole in use (migration 05: a `students` link whose coach is not a coach): its audit counts them, its runbook page lists and removes them, and the file is run again — it never deletes them itself. `supabase/tests/migration-conventions.test.js` runs every migration through refused-out-of-order → applied → refused twice → rolled back → applied again, and compares the catalog at each step, so a rollback that leaves an object behind fails. It then applies the list once more with the `optional` migrations left out: a `required` or `contract` migration that uses what an optional one made fails there. `schema.sql` inserts the names of the migrations merged into it, so a database installed from it starts with a complete ledger.
- **`app_private` is out of the API's reach, and has no RLS on purpose.** The schema is not in the exposed schemas and `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` hold no privilege on it (`supabase/tests/migration-ledger.test.js`). The ledger is not in `public` because a `public` table needs RLS, and a table with RLS and no policy reads as empty to a read-only role, so a check of the ledger before a deploy would read "nothing is applied". Never add `app_private` to the exposed schemas, and never grant an API role `USAGE` on it.
- **`supabase/tests/snapshots/rls-matrix.txt` is the record of who can do what.** `rls-matrix.test.js` measures, for anon, two athletes, their coach, another coach and the service role, how many rows of each table they can read, insert, update and delete, and compares the result with that file. A migration that rewrites policies or grants and means to change nothing must leave it untouched; one that means to change a cell regenerates the file and commits the diff. A cell reads `denied` when the whole statement is refused for want of a privilege — on the table, or on a function one of its policies calls: since migration `2026_10_13_14_table_grants_lockdown.sql` that is every `anon` cell of a table of `public`: without a session there is no privilege on any of them.
- **Nothing in the repository applies SQL to production, and nothing may.** It is applied one checkpoint at a time, following `docs/RUNBOOK.md` and the pages under `docs/runbook/`: a backup, then for each migration its audit, the migration, the audit again, and the ledger at the end — by the owner, or by Claude in a session the owner opens for that checkpoint, on the conditions the index gives ("Who runs a checkpoint"). No script, workflow or test runs a migration against the live project, no agent does as part of a package, and `supabase db push`, `supabase migration up` and `supabase migration repair` are never used: the folder is not a CLI migration history. A command or a query that a runbook page gives is read off the page and run by a test (`supabase/tests/rpc-auth.test.js`, `supabase/tests/function-acl.test.js`, `supabase/tests/auth-accounts.test.js`, `src/test/authConfig.test.js`), and `src/test/runbookIndex.test.js` holds the index to the manifest and to those pages — so a command is changed on its page, and the tests follow the page.
- **`profiles.full_name` is self-editable; `id` and `created_at` are pinned for everyone, `role` for every request that carries a user id.** The `Users update own profile` policy lets a signed-in user UPDATE their own row — every column of it — and the `profiles_pin_immutable_columns` BEFORE UPDATE trigger is what keeps the role out of their hands: when `auth.uid()` is not NULL it puts `role` back, **silently** (the rename in the same payload is saved, no error — read the row, not the row count). "With a user id" is the request, not the role the statement runs as: a `SECURITY DEFINER` function called from the app is still that user and writes no role either. Without a user id — the SQL editor, `supabase db query`, the service-role key — the role is written (migration `2026_10_11_07_profile_rules.sql`, review P20: the pin used to apply to every caller, so the documented promotion answered `UPDATE 1` and did nothing). A request without a session is not such a caller: `anon` has no policy on `profiles` and reaches no row — which is the only thing that holds it, so never give `anon` an UPDATE policy there. If a future Profile feature needs to mutate a new column, the policy already allows it; a new column the user must NOT write (anything role-adjacent) has to be added to that trigger function, or it is theirs. `supabase/tests/profiles.test.js` holds it.
- **A role change never contradicts who coaches whom.** The same trigger refuses, with `23514` and a sentence, to make a coach of an account that is somebody's athlete (`students.profile_id`) and to take the role from a coach who has athletes (`students.coach_id`). It is the rule of `trg_check_students_link` — every link joins a `coach` to a `student` — asked when the other side of it moves; without it the promotion that now works would be a way to write exactly the rows that gate refuses. Raised, not silent: the caller is the owner. Consequences: the role comes first and the links after it (a fixture promotes, then links — `createUser({ role: 'coach' })` in `supabase/tests/harness.js`); an athlete with logged training cannot become a coach on the same account (their link cannot be deleted), a coach gets an account of their own; a coach is made an athlete again only once their athletes have moved. KNOWN residual gap: the two triggers read each other's table without a lock, so a link written and a role changed in two transactions at the very same moment can pass each other. Both are owner-only writes.
- **A new account is always `role='student'`, and always has a name.** `handle_new_user` (the `on_auth_user_created` trigger on `auth.users`) never reads the role from the sign-up metadata — the anon key is public, so that metadata is attacker-controlled (2026_07_12) — and stores a `full_name` that is never empty (migration `2026_10_11_07_profile_rules.sql`, review B10): `raw_user_meta_data->>'full_name'` without the white space around it, else the part of the e-mail address before the `@`, else `'Athlete'`. An account made with *Add user* in the dashboard carries no name at all, and an empty one passes every `COALESCE(name, 'Your athlete')` of the push triggers: the coach read " completed Session 3". The function runs inside the INSERT that creates the account, so it **must never raise and never produce NULL** (`profiles.full_name` is NOT NULL), and it **must stay `SECURITY DEFINER`** (the INSERT is GoTrue's, which holds nothing on `public`): either mistake closes account creation for everyone ("Database error saving new user"). The migration does not write the trigger on `auth.users` — the table is the platform's — and refuses to run where no trigger calls the function. KNOWN residual gaps: (1) the profiles created before the migration keep their empty name until somebody types one (`docs/runbook/migrations/07-profile-rules.md`, "Accounts without a name"; line 10 of its audit counts them); (2) nothing stops a user from saving a name of blanks through the API — the app does not send one — and the push triggers still read `COALESCE(name, …)`.
- **A coach is made by the owner of the database, with one statement, and by nobody else.** `update public.profiles set role = 'coach' …` from the SQL editor (the statement, by e-mail address, and what it answers: `docs/runbook/migrations/07-profile-rules.md`, "Making a coach"). The app has no screen for it and no API path reaches it: there is no RPC for it on purpose — a function that changes a role is called without a user id or changes nothing. Each coach is a tenant of their own: a new coach starts with an empty roster and an empty library, and sees nothing of another coach's.
- **`students` is the root of authorization, and no signed-in user creates, deletes or moves a link.** Every "Coaches …" policy asks this table who coaches whom, so a row that should not exist gives its `coach_id` the reach of a coach over its `profile_id`. The table has NO `INSERT` policy and NO `DELETE` policy (migration `2026_10_11_05_students_role_gate.sql`, review P7 / L2): a link is made and removed by the owner of the database in the SQL editor, or with the service-role key. `Coaches update their students` stays, for the coach of the row who is a coach, with a `WITH CHECK` — for the columns that are not the link. `trg_check_students_link` (`check_students_link()`, `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE`) holds what a row may say **for every writer, `postgres` and the service role included**: `coach_id` is a profile with the role `coach`, `profile_id` one with the role `student`, and they are two profiles (`23514`); with a user id, `id`, `profile_id` and `coach_id` do not change at all (`42501`, `the coach-athlete link … cannot be changed from the app`), whatever role the statement runs as — so a function that moves an athlete is not granted to `authenticated`. A row whose link does not move is not judged again: a column next to it can always be written. Never add a write policy on `students` that reads `coach_id = auth.uid()` alone — any account can put its own id there, which is how an athlete made itself the coach of its coach. The app only reads the table. KNOWN residual gap: the trigger judges a link when it is written, not when a role changes — promoting a profile that is somebody's athlete, or demoting a coach who has athletes, leaves a link the gate would refuse; `profiles.role` is written by the owner only. `supabase/tests/students-gate.test.js` holds all of it.
- **A policy that lets a COACH write asks for the role: `(SELECT public.is_coach())`.** `is_coach()` (SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, in the allow-list of `authenticated`) is true when the caller's profile has the role `coach`; `profiles.role` cannot be written by a signed-in user. `Coaches manage own library` on `exercise_library` asks it in its `WITH CHECK`, so only a coach adds or edits a library row; its `USING` is still `coach_id = auth.uid()` alone, because a `FOR ALL` policy's `USING` is also what a `SELECT` goes through, and `is_coach()` is not executable without a session — a read that answered "no rows" would answer `permission denied for function is_coach`. For the same reason an `INSERT` or an `UPDATE` on `exercise_library`, and an `UPDATE` on `students`, sent without a session are refused with that message (`42501`, mapped to `errors.notAllowed`) where row-level security used to answer. Written as a sub-select the function is asked once per statement, not once per row.
- **The exercise of a slot, and the substitute of a swap, come from the library of the program's coach — asked when the row is created or the exercise changes.** `trg_check_slot_exercise_in_coach_library` (`BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF exercise_id` on `exercise_slots`) and `trg_check_deviation_substitute_in_coach_library` (`BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF substitute_exercise_id` on `slot_deviations`) compare `exercise_library.coach_id` with `students.coach_id` of the athlete the program belongs to and raise `23514` (`… is not in the library of the coach of this program`), for every writer. They do NOT judge a row whose exercise stays what it is: after an athlete changed coach, the slots and swaps that still hold the former coach's exercises can be edited in every other column, and take an exercise of the new library the day theirs is replaced. Two consequences to keep in mind: **a copy is an `INSERT`** — duplicating a session, a week or a program that holds another coach's exercise is refused until that exercise is replaced, and a function that copies slots must decide what it does with such a slot before it inserts; and **the app saves a swap as an upsert**, for which PostgreSQL fires the `INSERT` trigger on the proposed row even when the row exists — the deviation trigger therefore lets an `INSERT` through when the slot already has a deviation with that same substitute (the statement can only become the `UPDATE` of that row). Remove that exception and a swap made before a change of coach can no longer take a note.
- **What an account or a library exercise takes with it when it is deleted is decided per foreign key, and a coach's account takes nothing of an athlete's.** Migration `2026_10_13_10_goals_fk_coach_links.sql` (review P18 / L7, B9). A row that belongs to the account alone goes with it: `students.profile_id`, `goals.student_id` and the athlete's logs and messages are `ON DELETE CASCADE`. A row that somebody else's training hangs on holds the account: `students.coach_id` and `goals.coach_id` are `ON DELETE RESTRICT`, so the profile of a coach is not deleted while an athlete is linked to it or a goal names it — `23503`, nothing removed, whoever deletes (the dashboard's *Delete user* included). And a library exercise that anything names is not deleted: `exercise_slots.exercise_id`, `slot_deviations.substitute_exercise_id` and `goals.exercise_id` are all `RESTRICT`; `goal_progress` still follows its goal (`CASCADE`), because deleting a goal is its coach's decision. Three things to keep:
  - **A new reference to `profiles` or to `exercise_library` chooses its `ON DELETE` by that rule**, and a new reference to `exercise_library` also joins the three that `useExerciseUsage` counts for the delete dialog.
  - **The refusal names the first thing in the way, and which one comes first is not fixed.** The triggers of the foreign keys on one table fire in the order of their internal names, and a re-created constraint gets a new one: deleting a coach's profile is refused by `students_coach_id_fkey`, by `goals_coach_id_fkey`, or — when the `DELETE` reaches the coach's library first — by `exercise_slots_exercise_id_fkey` or `goals_exercise_id_fkey`. Code that reads the error goes by `23503`, never by the constraint name; `supabase/tests/goals.test.js` asserts the name only where one key alone is in the way.
  - **A coach whose athletes moved on with their programs cannot be deleted**: their exercises are still in those sessions. The account stays (`docs/runbook/migrations/10-goals-fk-coach-links.md`, "Removing a coach's account").
- **`exercise_library.archived_at` takes an exercise out of the pickers, and the database refuses nothing about an archived exercise.** The column exists since migration `2026_10_13_10_goals_fk_coach_links.sql` because an exercise that was ever used can no longer be deleted. It is written by the owner of the row through `Coaches manage own library` — in the app by `useArchiveExercise` (`{ id, archived }`: now, or `null` again) from the library page — and read by their athletes like any other column: an archived exercise is still the name of what they trained. A slot that holds one stays, a new slot can take one, a goal on one is still logged against, and a function that copies sessions copies their slots whatever `archived_at` says — "not offered for new work" is the client's rule ([`isActiveExercise`](../src/hooks/useExerciseLibrary.js)). Do not add a constraint or a trigger that refuses one: every copy of a block that uses it would fail. On the client: `useExerciseLibrary` returns archived rows to both roles, and must keep doing so — `SessionView`, `SessionReview` and the history look names up in it; the PICKERS filter with `isActiveExercise` (`coach/SessionEditor` "Select exercise…", `coach/StudentGoalsSection`, and on the athlete's side `student/SlotDeviationBar` and `student/StudentProgramAuthor`); a slot and a goal embed their own exercise (`exercise:exercise_library(*)`), so one that holds an archived exercise still shows it. [ExerciseLibrary.jsx](../src/components/coach/ExerciseLibrary.jsx) lists the exercises on offer with edit, archive and delete, and the archived ones in an "Archived · N" drawer under them — closed until opened, every archived exercise whatever the search and the type pills say — with restore and delete. A test that mocks `../hooks/useExerciseLibrary` for a component that filters spreads `await vi.importActual(…)` into its factory, or `isActiveExercise` is undefined there.
- **"The coach of this athlete" is `students.coach_id` today — never a column that remembers who wrote a row.** `goals.coach_id` says who manages a goal; who may read what hangs under it is asked of the link as well. `coach_profile_for_goal(goal)` — the helper the read policy of `goal_progress` is written with — answers the profile that set the goal only while `students` links it to the goal's athlete, and NULL otherwise (migration `2026_10_13_10_goals_fk_coach_links.sql`, review P21: it used to answer `goals.coach_id` alone, and a former coach went on reading every attempt of an athlete who had moved). That is the rule `Coaches manage goals for their students` already asked of the goal itself. A new policy for a coach on a table that carries its own `coach_id` asks the link too, directly or through such a helper.
- **An athlete changes coach with `public.transfer_student(athlete profile id, new coach profile id)`, and with nothing else.** It moves `students.coach_id` and hands the athlete's goals to the new coach (`goals.coach_id`) in one statement, and returns `{ student_id, from_coach_id, to_coach_id, link_moved, goals_moved }`; called with the coach the athlete already has, it moves no link and hands over the goals a former coach still held. Rewriting `students.coach_id` by hand leaves the goals with a coach who can no longer read them, and no coach reads their progress until they are handed over. It is for a caller without a user id — the SQL editor, the service-role key: it is not granted to `authenticated`, it raises `42501` first thing when `auth.uid()` is not NULL, and it is `SECURITY INVOKER`, so it has no right its caller lacks; `trg_check_students_link` refuses the move with a user id whatever function it comes from, and judges the new link (a coach, an athlete, two profiles). Rules to keep:
  - **A table that gets a `coach_id` next to a `student_id` joins the body of `transfer_student`** — in its migration and in `schema.sql` — or its comment says why the rows stay with the former coach.
  - **A screen that moves an athlete calls it from an Edge Function with the service-role key**, never through a `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper: inside a user's request `auth.uid()` is still that user, and both the function and the trigger refuse.
  - **What it does not move.** The exercises: the slots of the athlete's programs, their swaps and their goals keep pointing at the former coach's library. The athlete and the new coach read those exercises — and nothing else of that library — through "Users read the exercises their training uses" (next bullet); neither may write one, and a new slot, swap or copy takes an exercise of the library of today. On a database without that policy the embed `exercise:exercise_library(…)` of a session read comes back null for such an exercise, and the athlete's pages name it "Exercise unavailable" (`sessionNavPastReview.exerciseUnavailable`: the chip and the panel of the session screen, the exercise lines of the next session on the training list); the coach's editor draws a card that says so, and the review keeps the slot under `coachEditorNavErrors.exercise.unavailableTitle`. Code of the session page reads `slot.exercise?.…`, never `slot.exercise.…`: a null exercise used to throw and take the whole page down. The conversation with the former coach: both keep their messages, the two can no longer write to each other, and a message of the former coach that the athlete had not opened stays counted by `useUnreadMessageCount`, which counts every unread message addressed to the user while `StudentMessages` shows the conversation with the coach of today only. The former coach's notifications about the athlete.
- **An exercise is read by its coach, by that coach's athletes, and by whoever reads something that uses it.** "Users read the exercises their training uses" (migration `2026_10_16_23_library_read_follows_program.sql`, review B8) asks `exercise_readable_through_use(exercise)` — `STABLE`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, in the allow-list of `authenticated`, true or false and never NULL — whether a slot of a program the caller reads as its athlete or its coach (`students.profile_id` / `students.coach_id`, the policies of `programs`), a swap on such a slot, or a goal the caller reads names the exercise. Without it, after a change of coach the app embedded NULL for every exercise of the blocks written before it and broke. The policy is for `SELECT` only and its name sorts after "Coaches manage own library" and "Students read their coachs library": PostgreSQL asks permissive policies in the order of their names and stops at the first yes, so an exercise of the caller's library or their coach's never reaches the function. A new reference to `exercise_library` that an athlete or their coach reads joins the function. `useExerciseLibrary` reads an athlete's library without a filter: after a change of coach the exercises of the former coach their training uses are in the swap picker of `SlotDeviationBar`, where `trg_check_deviation_substitute_in_coach_library` refuses a swap to one (`23514`), and in the picker of `StudentProgramAuthor`, where `save_draft_tree` refuses the whole tree once one is picked (`exercise_unavailable`; `draftSaveErrorHandler` keeps the draft on the device until the exercise is taken out). A goal is the one use without a library rule: `goals.exercise_id` may name an exercise of any library (slots and swaps have the gates of migration `2026_10_11_05_students_role_gate.sql`, goals none), so a coach who writes a goal through the API on an exercise of another library — by its id — reads that exercise through the policy.
- **Every foreign key of `public` has an index that starts with its column, and no index repeats the first column of a unique key.** Migration `2026_10_16_24_indexes_cleanup.sql` (review L28, P62) dropped nine indexes a unique key stood in for (`idx_students_profile_id`, `idx_weeks_program_id`, `idx_sessions_week_id`, `idx_set_logs_slot_id`, `idx_session_confirmations_session_id`, `idx_slot_comments_slot_id`, `idx_slot_deviations_slot_id`, `idx_set_log_videos_set_log`, `bodyweight_logs_student_idx`) and indexed the foreign keys that had none: `client_errors (user_id, created_at DESC)` — what the cap counts —, `exercise_slots (exercise_id)` — what `exercise_readable_through_use` reads —, `goals (coach_id)`, `slot_comments (student_id)`, `slot_deviations (student_id)` and `(substitute_exercise_id)`, and `messages (sender_id, created_at DESC)` and `(recipient_id, created_at DESC)`, a conversation from one side, newest first. A new foreign key gets its index in its migration — its own, a unique key that starts with its column, or one on `WHERE <column> IS NOT NULL`; `supabase/tests/small-hardening.test.js` asks the catalog. The six indexes the advisors call unused stay until they are measured.
- **Public sign-up is off, and only the dashboard holds that setting.** The app is invite-only: it has no sign-up screen, nothing in `src/` calls `signUp`, and accounts are created in the Supabase dashboard. `disable_signup = true` — with the site URL, the redirect list and the 8-character minimum listed in `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md` — is set by hand by the project's owner, following `docs/runbook/20-auth-config.md` (Checkpoint A0 of `docs/RUNBOOK.md`). No code can enforce it, so nothing in the repository may undo it: a `supabase/config.toml` carries `[functions.<name>]` tables and nothing else, and no script or workflow runs `supabase config push`, which would overwrite the remote Auth settings with the CLI's defaults (sign-up on, site URL on localhost, 6-character passwords). `src/test/authConfig.test.js` holds both, and runs the page's read-only check of `/auth/v1/settings`, read off the page, against a stand-in server. Do not "harden" Auth in the dashboard without the client change that goes with it: captcha locks everyone out of the deployed app (sign-in sends no captcha answer), and "Secure password change" makes its change-password dialog fail (it sends no re-authentication code).
- **Students can DELETE only their own extra sets** (2026_07_12): the `Students delete own set logs` policy requires `is_student_added = true`, closing the DELETE + re-INSERT lane around the target-pin trigger. The delete guard on `set_logs` follows the same line: a logged extra set goes for the athlete who added it and for nobody else — the coach is refused like on any logged set. `is_student_added` cannot be written by the athlete's own UPDATE (`pin_set_log_targets_for_student`, migration 06), so a prescribed set cannot be turned into a deletable one. KNOWN residual gap: the student INSERT policy still accepts `target_*` values — it must, while `useEnsureSetLogs` materializes prescribed rows under the student JWT. The function that does it server-side exists (`ensure_set_logs`, migration `2026_10_13_13_ensure_claim_rpcs.sql`) and marks its rows with `app.trusted_set_log_insert`; the INSERT is closed by a `contract` migration once no deployed app inserts prescribed rows itself. Don't "fix" the INSERT policy before that.
- **A session is read-only for its athlete when its program is not the block being trained, when the coach archived it, or when the coach reviewed it — and the database asks all three.** An athlete's write to anything that hangs under a session asks three questions: `student_profile_for_slot(x) = auth.uid()` (the row is mine), `program_active_for_slot(x) = true` (my program is out of the trash, and is the active one or was switched off less than 72 hours ago — next bullet) and `session_writable_for_slot(x)` (the session is neither archived nor reviewed). Five surfaces carry them since migration `2026_10_12_08_session_write_gates.sql` (review P58): `set_logs`, `slot_comments`, `slot_deviations`, `set_log_videos` and the files of the `set-videos` bucket (`storage.objects`). Before it `set_logs` asked nothing about the session, `slot_comments` nothing about the review, `slot_deviations` asked "archived" on INSERT only, and videos — row and file — asked nothing at all, so a second device that still showed a reviewed session open kept rewriting what the coach had reviewed. Reading is never gated: SELECT is a policy of its own on each table, and an athlete sees every session they ever trained. Coach writes are never gated this way, and `adopt_swap`, `adopt_skip` and `decline_promote_request` write `slot_deviations` of a reviewed session as SECURITY DEFINER. `session_confirmations` is not one of the five, and since migration `2026_10_13_09_review_lifecycle.sql` it is closed by the review all the same: its UPDATE and DELETE policies ask "program active" and `session_writable(session_id)`. Its INSERT policy asks "not archived" and "program active", and nothing about the review (see "A review needs a confirmation and closes it", under Coach session review). UI-side the lock is `isReadOnly` in `SessionView`: `isClosed` (derived from `session.program_is_active` + `archived_at`) or `session.reviewed_at` set (see "A session the coach has reviewed is read-only for the athlete", below). `supabase/tests/session-gates.test.js` holds the database's half, with every write of the app replayed on a session in progress, confirmed, reviewed, archived, of a past block and of a trashed one.
- **A block stays open for its athlete for 72 hours after it is switched off — in the database, and only there.** The app queues what an athlete logs without a connection; when the coach activates the next block in between, the workout is replayed against a block that is no longer the active one, and before migration `2026_10_13_17_replay_grace_window.sql` every set of it reached no row (PGRST116), the confirmation and the notes were refused, and nothing could be sent again (review P55). Since then `programs.deactivated_at` holds the time `is_active` last went from true to false, and `program_active_for_session(session)` answers yes for a program that is out of the trash and is the active one **or** is approved and was switched off less than 72 hours ago. `program_active_for_slot`, the policies of `set_logs`, `slot_comments`, `slot_deviations`, `set_log_videos`, `session_confirmations` and of the `set-videos` files, and `ensure_set_logs` all ask that one function, so the window is the same for every write of an athlete — and `interval '72 hours'` is written there and nowhere else. Three things it does not open: a session the coach archived or reviewed (`session_writable`), a block in the trash (taken out inside its 72 hours it is open again for what is left of them), and a draft. A block that was switched off before the migration has no time and no window. `supabase/tests/replay-grace.test.js` holds it, with the athlete's queue (`MUTATION_KEYS`) replayed after a switch.
- **"Open for its athlete" and "the block being trained" are two questions since migration 17.** `program_active_for_session` / `program_active_for_slot` answer the first — for three days after a switch, for two blocks of one athlete. `programs.is_active` answers the second, for one. What an athlete may WRITE asks the function. Which block is the current one — the queue (`buildQueue`), "No program assigned yet", `session.program_is_active` and the "From a past program — read-only." banner of `SessionView`, a count "of the active block" — reads `is_active`, and must go on doing so: the screens show a block that is not active as read-only from the moment of the switch, and the window is for what was already waiting on a phone. Do not derive the lock of a screen from the window, and do not write a policy that compares `deactivated_at` itself.
- **`programs.deactivated_at` is written by a trigger, and by no request that carries a user id.** `trg_stamp_program_deactivated_at` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE on `programs`) writes `now()` when a statement switches a block off — the two UPDATEs of `useSetActiveProgram` and of `useCreateProgram` with `setActive`, `activate_program`, a block sent to the trash while it is active — and NULL while the row is active. Any other write of a signed-in user leaves the column as it was (NULL on a new row), **silently**: the statement is accepted and the rest of it is saved, like the pins of migration 06. It has to be held: the author of a draft writes the row of their draft, and a time put there would keep the block open for its athlete after the coach approves it. A request without a user id — the SQL editor, the service-role key — writes it as it likes on a block that is not active: that is how the owner closes a window early (`NULL`) or opens one again (`now()`), `docs/runbook/migrations/17-replay-grace-window.md`. A hook never sends the column, and a function that switches a block off writes `is_active = false` and nothing more.
- **`session_writable` is the lock of the SESSION and says nothing about the program.** `session_writable(session)` and `session_writable_for_slot(slot)` answer "not archived and not reviewed" — `true` for a session of a past block, of a trashed block or of a draft. Whether the program is the one being trained is `program_active_for_session` / `program_active_for_slot`, and a policy asks both. So does a SECURITY DEFINER function that writes for an athlete: it goes around the policies, and one that asked `session_writable` alone would write into past blocks. Both never answer NULL (a session that does not exist is not writable — `IF NOT session_writable(x) THEN RAISE` refuses what it should; keep the `COALESCE(…, false)`). What closes a session is said in that function, in one place: a new column that locks a session joins it, not the policies. `session_writable(uuid)` and `session_writable_for_slot(uuid)` are both executable by a signed-in user: the first is asked of a session id by the UPDATE and DELETE policies of `session_confirmations`, the second of a slot by the five surfaces.
- **The lock is `reviewed_at`, not the confirmation.** Set-log writes replay in per-row scopes and the confirmation in `offline-writes`, so a set logged offline may arrive after the confirmation of its session. A confirmed session that the coach has not reviewed is written like one in progress. Do not move the gate to `session_confirmations`: the last sets of an offline workout would be refused. The app can no longer make a session reviewed without a confirmation (migration `2026_10_13_09_review_lifecycle.sql`), but such sessions exist — reviewed before that migration from a `session_deviation` notification while the athlete was still training, or whose confirmation was undone after the review — and the owner of the database can still make one. Such a session is closed like any reviewed one, while `buildQueue` — which drops a session when it is confirmed or archived, never when it is reviewed — keeps listing it as to do. The way out for its athlete is the confirmation: the INSERT policy of `session_confirmations` does not ask about the review, and a confirmation made on a reviewed session cannot be undone. A client that shows such a session as open sends writes that are all refused; line 13 of the audit of migration 08 counts these sessions, and its runbook page ("Sessions reviewed that their athlete has not confirmed") lists them and gives the statement that takes a review back.
- **A refused athlete write has two shapes, and one of them is silent.** An INSERT — an upsert included, which PostgreSQL judges as the INSERT it starts as — raises `42501` (`errors.notAllowed`). An UPDATE or a DELETE on a closed session reaches no row and raises nothing: `.update(…).select().single()` then fails with PGRST116 (`errors.generic`), and a bare `.delete()` or `.update()` answers success with nothing changed. A hook that deletes or updates without reading the row back cannot tell "done" from "refused"; on a session that may be closed, gate the affordance in the UI or select the row.
- **A set video is stored at `<athlete's profile id>/<exercise slot of its set>/<file>`, and nowhere else.** The WITH CHECK of `set_log_videos` (INSERT and UPDATE) requires `storage_path` to be exactly there — `split_part(storage_path, '/', 1) = auth.uid()` and `set_video_slot(storage_path) = slot_for_set_log(set_log_id)` — and the storage policies judge a file by the slot its path names: an athlete adds or removes a file only under a slot of their own, in a writable session of their active program. So `useUploadSetVideo` must keep building the path from `user.id` and the set's own `exercise_slot_id`; a path under another slot is refused at the row (`42501`), and a file uploaded there cannot be attached to the set. Without the rule a row could point at a file under a session still to come, and that file stayed replaceable after the review. An account nobody coaches has no slot and uploads nothing (review L5); nobody uploads under another athlete's exercise. There is no UPDATE policy on the files: one is never overwritten or renamed. KNOWN residual gaps: a row written before the rule keeps whatever path it has (the audit of migration 08 counts them; the athlete can still read it, and remove it while its session is writable); a file whose row is gone stays in the bucket, readable by its athlete alone — since migration `2026_10_16_25_storage_video_policies.sql` the coach reads a file only through a video that names it (next bullet) — and removable by the athlete only while the session of the slot in its path is writable — which is also what a clean-up that failed leaves: the old file of a replace, the file of a deleted video, the file of an upload that was cancelled or whose row was refused. The app removes each of them best-effort and reports the ones it could not remove (`removeUnreferenced` in `hooks/useSetVideo.js` → `reportError`, with the path), so they show in the coach's error list; nothing in the app lists or removes them afterwards; `docs/runbook/migrations/25-storage-video-policies.md` ("Files that no video names") lists them for the owner, who removes one through the Storage API (the dashboard) — never with `delete from storage.objects`, which leaves the bytes behind. Two devices that replace the video of the same set at the same moment leave the file of the one that wrote its row first: each read the row before its upload.
- **A coach reads a set video's file through the video that names it, never through the folder alone.** "Coaches read student set videos" on `storage.objects` (migration `2026_10_16_25_storage_video_policies.sql`, review B7) lets a coach read a file of `set-videos` when it is in the folder of one of their athletes (`students.coach_id = auth.uid()`) and a row of `set_log_videos` names it: `storage_path = objects.name`, and `student_profile_for_set_log(set_log_id)` is the athlete whose folder holds the file. The row is read with the coach's rights ("Coaches read student set log videos"), so after `transfer_student` the new coach reads the files the athlete's videos name and the former coach none; a row written by hand that names a file of another folder lends nobody that file. `storage.objects` has no foreign key to `set_log_videos`, and without the rule the coach listed and signed links to the old file of a replace, the file of a deleted video and an upload that was cancelled. The athlete's own policy ("Students read own set videos") still reads every file of their folder. The app is not concerned: `useSetVideoSignedUrl` signs the paths it read from `set_log_videos`. The policy casts no part of the path, so a file of another shape is not read and does not fail the statement. `supabase/tests/storage-policies.test.js` holds it, and `rls-matrix.test.js` has a file no video names (the coach reads 3 of the 5 files).
- **The row of a set video always names a file that exists: the new file first, then the row, then the old file.** [`useUploadSetVideo`](../src/hooks/useSetVideo.js) (`saveSetVideo`) uploads the clip at a path of its own, then points the row at it — an UPDATE of the row that is there (`set_log_id` is UNIQUE, so "insert the new row, delete the old one" cannot be written), an INSERT when the set had no video — and removes the previous file last. `useDeleteSetVideo` deletes the row with `.select('id')` and removes the file only when a row came back: the database answers an athlete's delete on a closed session by reaching no row, without an error. Either order reversed loses a video — a failed upload after the old file is gone, or a row left pointing at nothing. Three rules to keep when editing it: **a file is removed only when it is certain that no row names it** — after a row write the database refused (`refusedByDatabase`: a SQLSTATE or a `PGRST` code), never after one that got no answer, because that row may name the new file by now; **the last step never fails the mutation** — `removeUnreferenced` reads the error of `remove()`, treats an empty answer as "not removed" (what Storage returns when its policy keeps the file out of reach), and passes what stays behind to `reportError` with its path; and **the file is checked before the first request** — `videoFileProblem` in [lib/setVideoFile.js](../src/lib/setVideoFile.js): one of the bucket's three types, not empty (`size_bytes` is `CHECK > 0`), 25 MB at most. `lib/setVideoFile.test.js` reads the bucket's list out of `supabase/schema.sql`: a type added to the bucket is added to `ACCEPTED_VIDEO_TYPES` in the same change.
- **The progress and the cancel of a video upload are module state, by set — not state of the button.** `VideoUploadButton` lives in the set's sheet, whose content is unmounted when the sheet closes, while the upload goes on: the mutation does not belong to the component. [lib/setVideoUploads.js](../src/lib/setVideoUploads.js) holds one entry per set that is uploading (`beginVideoUpload` / `end()` around `saveSetVideo`, `cancelVideoUpload` from the button) and the button reads its set through `useSyncExternalStore`, so the one that is mounted again shows the upload and can stop it, and a second upload of the same set is refused. So: **never abort an upload when the button unmounts** (it would kill the upload each time an athlete finishes the exercise), and **do not move progress, the `AbortController` or an `onError` into the button** — a callback passed to `mutate()` is dropped at unmount. For the same reason a failed upload is told from the hook: the mutation carries `meta: { skipErrorToast: true }` and its own `onError` pushes one toast (`uploadErrorKey`), whether or not the button is still there; removing one without the other gives two messages or none. A cancel (`isAbortError`) says nothing. Cancel is honoured up to the row write: stopped after the last byte and before it, the new file is removed; after it the replace has happened. The upload itself is [lib/storageUpload.js](../src/lib/storageUpload.js) (`uploadObject`), an `XMLHttpRequest` that sends the request storage-js sends for a Blob, because `supabase.storage.upload()` has no progress and takes no signal — do not go back to it. The two lines the button adds (the `progressbar` and the refusal) stand under its buttons. Its player (`ui/VideoLightbox`) opens inside the sheet it was opened from (`container`: the page behind a modal `<dialog>` is inert and under it), and keeps Escape for itself — heard in the capture phase of the window — so that the sheet stays open behind it.
- **A session the coach has reviewed is read-only for the athlete and its confirmation cannot be undone — and [SessionView.jsx](../src/components/student/SessionView.jsx) reads that from `session.reviewed_at`, never from the feedback message.** The page keeps three facts apart: `isClosed` (past program or archived — nothing can be written, the confirmation included), `isReviewed` (`session.reviewed_at` is set) and `isReadOnly = isClosed || isReviewed`, which is what `SessionExercise` receives and what locks the sets, RPE, actuals, extra sets, swap and skip, the note for the coach, and the video's replace and delete (`VideoUploadButton` disables both on `disabled`; playing stays). `useEnsureSetLogs` is switched off on a read-only session (`enabled: false`): rows added to a reviewed session would be sets the coach never read, and `ensure_set_logs` refuses a session that is closed. Where "Undo confirmation" was, a reviewed session shows `ReviewedLock`: the reason (`sessionReviewedLock.locked`) and a link to `/student/messages`. There is no reopening in the app; the link is the athlete's only way to get something corrected. Rules to keep when editing it:
  - **A reviewed session can still be CONFIRMED.** Sessions reviewed without a confirmation, or whose confirmation was undone after the review, exist from before the two were tied together. Read-only without a Confirm button would leave such a session "Next" in the training list for good (`buildQueue` drops a session only when it is confirmed or archived), so the page shows the lock (`sessionReviewedLock.lockedUnconfirmed`, which says the confirmation will be final) above the Confirm button. Only `isClosed` hides Confirm.
  - **A closed session keeps its own line.** Past program and archived win over reviewed ("From a past program — …", "Archived by your coach — …"), and show no link: no message to the coach reopens a past block.
  - **`reviewed_at` comes from a cached row, and nothing pushes a review to the athlete's device.** `['session', sessionId]` is served without a request while it is fresh and is persisted for offline use, so "not reviewed" is only as true as the row is recent. The page asks for the row again — `refetch({ cancelRefetch: false })`, which joins a request already on its way — at the three moments a review may have happened behind the cache: when it shows a confirmed session that is not read-only (the only kind a coach reviews), when `useSessionFeedback` returns a message while the row still says "not reviewed" (feedback arrives over the messages channel a second after it is sent), and on the tap on "Undo confirmation" (a review without feedback sends nothing). These three do not depend on the query's `staleTime` or on a refetch on focus: a change to either must leave them in place. Offline the request waits; nothing on the page waits for it.
  - **The undo dialog is open only while `canUndo`** (`isConfirmed && !isReadOnly`), so a "reviewed" answer that arrives while the athlete reads it takes its button away.
  - **Do not key the lock on the feedback message.** A review without feedback has none, and the page would lock on another rule than the column everything else reads. The message is a reason to read the row again, nothing more.

  This is the client half: it shows the lock and does not depend on a migration. The database holds the same rule for the athlete's writes in migrations 08 (`session_write_gates`) and 09 (`review_lifecycle`). A test that mocks `useSession` for this page has to give the query's `refetch` (`mockRefetchSession` in `SessionView.test.jsx`).
- **Logged training can never be hard-deleted; "delete" is archive-first** (2026_07_13). `programs.deleted_at` is a trash flag: `useDeleteProgram` stamps it AND clears `is_active` (a trashed program must never hold the one-active-per-student slot); every program query filters `deleted_at IS NULL`; `useRestoreProgram` NULLs it (program returns **inactive**). `program_active_for_session` folds in `deleted_at IS NULL`, so a trashed program is never writable regardless of `is_active` — and of the 72 hours a block stays open after it is switched off (migration 17). **You cannot trash the ACTIVE program** (`canDelete = !is_active` in ProgramSwitcher) — it would strand the student. Hard delete is offered only from the trash and only when the program has zero logged sets — `useHardDeleteProgram`'s count check is the friendly message, the database is the guarantee (next bullets). A blocked delete surfaces `errors.hasLoggedTraining` ("archive instead"). Below the session, the coach's client refuses on its own before it writes — removing an exercise, removing a set or lowering a set count over a logged set throws the same error from `useSession.js` (see the logged-set entry under `set_logs`).
- **A logged set has ONE definition: `set_log_is_logged(set_logs)`.** Done, failed, skipped, an RPE or an actual (`done OR failed OR skipped OR rpe IS NOT NULL OR actual_reps IS NOT NULL OR actual_weight_kg IS NOT NULL`) — migration `2026_10_11_04_logged_history_guards.sql`. It takes the row (`set_log_is_logged(sl)`), so a caller cannot leave a column out. Every guard below reads it, and so do `adopt_swap` and `adopt_skip`. A new rule about "already trained" calls the function and never repeats the columns: a predicate spelled out in two places ends up shorter in one of them (review P17). It is not granted to `authenticated`: only SECURITY DEFINER functions call it, as their owner — a client that needs the same answer reads the six columns. A set that carries a `set_log_videos` row is protected like a logged one by the delete guards; that is written next to each call (`OR EXISTS (… set_log_videos …)`), not inside the function.
- **A logged set cannot be hard-deleted at any level, cascades included.** Five `BEFORE DELETE` row triggers: `block_program_delete_with_logged_sets`, `block_week_delete_with_logged_sets`, `block_session_delete_with_logged_sets`, `block_slot_delete_with_logged_sets` (on `exercise_slots`) and `block_set_log_delete_when_logged` (on `set_logs`). The last one is the net under the others: a row trigger fires on a cascade too, so whatever starts a delete — the editor, the API, a `students` row, a profile, an account — the logged row itself refuses. One exception: an extra set (`is_student_added`) deleted by the athlete who added it; in a cascade the slot is already gone, `student_profile_for_slot()` answers NULL and the exception does not apply. A comment or a deviation alone does not hold a slot: the coach removes an exercise nobody trained with them. Every message contains `logged set`, which `lib/mutationErrors.js` maps to `errors.hasLoggedTraining` — a new guard keeps those two words. `supabase/tests/history-guards.test.js` holds all of it, the cascades with the upper guards switched off included. KNOWN residual gap: the coach's `FOR ALL` policy on `set_logs` has no column pin, so a coach who calls the API by hand can still write `done`, `rpe` or `actual_*` — un-log a set, then delete it. The app never writes those columns as a coach; the guards stop what the editor and a cascade do, not a coach who sets out to falsify a log.
- **`exercise_slots.sets` cannot go below a logged prescribed set** (`block_slot_sets_below_logged_set`, `BEFORE UPDATE OF sets`). The rows it looks at are the ones a lower count orphans: not `is_student_added`, `set_number` above the new count, logged or with a video. The guard is on the count, not only on the rows, because the session editor lowers a set count in two writes — the count first, then the DELETE: refusing the first leaves nothing changed. The refusal is the net, not the interface: a client that removes or drops sets must read the logged flags first and say no itself.
- **The targets of a logged set are pinned for a signed-in user — silently.** `trg_pin_logged_set_log_targets` (`BEFORE UPDATE` on `set_logs`, entered only when a `target_*` column changes) puts `target_reps`, `target_duration_seconds`, `target_weight_kg` and `target_rest_seconds` back on a row that `set_log_is_logged`. A set done as prescribed has no actual: its targets ARE what the athlete lifted. It does not raise, because the editor writes a target change to every set of the exercise in one statement and a refusal would abort the edit for the sets still to come. So **an UPDATE of targets answers "1 row" for a logged set and has changed nothing**: a client fans a target change out to pending sets only, and never reads a row count as "written". The athlete is pinned on every row, logged or not, by `pin_set_log_targets_for_student`. A statement with no user id (the SQL editor, the service role) is not pinned: that is a deliberate correction.
- **The one way through the delete guards is `SET LOCAL app.allow_history_delete = 'on'`, and it is not for the app.** `history_delete_allowed()` is true when the transaction set that value AND `auth.uid()` is NULL — the SQL editor, a migration, a function called with the service-role key. The six refusals (the five delete guards and the set-count guard) read it; for a signed-in user it does nothing, whoever set it. Never drop or disable a guard trigger to delete something: one that is not put back leaves the door open for everyone. The owner's recipe is in `docs/runbook/migrations/04-logged-history-guards.md`; a function that must delete history (erasing an account) calls `set_config('app.allow_history_delete', 'on', true)` itself and is executable by the service role only.
- **A library exercise that is in use is not deleted, and the client decides that on a usage read made since the dialog opened.** Three foreign keys point at `exercise_library`: `exercise_slots.exercise_id`, `slot_deviations.substitute_exercise_id` and `goals.exercise_id`. `useExerciseUsage(exerciseId)` in [useExerciseLibrary.js](../src/hooks/useExerciseLibrary.js) counts all three on the server and returns `{ slots, goals, swaps }`; [ExerciseDeleteDialog.jsx](../src/components/coach/ExerciseDeleteDialog.jsx) offers Delete only when the three are zero, lists the counts otherwise, and says that it could not check when the read failed or waits for the network. The database refuses the same delete for all three (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`; for goals since migration `2026_10_13_10_goals_fk_coach_links.sql`); the dialog does not depend on that, and against a database that does not have the migration yet it is what keeps the athletes' goals and their logged progress. Rules to keep when editing it:
  - **"Nothing uses it" is never taken from the cache.** `useExerciseUsage` sets `gcTime: 0` and `staleTime: 0`, and the dialog's body acts only on an answer read since it mounted: `usage.isFetchedAfterMount` **and** `usage.dataUpdatedAt >= openedAt`. Both are needed — a failed fetch also sets `isFetchedAfterMount`, and a timestamp alone cannot tell a read made in the millisecond before the open from one made since. `ExerciseDeleteDialog.test.jsx` holds one case for each.
  - **A count that did not come back is an error, not a zero.** "Unknown" must not read as "unused".
  - **The successful read is tested first and nothing un-reads it**: a refetch on window focus that fails later leaves the counts, and the Delete button, as they were.
  - **The dialog stays mounted and its body is mounted by each open.** Closing goes through `<dialog>.close()`, which returns focus to the row's button; the body (`UsageCheck`) takes `openedAt` when it mounts, so it must not be lifted into the part that stays.
  - **An exercise in use is offered "Archive instead", never a Delete.** The dialog shows it when the page passes `onArchive(id)` and the exercise is not archived already (`isActiveExercise`); it calls `onArchive` and closes. It is drawn first in the button row, in the place the disabled Delete held while the usage was read: a tap aimed at that button as the answer lands archives — which the drawer's restore undoes — and never deletes. Do not put it where Cancel was. Tests: `ExerciseDeleteDialog.test.jsx` ("archiving instead").
  - **While the usage is being read, Delete is rendered and disabled**, so the button under a finger does not change when the answer arrives.
  - **The counts are what the signed-in coach can read.** A reference they cannot see (legacy sessions of an athlete who moved to another coach) still stops the delete in the database, and `errors.inUse` says so. The slot count includes trashed programs and archived sessions, and the dialog's text says that.

  A test that mocks `useExerciseUsage` for the library page has to give the query's `isFetchedAfterMount` and `dataUpdatedAt`, or the dialog stays on "Checking…" (`usageQuery()` in `ExerciseLibrary.test.jsx`).
- **Setting a block active, reordering the sessions of a phase and reordering the phases of a block are each ONE call of a function that does it in one statement.** Migration `2026_10_13_11_activate_reorder_rpcs.sql` (review P16 and P51) holds three `SECURITY INVOKER` functions, executable by a signed-in user and written on the model of `move_session`: they give the caller no right the policies of `programs`, `weeks` and `sessions` do not. `useSetActiveProgram` is one `activate_program` call, `useReorderSessions` one `reorder_sessions` call, `useReorderWeeks` one `reorder_weeks` call. `useCreateProgram` inserts the program INACTIVE and, with `setActive`, calls `activate_program` afterwards: the block the athlete trains is switched off by the statement that switches the new one on, and by no request before it. A hook that goes back to several requests brings the hole back — each request is a transaction of its own, so one that is lost leaves the athlete without an active block — their page says "No program assigned yet", and what they log in the block that was switched off is saved for 72 hours (migration 17) and refused from then on — or leaves sessions numbered 100000 and more in the athlete's queue. The contract:
  - **`activate_program(p_program_id)`** switches every other active program of the athlete off — one in the trash included — and this one on, and returns `{ program_id, student_id, already_active, deactivated }` (`student_id` is the `students` row, `deactivated` a count). Called for the program that is active already it writes nothing and answers `already_active: true`: a request sent twice is not an error. It refuses, before any write: `program_not_found` (no such program that the caller can read — somebody else's athlete and a wrong id are the same answer), `not authorized` (`42501`: the caller reads it and may not write it — its athlete), `program_is_draft`, `program_in_trash`.
  - **`reorder_sessions(p_week_id, p_ids)`** and **`reorder_weeks(p_program_id, p_ids)`** take EVERY child of the parent, once each, in the order wanted — archived sessions included, because the unique key counts them — and return nothing. Afterwards `sort_order` is 0 to n-1 and `week_number` 1 to n, whatever the numbers were: a phase that an interrupted reorder left at 100000 and more is put right by the same call. A list that is not exactly the children is refused with `reorder_ids_mismatch` (its `details` count what is missing, foreign, or there twice) — which is what a page loaded before a session was added, moved or deleted sends: refetch, then try again. Also `week_not_found` / `program_not_found` and `not authorized` as above. A list that is the order already writes nothing.
- **The refusals are `P0001` with that fixed word as the message** (HTTP 400), except `not authorized`, which is `42501` and reaches the toast as `errors.notAllowed`; the others fall to `errors.generic` in `mutationErrorKey`. A hook sends a refused request once and never again by itself: `onError` puts the order on screen back — unless a later drag of the same scope waits, whose order it would remove — and the lists are read again in `onSettled` — after a failure too, because what the functions refuse is something the page still shows (a list from before a session was added in another tab, a program that went to the trash). `useCreateProgram` invalidates in `onSettled` for one more reason: a create whose activation is refused has still created the program, inactive.
  - **Two calls for the same athlete, phase or block wait for each other** (the parent is locked first), so the last one sent is the one that stays — the database needs no scope from the client to stay consistent. The client has one all the same, for the list on screen: "The order of a block is written one drag at a time" under "Training is a QUEUE".
  - **`useReorderPrograms` has no function**: `programs.sort_order` has no unique key, so no order of arrival is refused, and the hook keeps its one request per program in two passes (park at 100000 + n, then place). Two drags could still cross — the requests of the second landing among those of the first — so `useReorderPrograms(studentRowId)` runs under a mutation scope per athlete, like the reorders of a block. `ProgramSwitcher`, which calls it, is mounted once per athlete for the reason given under "The order of a block is written one drag at a time": the hook must not change scope while a reorder waits.
- **Duplicating a session, a phase or a block, and copying sessions to another phase, are each ONE call of a function that makes the whole copy or none.** Migration `2026_10_13_12_copy_rpcs.sql` (review P52) holds four `SECURITY INVOKER` functions, executable by a signed-in user: what is copied is what the caller can read, where it goes is where the caller can write, so a coach copies for their own athletes and from one of them to another, and nobody else does. `useDuplicateSession` is one `duplicate_session` call, `useCopySessions` one `copy_sessions` call, `useDuplicateWeek` one `duplicate_week` call and `useDuplicateProgram` one `duplicate_program` call ([useDuplicate.js](../src/hooks/useDuplicate.js)); the file reads and writes no table itself. A hook that goes back to its own requests brings the hole back — one request per row or per table (thirty to forty for a block), each a transaction of its own, so a request that is lost leaves a session with no exercise, or exercises with no set, in the block the athlete trains — and with it the `.in()` over every exercise id of a phase and the read that is cut at 1000 sets. The contract:
  - **`duplicate_session(p_session_id, p_title_suffix default ' (copy)')`** puts the copy RIGHT AFTER its source — the sessions that follow move down by one, archived ones included — and returns `{ session_id, week_id, sort_order, title, slots, set_logs, slots_left_out }`. A session without a title is called `Session` before the suffix; with `''` or `null` the title is copied as it is. The copy is never archived. The app sends the suffix: `useDuplicateSession` takes `{ sessionId, titleSuffix }` and sends `p_title_suffix` when `titleSuffix` is given (the key is left out otherwise); `ProgramSheet` sends a space and `coachSheetUx.copySuffix` — " (copy)", " (copie)", " (Kopie)" — and `''` for a source without a title (blank included), whose copy stays untitled and is named by its position. A copy made is announced by an info toast whose Undo (`uiSharedHelpers.undo`) deletes the answer's `session_id` through `useDeleteSession`. The toast and the delete are both in the hook's own options, so Undo still works once the sheet is gone. A copy has no logged set, so the delete guards let it go. Only the row being copied waits (`isPending` and `variables.sessionId`).
  - **`copy_sessions(p_session_ids, p_dest_week_id)`** makes one copy per entry of the list, in the order of the list, after every session the phase holds (archived ones included), with the titles as they are and none archived, and returns `{ session_ids, week_id, slots, set_logs, slots_left_out }` — `session_ids` in the order of the list. An entry the caller cannot read refuses the whole list (`session_not_found`); an empty list copies nothing.
  - **`duplicate_week(p_week_id, p_dest_program_id default null, p_label_suffix default ' (copy)')`** adds the phase as the LAST phase of its program — or of `p_dest_program_id`, which is how "copy this phase to…" reaches another block or another athlete — with its sessions in their order, numbered from 0, the archived ones still archived. A phase without a name gives a copy without one. Returns `{ week_id, program_id, week_number, label, sessions, slots, set_logs, slots_left_out }`.
  - **`duplicate_program(p_program_id, p_target_student_id default null, p_name_suffix default ' (copy)')`** makes a block for the athlete of the source, or for `p_target_student_id` — the id of a row of `students`, what `programs.student_id` holds, not a profile. The copy is `approved`, **not active**, out of the trash and the coach's (`created_by` NULL) whatever its source is (a draft, a block in the trash, the block being trained), last in the athlete's list, its phases numbered from 1 with their names as they are. Returns `{ program_id, student_id, name, sort_order, weeks, sessions, slots, set_logs, slots_left_out }`.
- **The answer is an object, and the id is in it under a fixed key.** Keys are added, never renamed. The arguments that have a default may be left out of the call: the hooks send no suffix, so a copy is named with the function's own `' (copy)'`. A hook returns the answer as it is — what a caller reads the new id from (`session_id`, `session_ids`, `week_id`). `useDuplicateProgram` adds one key, `id` (the value of `program_id`): "Duplicate program" selects the copy by it, and the hook seeds `['programs', <student_id of the answer>]` with a row of `useProgramsForStudent` — `approved`, inactive, not submitted, `weeks: []` — in its own `onSuccess`, which runs before the caller's, because `CoachHome` drops a `?program` that is not in the list.
  - **The refusals are `P0001` with a fixed word as the message** (HTTP 400): `session_not_found`, `week_not_found`, `program_not_found`, `student_not_found` (nothing with this id that the caller can read — somebody else's athlete and a wrong id are the same answer) and `copy_source_changed` (what was to be copied was deleted or moved while the call waited). `not authorized` is `42501` (the caller reads the place and may not write it — the athlete) and reaches the toast as `errors.notAllowed`; the others fall to `errors.generic` in `mutationErrorKey`. Nothing was written in any of those cases, so a hook sends a refused copy once, never again by itself, and has nothing to clean up. It reads the caches again in `onSettled` — after a refusal too, because what the functions refuse is something the page still shows (a session deleted in another tab, a block that went to the trash): `['program']`, `['week']`, `['session']`, `['set-logs']`, `['student-program-details']` and the coach dashboard for the four, plus `['programs']` — the lists of every athlete, whose rows count their phases — for a new phase and a new block.
  - **Two copies into one phase, one program or one athlete's list wait for each other**, and so do a copy and a reorder of the same phase: the parent is locked first, in the order `reorder_sessions` and `reorder_weeks` take it.
- **A copy is the prescription, and nothing of what was trained.** Copied: a session's title and recommended weekday; everything the coach wrote on an exercise (`exercise_id`, `sets`, the slot's own numbers, `sort_order`, `superset_group`, `notes`, `record_video_set_numbers`); the number and the four `target_*` of each prescribed set. Not copied: `scheduled_date`, `reviewed_at`, `performed_at`; `done`, `failed`, `skipped`, `rpe`, `weight_kg`, `actual_*`, `logged_at`, `failed_at`; the sets the athlete added (`is_student_added`); `slot_comments`, `slot_deviations`, videos, confirmations. `archived_at` is carried by `duplicate_week` and `duplicate_program` only. A set of a draft does not exist before `approve_program`, so the copy of a draft holds exercises without sets: the athlete's app adds them when the session is opened (`useEnsureSetLogs`). This is what the app copied with its own requests until wave 2, column for column — `supabase/tests/copy-rpcs.test.js` replays those requests and holds the two against each other — with two differences the coach sees: the copy of a session stands RIGHT AFTER its source instead of at the end of the phase, and a duplicated phase or block has its sessions numbered from 0 and its phases from 1 whatever the numbers of the source were.
- **`slots_left_out` is not zero when exercises could not be copied, and the app must say so.** A slot takes an exercise of the library of its program's coach (`trg_check_slot_exercise_in_coach_library`). After `transfer_student`, an athlete's older blocks still name the former coach's exercises: a copy of one holds the sessions and the exercises that are in the new coach's library, and `slots_left_out` counts the others — the function does not fail. Without a change of coach it is always `0`. The four hooks of [useDuplicate.js](../src/hooks/useDuplicate.js) say so through one `useLeftOutNotice`: an `info` toast with the count (`coachDuplicateRpc.leftOut`), pushed from the hook's own `onSuccess` so that it is shown whatever became of the component that asked for the copy. A fifth caller of one of the functions goes through it too — the copy looks complete and is not.
- **A column added to `programs`, `weeks`, `sessions`, `exercise_slots` or `set_logs` is listed in `WHAT_A_COPY_IS`** at the top of `supabase/tests/copy-rpcs.test.js` as `copied` or `left`, or the DB suite fails. A copied column is added to the three `INSERT`s between the marks `-- >>> the copy` and `-- <<< the copy`, which are the same lines in the four functions, in the migration that re-creates them and in `schema.sql` (`supabase/migrations/README.md`, "A migration that adds a column to a program, a phase, a session, an exercise slot or a set").
- **The missing sets of a session, and the registration of a device for notifications, each have a function that does it for the account that calls.** Migration `2026_10_13_13_ensure_claim_rpcs.sql` (the database's half of review L8, P22 and P15) adds two `SECURITY DEFINER` functions, executable by a signed-in user, each of which asks who calls before it does anything. `useEnsureSetLogs` calls the first, with the id of the session and nothing else, when the session page counts a prescribed set without a row (the bullet "**Opening a session starts no write, and nothing that could wait for a network.**" under "Offline-safe student writes"); `enablePush`, `reconcilePushSubscription` and `resumePushSubscription` of `lib/pushNotifications.js` register a device through `claim_push_subscription` and through nothing else (P15; "Web Push" above). The contract:
  - **`ensure_set_logs(p_session_id)`** returns an `integer`: how many rows of `set_logs` it added, `0` when none was missing. For every exercise of the session it adds every number from 1 to `exercise_slots.sets` that has no row — read in the database at the moment of the call, never sent by the app — not done, with the targets of the exercise (`reps` or `duration_seconds`, `weight_kg`, `rest_seconds`), which is what the editor and `approve_program` write. A row that stands on a number is left alone, an extra set of the athlete included; nothing that exists is touched; a second call adds nothing. It refuses, before any write: `not authorized` (`42501`) when the request carries no user id or the session is not the caller's own — somebody else's session, the session of an athlete the caller coaches and no such session are the same answer; `program_not_active` when the session is the caller's in a program that is not the block being trained (a past block, a draft, a block in the trash); `session_not_writable` when the coach has archived or reviewed it. A confirmed session that is not reviewed yet is still completed.
  - **`claim_push_subscription(p_endpoint, p_p256dh, p_auth, p_user_agent default null)`** returns `{ subscription_id, outcome }`. `outcome` is `created` (nobody held the endpoint: a new row for the caller), `refreshed` (the caller held it: the keys sent are stored, `last_seen_at` is now, and the user agent is replaced unless it is left out or `null`) or `rehomed` (another account held it **with these two keys**: the row keeps its id and is the caller's now — the other account no longer reads it, and receives nothing on that device). It refuses, before any write: `not authorized` (`42501`) without a user id; `subscription_incomplete` when the endpoint or one of the two keys is `null`, empty or blank; `endpoint_keys_mismatch` when another account holds the endpoint and the keys sent are not the ones stored with it. Any signed-in account may call it — an athlete, a coach, an account nobody coaches — as any account may register a device today.
  - **The refusals other than `not authorized` are `P0001` with that fixed word as the message** (HTTP 400) and fall to `errors.generic` in `mutationErrorKey` until a hook maps them; `not authorized` reaches the toast as `errors.notAllowed`. The refusals of `claim_push_subscription` do not go through `mutationErrorKey` at all: `refusal` in `lib/pushNotifications.js` turns them into the codes of that file (`endpoint_keys_mismatch` → `keys_mismatch`, `42501` → `not_signed_in`, anything else → `generic`), and the notification toggle prints the sentence of the code. Nothing was written in any of those cases. Keys and outcomes of the answer of `claim_push_subscription` are added, never renamed.
  - **A hook that calls `ensure_set_logs` does not call it for a session it shows as read-only**: the function refuses, and every opening of a past session would log an error. It needs no client-side list of what is missing to be correct — the function decides — only to know whether a call is worth sending.
  - **Two calls at once are harmless**: two `ensure_set_logs` for one session wait for each other's rows and the second answers `0`; two claims of one endpoint are served one after the other, and the second is judged against what the first left.
- **`app.trusted_set_log_insert` is the mark of `ensure_set_logs`, and nothing else may set it.** The function sets it to `'on'` for the transaction around its one `INSERT` and back to `'off'` before it returns. Nothing reads it yet: it is how a contract migration that turns an athlete's own `INSERT` into an extra set without targets (review L6, migration 43 of `docs/BACKLOG.md`, not written) would tell a prescribed set the function adds from one the athlete inserts by hand — `auth.uid()` is the athlete in both. An API user cannot set it because they run no statement of their own: a function executable by `authenticated` that sets a setting named by its caller would hand every athlete the word. `ensure-claim.test.js` reads the body of the function: one `INSERT`, between the two lines.
- **A push subscription changes hands only with its keys.** `push_subscriptions` holds one row per endpoint, and `auth` is a secret the browser shares with the database — readable by the owner of the row and by the service role only. `claim_push_subscription` moves the row of another account to the caller on that proof and on nothing else; somebody who has learnt an endpoint cannot point it at their account. The four owner-only policies of the table stay: the app deployed before wave 2 writes the table itself.
- **No mutation failure is silent** (2026_07_13). `queryClient`'s `MutationCache.onError` routes every unhandled mutation error through [`lib/mutationErrors.js`](../src/lib/mutationErrors.js) (PG SQLSTATE / constraint-name → i18n key) into a global toast ([`lib/toast.js`](../src/lib/toast.js) + [`ui/ToastHost`](../src/components/ui/ToastHost.jsx) in AppShell). A mutation that renders its OWN inline error opts out with `meta: { skipErrorToast: true }` (the message composers, `useHardDeleteProgram`) — and so does one whose message holds something the cache cannot know: `useArchiveSessions` pushes its own toast from `onError`, "N of M archived" when some rows landed and the line of `mutationErrorKey` when none did. A hook that opts out says something for EVERY error, or the failure is silent again. Production crashes report to the `client_errors` table via [`lib/errorReporter.js`](../src/lib/errorReporter.js) (window.onerror + unhandledrejection + `ErrorBoundary.componentDidCatch`) — insert-own / coach-read RLS, `role` pinned to the real profile role, `url` redacted of auth-token hashes, skipped entirely when unauthenticated. Coach triage view: a collapsible list at the bottom of the Athletes roster (CoachHome).
- **`client_errors` also holds lines the database wrote: `role = 'database'`, `user_id` NULL.** Where pg_cron is switched on, `record_push_failures()` (migration `2026_10_13_19_pg_cron_jobs.sql`) writes one line per hour that is over and in which a request the database sent through pg_net was not answered with a status from 200 to 299 — "Push: 3 of 41 requests the database sent to send-push failed between … UTC — 2 × HTTP 502, 1 × no answer in time" — with the hour as its `url` (`pg_net 2026-10-13T14:00Z`), which is also what keeps an hour from being written twice. Review P35: a push that could not be sent was in the log of a function and nowhere else. No signed-in user can write such a row (the `INSERT` policy holds a report to the account and the role of its author), `useClientErrors` shows it like any other line (the role in capitals, the message, the `url`), and `run_retention()` deletes it after 90 days. "Authors and their coach read client errors" (migration `2026_10_16_22_client_errors_scope.sql`) keeps the lines without a `user_id` readable by every coach, and `trg_cap_client_errors` lets them through: it judges a request that carries a user id only. And `send-push` answers 2xx for everything that is not a failure — an account without a device included — or the list fills with lines that mean nothing.
- **A client error is read by its author and by the coach of its author; a line without an author by every coach.** "Authors and their coach read client errors" (migration `2026_10_16_22_client_errors_scope.sql`, review B6, L20) replaced "Coaches read client errors", under which every coach read every line — the message, the stack, the page and the browser of the athletes of any coach. "The coach of the author" is `students.coach_id` today: a line of a former athlete is read by its author only, and one of a deleted account (`ON DELETE SET NULL`) by every coach. "Is a coach" is asked of `profiles` in the policy itself, not with `is_coach()`. `useClientErrors` is unchanged: it reads for a coach only.
