- **An account keeps 30 client errors an hour, dated when they arrive.** `trg_cap_client_errors` (`cap_client_errors()`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `BEFORE INSERT`) sets `created_at := now()` for a request that carries a user id — a date the client sends would escape both the hour and `run_retention()` — and drops the line without a word (`RETURN NULL`) once the caller has 30 lines of the last hour, counted by `auth.uid()`. The request answers as if the line was kept; `errorReporter.js` ignores the answer either way. The owner, the service role, the job of migration 19 and a restore are not judged. Two requests in the same instant can both pass at 29: a ceiling of about 30, not a count. Nothing old is deleted there: `run_retention()` does.
- **A toast with an action is never merged with another, and its action is a way back, not a commit.** `pushToast` de-dupes on (kind, message) so that a burst of the same failure is one line — but only among toasts without an `action`: two "Set removed" toasts carry two different callbacks, and merging them gives the second set the undo of the first. The host calls `action.onClick` at most once and only on the button; a toast that runs out, is dismissed or is pushed off the screen (the store keeps the three newest) calls nothing, so the write the toast announces must already be made. `pushToast`'s signature and the exports of [`lib/toast.js`](../src/lib/toast.js) (`pushToast`, `dismissToast`, `getToasts`, `subscribeToasts`) are what other modules and their mocks rely on. Tests: `lib/toast.test.js`, `ui/ToastHost.test.jsx`.
- **A SECURITY DEFINER RPC refuses a caller without a user id first, and compares with `IS DISTINCT FROM`.** A definer bypasses RLS, so the line that compares the caller with the owner of the data is the whole gate. Written `IF v_coach_id IS NULL OR v_coach_id <> auth.uid() THEN RAISE`, it lets everyone without a session through: with `auth.uid()` NULL the comparison is NULL, not true, and an `IF` on NULL skips its branch. `adopt_swap`, `adopt_skip`, `decline_promote_request`, `approve_program` and `send_back_program` authorize with `IF auth.uid() IS NULL OR v_coach_id IS DISTINCT FROM auth.uid() THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'not authorized'` (migration `2026_10_03_02_rpc_auth_null_guard.sql`, review finding P1). Keep that line, as written, whenever one of them is re-created — `CREATE OR REPLACE` replaces the whole body. `supabase/tests/rpc-auth.test.js` calls each of the five as the service role, a token of role `authenticated` with no `sub`, `postgres`, another coach, the athlete, another athlete and an athlete of another coach, and requires `not authorized` and an unchanged database; it also lists the SECURITY DEFINER functions of `public` that are neither triggers nor `STABLE` and fails when one appears that is not in its table of calls. The service role and `postgres` are refused too: neither has a user id. `anon` no longer reaches the body — since migration 03 it may execute no function, and the same test requires `permission denied for function …` for it — but the line in the body stays the gate for every caller that does hold EXECUTE.
- **No function of `public` is executable without a session, and a signed-in user executes an allow-list.** Supabase grants `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` EXECUTE, by name, on every function `postgres` creates in `public`, on top of the EXECUTE PostgreSQL gives `PUBLIC`; `REVOKE … FROM public` next to a function removes one of those four grants. Migration `2026_10_11_03_function_grants_lockdown.sql` takes everything back from `PUBLIC`, `anon` and `authenticated` and grants `authenticated` an explicit list: the helpers the policies call and the RPCs the app calls. A policy is evaluated with the privileges of the user who queries the table, so **a helper that is missing from the list fails every query on every table whose policies name it** (`permission denied for function …`) — so does a function in a column default, a check constraint, a view or an index expression. A trigger function gets no grant: a trigger fires whatever the privileges of the user whose statement fires it (GoTrue fires `handle_new_user` as `supabase_auth_admin`, which may not execute it). `service_role` and the owner keep everything. In `schema.sql` the list is the `FUNCTION PRIVILEGES` block at the end of the file, the only place that grants or revokes on a function; it stays below every function of the file. `supabase/tests/function-acl.test.js` holds all of it against the catalog — anon and `PUBLIC` execute nothing, `authenticated` exactly the lists `HELPERS` and `RPCS` of that file, every function a policy depends on (read from `pg_depend`) is a helper of the list, every name `src/` passes to `supabase.rpc()` is an RPC of the list — and a function that joins `public` fails it until it is listed or is a trigger function.
- **A function `postgres` creates starts closed.** Since the same migration, the default privileges of `postgres` give a new function no EXECUTE for `PUBLIC` in any schema (a global default: the `PUBLIC` grant is PostgreSQL's built-in one, and a per-schema default can only add to it) and none for `anon` or `authenticated` in `public`. A function created by a migration, or by hand in the SQL editor, is executable by `postgres` and `service_role` until a `GRANT` names someone else. A migration that adds a function says so next to it (`REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION … FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated`, then `GRANT EXECUTE … TO authenticated` when a policy or the app calls it) — rules in `supabase/migrations/README.md`, "A migration that creates or re-creates a function". The defaults of `supabase_admin`, the platform's own role, are untouched: `postgres` cannot change them, and nothing of the app is created by that role.
- **Every function runs with `search_path = public, pg_temp`.** All functions of `public` carry `SET search_path = public, pg_temp` and write the names in their bodies with a schema (`public.sessions`, `auth.uid()`, `net.http_post`, `vault.decrypted_secrets`). Without the clause a `SECURITY DEFINER` function resolves names with the caller's path; with `public` alone, a temporary object of the caller still comes first. `CREATE OR REPLACE` **drops a `SET` clause the new text does not repeat** (and keeps the grants): repeat it whenever a function is re-created, in the migration and in `schema.sql`. `function-acl.test.js` fails on any function of `public` whose setting is not exactly that one.
- **No table of `public` is reachable without a session, and a signed-in user holds the four privileges about rows — nothing else.** Supabase grants `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` every privilege, by name, on every table `postgres` creates in `public`: row-level security was the only gate, and it is a gate for rows. `TRUNCATE` is not subject to it and fires none of the `DELETE` triggers that guard logged training — `TRUNCATE public.profiles CASCADE`, run as `anon` or as any signed-in user, emptied every table of the app. Migration `2026_10_13_14_table_grants_lockdown.sql` (review L24) takes everything on every table, view and sequence of `public` away from `anon` and `PUBLIC`, and leaves `authenticated` `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on a table — the four the policies judge — and `USAGE` on a sequence; `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, `TRIGGER` and `MAINTAIN` are the owner's and the service role's. In `schema.sql` this is the `TABLE PRIVILEGES` block at the end of the file, the only place that grants or revokes on a table of `public`; it stays below every table, view and sequence of the file. `supabase/tests/table-acl.test.js` holds it against the catalog, for every table there is. A `SECURITY INVOKER` function runs with these rights: one that needs more than rows — a `TRUNCATE`, a `REINDEX` — fails for every signed-in user, and is written `SECURITY DEFINER` with its own check of who calls instead. A row lock (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, which the functions of migrations 11 and 12 take) asks for `UPDATE`, which stays.
- **Every table of `public` has row-level security, and a table `postgres` creates starts with the privileges of the others.** The default privileges of `postgres` in `public` give a new table nothing for `anon` and the four privileges for `authenticated` — so a table created without `ALTER TABLE … ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, by a migration or by hand in the SQL editor, is read and written in full by every signed-in account. `table-acl.test.js` fails on a table without row-level security, and on a view that is not `WITH (security_invoker = true)`: a view reads its tables with the rights of its owner, whom row-level security does not hold, and shows every row to whoever may read it. A migration that adds a table follows "A migration that creates a table, a view or a sequence" in `supabase/migrations/README.md`. The defaults of `supabase_admin`, the platform's own role, are untouched — `postgres` cannot change them.
- **Nothing is read or written without a session.** A request that carries only the anon key is refused by every table of `public` — `permission denied for table …`, `42501` — before a policy is evaluated, and by every function (migration 03). The app sends none: the login page talks to `/auth/v1` only, and the one way there is a session that expired and could not be renewed, where `mutationErrorKey` shows the refusal as `errors.notAllowed` and a read fails instead of resolving to no rows. A feature that must serve something without a session cannot do it with a policy alone: it needs a deliberate `GRANT … TO anon` on one table or one function, which `table-acl.test.js` and `function-acl.test.js` refuse until the rule is changed there, with the reason.
- **Rows nobody reads any more are deleted by `run_retention()`, by their age — and nothing an athlete trained is ever deleted that way.** Three tables fill by themselves and nothing deleted a row of them (review L27): `scheduled_pushes`, `notifications`, `client_errors`. Since migration `2026_10_13_18_retention.sql` one function deletes a rest push 7 days after it was written — never one that is still to be sent: `status = 'pending' AND canceled_at IS NULL AND fire_at > now() - interval '60 seconds'`, what `claim_rest_push` would still hand to a sender —, a notification 90 days after its recipient READ it — never one that was not read —, and a client error 90 days after it was reported, and answers `{ "scheduled_pushes": n, "notifications": n, "client_errors": n }`. Every table of `public` is in one of the two lists of `supabase/tests/retention.test.js` — `DELETED_BY_A_RUN` with its age, `KEPT_FOR_GOOD` with the reason — and the test fails on a table that is in neither: a new table that grows with events gets its `DELETE` in that function, and a table of training never does. The 7 days are also the age at which `schedule_rest_push` deletes the caller's own rows; the test holds the two to the same rows.
- **`run_retention()` is run by the owner or by a job, and waits for nobody.** Nothing calls it: no trigger, no other function, no app, and applying a migration never does — the test refuses a function of `public` whose text names it. The owner runs it once a month (`docs/runbook/70-admin-procedures.md`), or the nightly pg_cron job of the optional migration 19 does; without a run nothing breaks, the rows wait. It is `SECURITY INVOKER` and executable by its owner and the service role only (`NOT_FOR_USERS` of `function-acl.test.js`): given to `authenticated` it would delete what that user's policies allow — their own rest pushes — and nothing else. Each of its `DELETE`s takes its rows through `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, so a row another request holds — an account that is starting its next rest — is passed over and goes with the next run: a run never blocks the app and never deadlocks with it.
- **A migration that needs an extension the project may not have is `optional`, names the extension in the manifest, and exists in `schema.sql` inside one block.** `"extension": "pg_cron"` in `supabase/migrations/manifest.json` is what the tests go by. In `schema.sql` everything such a migration makes — a function included, created with `EXECUTE` — stands inside one `DO` block that asks `pg_extension` first and does nothing without the extension, and its line of the ledger is a separate statement under the same condition: a database installed without pg_cron is production without migration 19, to the last object, and that is the database every test file runs on. `parity.test.js` compares both projects. A function of such a migration is therefore in no list of `function-acl.test.js`: `cron-jobs.test.js` holds who may execute `record_push_failures()`, with whose rights it runs and its `search_path`. No later migration and no client may need what such a migration makes, and its rollback is never refused for a later migration.
- **A job is made, changed and removed through pg_cron's functions, never by writing `cron.job`.** `postgres` reads that table and may not write it — on Supabase, and in the stub of the suite (`supabase/tests/shim-pg-cron.sql`): `cron.schedule(name, schedule, command)`, `cron.alter_job(jobid, active := …)`, `cron.unschedule(name)`. `cron.schedule` on a name that exists replaces the schedule and the command of that job and leaves it switched off if it was. The four definitions are one text in the migration, the block of `schema.sql`, the audit, the rollback, the two runbook pages and `JOBS` of `cron-jobs.test.js`, which compares them; `supabase/migrations/README.md` ("A migration that adds a job, or changes what one runs") has the rules.
- **Where the jobs run, old rows are deleted without anybody asking.** `sl-retention` runs `run_retention()` every night: the monthly routine of `docs/runbook/70-admin-procedures.md` is then nobody's, and applying migration 19 is agreeing to the first run — nearly every rest push there is, the notifications read more than 90 days ago, the client errors older than 90 days.

## UserMenu / profile surface

- **`UserMenu` is role-aware.** Pass `profileHref="/student/profile"` on student pages → the avatar becomes a direct `<Link>` to the Profile page (no popover). Coach pages omit the prop → avatar opens the legacy popover with theme/language/sign-out inline. The student popover has been retired; theme, language, and sign-out live exclusively in [StudentProfile.jsx](../src/components/student/StudentProfile.jsx). When adding a new student-side top-level page, remember to pass `profileHref` so the avatar keeps working.
- **The bell's popover hangs from the `UserMenu` group, not from the bell.** The group (`relative flex items-center gap-2 shrink-0`, in both modes) is the positioned box, and the wrapper of [NotificationBell.jsx](../src/components/notifications/NotificationBell.jsx) is not: the popover (`absolute right-0 top-12 w-[320px] max-w-[calc(100vw-2rem)]`) ends at the avatar's right edge, which is the page's right padding, and fits a screen of 320 to 390px. Anchored to the bell, an avatar further in, it began at `100vw - 384px`, off the left edge of a phone, in a `main` that does not scroll sideways. So a page that mounts `UserMenu` keeps it at its 16px right padding, and `NotificationBell` is mounted only inside `UserMenu`. The group takes no z-index: that would make it a stacking context, and the popovers' `z-30` (bell) and `z-20` (avatar menu) would count only inside it. The bell, the avatar and the bell's "Mark all read" (`sl-pill sl-pill-btn`) are 44px targets. Tests: `ui/UserMenu.popover.test.jsx` (the real bell inside the real menu, both modes), `notifications/NotificationBell.test.jsx`.
- **Student bottom-nav has exactly 3 tabs: Training, Stats, Messages.** It was four until Home and Sessions merged into one training surface (see the queue section). Goals is **not** a tab — it's reached from the Profile page's "Active goal" card via "View all". Bookmarks and deep links to `/student/goals` still work. Going back above three cramps mobile layout — extend Profile instead.
- **Student Profile is the avatar-tap destination on every student page.** It owns: display-name edit, coach card, lifetime totals, active goal, theme/language, email + change-password + sign-out. Don't add duplicate copies of these settings elsewhere on the student side.
- **Changing the password asks for the current one, and checks it with a sign-in before anything is changed.** `ChangePasswordDialog` in `student/StudentProfile.jsx` runs the checks that need no network first — current password given, new one of 8 characters, both new fields equal — so a new password that would be refused costs no sign-in attempt; then `signIn(user.email, current)` of `useAuth`, and `updatePassword` only when that sign-in returned no error. A wrong password is GoTrue's `invalid_credentials` (or a bare 400 without a code) and says `profilePasswordRecheck.currentWrong`; no network, a rate limit (429) or any other refusal says `profilePasswordRecheck.checkFailed` — never "wrong", and never the server's English. The check is a real sign-in of the same account: auth-js announces it as a `SIGNED_IN` of a session already known, which the provider does not act on (no `replayQueuedWrites`, `signOuts` unchanged), and a failed one leaves the session as it was. `signIn` must keep both properties, or a password change replays the queue or signs the athlete out. One submit runs at a time (a ref, not only the disabled button); Cancel during the check abandons the attempt, so `updatePassword` is never called behind a closed dialog — a change already sent cannot be called back, and its answer is dropped. Success and close clear the three fields. Server-side re-authentication ("Secure password change") stays off (the bullet "Public sign-up is off" under RLS / DB conventions). A forgotten password cannot be changed here: the app has no reset, and the owner hands over a password he chose (`docs/runbook/20-auth-config.md`).

## Messaging / notifications

- **Notifications are SECURITY DEFINER trigger-driven.** The `notifications` table has SELECT/UPDATE policies for the recipient only — there is **no client INSERT policy**. Events get into the table exclusively via DB triggers (`notify_coach_on_session_confirm` on `session_confirmations`; `notify_student_on_session_feedback` on `messages` when `session_id IS NOT NULL`). Adding a new event type = new trigger + new client `describeNotification` branch + new i18n key. The bell lives inside `ui/UserMenu` so every top-level page gets it for free; realtime updates are wired through `useNotificationsRealtime` mounted once in `AppShell`. Like messages, the table is in `supabase_realtime` with `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL`.
- **A notification about something the athlete can take back is removed with it, while it is unread.** Two AFTER DELETE triggers of migration `2026_10_13_09_review_lifecycle.sql` (review P57, L12): `trg_retract_session_completed_notification` on `session_confirmations` deletes the unread `session_completed` rows of the session (matched on `payload->>'session_id'` and `payload->>'confirmation_id'`), and `trg_retract_slot_deviation_notifications` on `slot_deviations` deletes the unread `session_deviation` and `promote_requested` rows of the exercise slot (`payload->>'slot_id'`). So confirm, undo, confirm leaves one line in the coach's bell, and a skip that is undone leaves none. A row the coach has read stays: it is their history, and its link may lead to a review page that says the session is not confirmed. Both fire on a cascade too — a session or an exercise that is deleted. They are the only DELETE on `notifications`: there is still no client policy for it. `useNotificationsRealtime` hears them on its unfiltered `DELETE` binding (`notificationBindings` in [lib/notificationCache.js](../src/lib/notificationCache.js); its other binding is `recipient_id=eq.<me>`), which carries the id alone: the line is taken out of `['notifications', 'list', me]` and an unread one lowers `['notifications', 'unread-count', me]`; an id that a whole list shorter than `NOTIFICATIONS_PAGE_SIZE` does not hold was someone else's and changes nothing, and one a full page does not hold reads the count again; a list with a read in flight is read again once that read has answered, since it may answer with the line. **A new kind of notification about a row that can be taken back names that row in its payload and gets its own AFTER DELETE trigger**; without it the bell keeps a line about something that no longer exists. The push of a confirmation carries the tag `session-confirm-<session id>`: with the id of the confirmation, which is a new one after each undo, the pushes of one session stacked on the lock screen — keep the tag when `notify_coach_on_session_confirm` is re-created.
- **The bell is not a history: a line its recipient read more than 90 days ago is deleted.** `run_retention()` (migration `2026_10_13_18_retention.sql`) deletes a notification 90 days after `read_at` — counted from when it was read, so a line opened today stays 90 days however old it is — and never one whose `read_at` is NULL. The unread count never moves because of it, and the list of the 30 most recent lines changes only for an account with fewer than 30 newer ones. A screen that must show what happened long ago reads the thing itself — the confirmation, the message, the swap — not its notification. The delete reaches an open app as a realtime `DELETE` event, which `useNotificationsRealtime` hears on its unfiltered `DELETE` binding: the line leaves the bell at once, and an unread count is never lowered by it, since a run deletes read lines only.
- **Ordinary chat messages fan out a Web Push too — but never a bell row** (2026_07_12). A second AFTER INSERT trigger on `messages`, `notify_recipient_on_chat_message`, fires only when `session_id IS NULL` (feedback rows keep their dedicated trigger below). It deliberately inserts NO `notifications` row — the Messages-tab unread badge is chat's in-app surface, and a bell entry would double-count. Payload quirks: tag `chat-<sender_id>` collapses a burst from one sender into one lock-screen entry; the deep-link surface and the no-name title fallback both derive from the SENDER's role (recipient surface = the opposite role). Same `send_push_event` call inside a best-effort `EXCEPTION` block as the feedback push; nothing is queued while the vault is not configured.
- **A student-surface write is either offline-keyed or explicitly gated — [offlineSafety.test.js](../src/lib/offlineSafety.test.js) enforces it.** The scan reads every `export function use…` in the student-write hook files and fails the build unless each mutation either carries a `mutationKey` (so a hydrated post-reload resume can find its `mutationFn`) or is named in `ONLINE_ONLY` with a reason. Two things to keep true when editing it: **add new student-write hook FILES to `FILES`** (useGoals, useBodyweight and useSetVideo were outside the scan for months), and remember it detects a write hook by `useMutation` *or* a `mutationFn` — a hook that delegates to a local factory would otherwise be skipped as a read hook and pass vacuously. Every `ONLINE_ONLY` entry is a promise that the write does not START without a network, and the same file checks it: the hook reads the state of the network itself, or every source file that calls the hook does — `useOnlineStatus()`, `onlineManager.isOnline()` or `navigator.onLine`. Without the guard such a write does not fail offline: it pauses, runs whenever the network returns on whatever the page held at the time, and — not being part of the queue — is gone if the app is closed first. The check is per file, so it cannot tell that the value is used on the right line; what it catches is an entry added with nothing near it looking at the network. A second scan in that file, "a write of the queue can be sent twice", holds the functions of `MUTATION_FNS` to `WHAT_A_QUEUED_WRITE_SENDS` — see the bullet on what the device stores, under "Offline-safe student writes".
- **Messaging composers are online-only, by explicit gate.** `useSendMessage` (and `useMarkSessionReviewed`) are NOT in the offline mutation registry, so an offline submit would pause on "Sending…" forever and a reload would silently drop it (no registered mutationFn to resume). Both [MessageComposer.jsx](../src/components/messaging/MessageComposer.jsx) and the coach's [SessionFeedbackComposer.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionFeedbackComposer.jsx) gate on `useOnlineStatus()` and show an offline hint; the draft stays in the box. If you add a new send surface, add the gate (or do the real fix: register the mutation with a client-generated id).
- **Enter sends a message only with a fine pointer, and never during an IME composition.** [MessageComposer.jsx](../src/components/messaging/MessageComposer.jsx) asks `window.matchMedia('(pointer: coarse)')` at the moment of the key (`touchScreen`): on a touch screen Enter is a newline and the Send button is the only way to send, because a soft keyboard has no Shift and a phone could otherwise not write a message of two lines; with a mouse Enter sends, and Shift, Ctrl or Meta with Enter is a newline. An Enter that confirms an IME conversion (`nativeEvent.isComposing`, or `keyCode` 229, which Safari gives once the composition is already over) sends nothing, on any device. `MessageComposer.test.jsx` stubs `window.matchMedia` for the touch case; the polyfill of `src/test/setup.js` answers `matches: false`, the mouse case.
- **A new message does not pull a reader out of the history.** [MessageThread.jsx](../src/components/messaging/MessageThread.jsx) records at each `scroll` of the log whether the reader is within `NEAR_BOTTOM_PX` (80px) of its end (`nearBottomRef`), and a new last message scrolls the thread to the bottom only then, or when the message is the reader's own. The flag starts true, so a thread opens at its bottom, and it is set back to true when `otherProfileId` changes, because `coach/CoachMessages` switches counterparts without remounting the thread. A bubble carries `wrap-anywhere` (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), not `break-words`: `break-words` leaves a long URL as the bubble's smallest width, the shrink-to-fit columns around the bubble keep that width, and the thread scrolls sideways.
- **Feedback notifications also fan out a Web Push.** Inside `notify_student_on_session_feedback`, after the in-app `notifications` INSERT, the trigger asks `send_push_event` for a push to the athlete; the [`send-push`](../supabase/functions/send-push/index.ts) Edge Function fans the payload out to every `push_subscriptions` row of the recipient — the web-push + VAPID stack of the rest timer. The call stands in an `EXCEPTION` block: when the request cannot be queued the trigger logs `send-push fan-out failed: …` and goes on, and when the vault is not configured nothing is queued and nothing is logged — the in-app bell path is never blocked. Whether the push arrived is not known to the database. Push delivery is one-shot (no `scheduled_pushes` row) because the event fires immediately; the unique partial index on `messages.session_id` already keeps it idempotent. The payload's `data.url` deep-links to `/sl_app/#/student/session/<id>` so `notificationclick` lands on the structured feedback card in `SessionView`.
- **Coach feedback messages render as session-reference cards.** Coach-authored messages with `session_id` set show a tappable "Re: <session>" card above the bubble — coach-side it deep-links to `/coach/student/:studentId/session/:sessionId/review`, student-side to `/student/session/:sessionId`. The Messages nav tab carries an unread-count badge fed by `useUnreadMessageCount`. The coach enters a thread either from `/coach/messages` or via the **Message** action at the top of the athlete page (`StudentOverview`).
- **Student surfaces feedback in two places.** [SessionView.jsx](../src/components/student/SessionView.jsx) reads `useSessionFeedback(sessionId)` and renders the body inline as a "Coach's feedback" card between the progress bar and slot groups when a feedback row exists — this is the structured destination the `SessionReferenceCard` deep-link from Messages lands on. the training list says « Retour du coach » on the row (or in the facts of the next session) of a session that has feedback; [StudentTraining.jsx](../src/components/student/StudentTraining.jsx) feeds it from `useMyFeedbackSessionIds()` (a single batched query for the student-recipient feedback rows). New incoming feedback is patched into `['messages', 'my-feedback-session-ids', me]` and `['messages', 'session-feedback', sessionId]` by `useMessagesRealtime`, so the mark and the card appear without a manual refresh.
- **Messaging is realtime + RLS-scoped.** `messages` rows have `sender_id`, `recipient_id` (both `profiles.id`), and an optional `session_id` (FK to `sessions`, ON DELETE SET NULL) used by the coach-feedback flow. RLS lets either party SELECT, only the sender INSERT (and only when the pair is coach-student via `profiles_are_coach_student()`); when `session_id` is non-null the policy also requires sender = `coach_profile_for_session(session_id)` and recipient = `student_profile_for_session(session_id)` so the link can't be forged. Only the recipient UPDATEs — the `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (`lock_message_fields_on_update`) pins every column except `read_at`, `session_id` included, except when the session it names is gone: then the UPDATE is the `ON DELETE SET NULL` of the foreign key, and NULL goes through (migration `2026_10_16_24_indexes_cleanup.sql`, review N9). Putting the id back left a feedback message naming a deleted session, and made the delete fail within the transaction that wrote the message. A feedback message whose session was deleted is an ordinary message: its sender may delete it. The sender DELETEs their own ordinary chat messages; the policy gates DELETE on `session_id IS NULL` so coach feedback rows (which set `sessions.reviewed_at` via `notify_student_on_session_feedback`) can't be removed and the one-shot review invariant holds. The table is in `supabase_realtime` with `REPLICA IDENTITY FULL`; [hooks/useMessages.js](../src/hooks/useMessages.js) opens **one** channel from `AppShell` (`useMessagesRealtime`) with three bindings (`messageBindings` in [lib/messageCache.js](../src/lib/messageCache.js)): `recipient_id=eq.<me>`, `sender_id=eq.<me>`, and an unfiltered `DELETE`, since a deletion carries the id alone under row-level security and Realtime does not filter it on a column. An event patches the entries that show its message and reads nothing — see the next bullet. `useSessionRefsForMessages` does a single batched fetch of titles/dates for the unique `session_id`s referenced by the visible thread. The Messages nav tab carries an unread-count badge fed by `useUnreadMessageCount`. The conversation list is one row per counterpart: `public.my_conversations()` gives it in one request over every row (migration `2026_10_16_26_coach_list_rpcs.sql`, optional; the bullet "The coach's two lists have a read function each" of "RLS / DB conventions"), and on a database without it `useConversations` reads every message of the account with `fetchAllRows` and folds them.
- **A message event changes the entries that show its message, and only them; it never invalidates the `['messages']` root.** `applyMessageChange` in [lib/messageCache.js](../src/lib/messageCache.js) puts an INSERT into its pair's thread (by time, the newest `THREAD_LIMIT` kept), the conversation list (last message, unread count, order) and `['messages', 'unread-count', me]`; an UPDATE merges the row; a DELETE removes the id from every cached thread and moves the conversation back to the message before it. Every other pair's thread keeps the same array. A message with a `session_id` also fills `['messages', 'session-feedback', sessionId]` and, for the athlete, `['messages', 'my-feedback-session-ids', me]` — the feedback card, the pill and the re-read of the session row in `SessionView` hang on those two. The rules that keep the counts right: the cache writes exactly the columns its read selects (7 for a message, 6 for the feedback entry), timestamps in PostgREST's form (`normalizeTimestamp` in [lib/realtimeCache.js](../src/lib/realtimeCache.js): Realtime hands timestamptz over as Postgres text, which Safari's `Date` cannot read); an unread count moves only by what a **whole** entry (cached, not invalidated) says the message was before, so one change applied twice — by the mutation and by its echo, by two bindings — counts once, and the id of a deleted message is remembered for that. The pair's thread and the conversation list are read at different times, and a change that lands between the two reads is in one of them only: what the later read says wins (`laterState` in `applyMessageUpsert`) — a list that holds the message as its last one over a thread that lacks it, a row that is read over one that is not (`read_at` is the one column the update trigger leaves to change, and the app sets it once and never clears it). A change that a read already had counts once when the thread or the list's last message holds it; a count read on its own between a change and its event, with neither of them holding it, counts the change a second time until the next resync — nothing in the cache tells that case apart. When no whole entry can say, the exact keys `['messages', 'unread-count', me]` and `['messages', 'conversations', me]` are read again, gathered for `REFRESH_DELAY_MS` so a burst of N events asks once (`createRefresher`); `payload.old` is never read for a column (it is the primary key alone). An entry marked as behind, or not read yet, is not patched (writing it would mark it current, or create it), and any entry with a read in flight when its event arrives is read again (`patchCached`, `refreshInFlight` in [lib/realtimeCache.js](../src/lib/realtimeCache.js)): at once when it is shown with data — the read is cancelled and sent again —, and otherwise once that read has answered. An invalidation only joins a first read, or the read of an entry nobody shows, and that read's answer — sent before the change — clears the mark, so `createRefresher` keeps such a key, a round of `REFRESH_DELAY_MS` at a time, until its read has answered. A DELETE, which carries the id alone, also reads again a thread with a read in flight that does not hold the id. `useSendMessage`, `useDeleteMessage` and `useMarkThreadRead` (which asks for the rows it changed with `.select('id, read_at')`) apply their own change through the same functions instead of invalidating the root.
- **The chat and bell channels outlive a lost connection, and the cache is read again whenever events may have been missed — and not at every switch of tab.** Both hooks open their channel through `watchPostgresChanges` in [lib/realtimeChannel.js](../src/lib/realtimeChannel.js), the only code that calls `supabase.channel` or `supabase.removeChannel`. **A channel joins with the session's token.** realtime-js puts in a join the token the client holds when `subscribe()` is called, and never sends the channel a token that arrives while that join is on its way; the server answers such a join with ok — the status is `SUBSCRIBED` — and refuses its `postgres_changes`, since `anon` reads no table (migration 14; the system message says "invalid column for filter"), so the channel hears nothing for as long as the page lives. The pages are drawn before auth-js has read the stored session ("Who is signed in comes from the device first", under "React / hooks discipline"), so `watchPostgresChanges` subscribes at once only when `supabase.realtime.accessTokenValue` is set, and otherwise once `supabase.realtime.setAuth()` — which asks supabase-js for the session's token — has settled (`untilAuthorized`); a watch stopped meanwhile joins nothing. Before that rule, every launch with a remembered role raced auth-js for the token, and the coach's unread badge, conversation list and read receipts — and the bell — stayed as they were on a launch that lost (end-to-end run of 2026-10-17, E14/E15). **A Realtime channel is never removed from an error status**: `CHANNEL_ERROR` and `TIMED_OUT` leave it to the client, which joins it again by itself, and a channel removed on its first error is never joined again — the chat and the bell then stay as they are until a reload, after every dropped socket and every iOS suspension. It is removed only after `MAX_JOIN_FAILURES` failed joins in a row with no `SUBSCRIBED` between them (a join the server refuses every time), and opened again with a new count on the next `online` or `visibilitychange`; a channel the server closed is opened again then, or once it is `REOPEN_AFTER_MS` old. Each opening has a topic of its own (`<topic>-<n>`): the client hands back a channel that is still leaving under the same name. `onResync(reason)` is called with one of `RESYNC`: `subscribed` on every `SUBSCRIBED`, the first included (what the page read while the channel was joining reached it on no channel), never held back; `online` on every `online`; `visible` on `visibilitychange` to visible only after an absence of `RESYNC_AFTER_HIDDEN_MS` (30 s, past one 25 s heartbeat of realtime-js) or more, after an absence that was not seen, or with the channel not joined — joining, in error, closed or let go. A desktop tab that is switched away from keeps its socket and is handed every event meanwhile, so a short absence with the channel joined reads nothing; a socket that dies during one is closed or fails its heartbeat, the client joins the channel again, and that `SUBSCRIBED` reads everything, a heartbeat or two later at worst. `online` and `visible` together ask once (`RESYNC_GAP_MS`), and a visible that reads nothing leaves that gap alone. Each hook answers with an invalidation of its root whose options are `resyncRefetchOptions(reason)`: `{ cancelRefetch: false }` after `online` and `visible`, so a read in flight is joined — it is most often React Query's own refetch on reconnect or focus, and a cancel would only send a second request (the thread, the conversation list and the bell's list do not pass `signal` on, so the first is not even stopped); `{ cancelRefetch: true }` after `SUBSCRIBED`, and for a resync that gives no reason, since a read sent before the join may be older than the first event the channel delivers. A read that stalled while the page was away is joined too; if the socket died meanwhile, the `SUBSCRIBED` of the channel joined again sends it again. The messages resync leaves `['messages', 'session-refs', …]` alone: it reads `sessions`.

## Coach session review

- **Coach session review is one-shot per session.** A session can be reviewed at most once — whether the coach left feedback or not. State lives on `sessions.reviewed_at timestamptz`. Two write paths set it: (1) the `notify_student_on_session_feedback` trigger stamps `reviewed_at = NEW.created_at` when a feedback message is inserted (idempotent — `WHERE reviewed_at IS NULL`), and (2) [`useMarkSessionReviewed`](../src/hooks/useSession.js) UPDATEs the row directly when the coach clicks "Finish without feedback". The top-of-page back arrow does NOT mark reviewed — it's a "leave and come back" gesture; only Send-feedback and Finish-without-feedback are completion gestures. [SessionReview.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionReview.jsx) renders four branches at its feedback step: [SessionFeedbackSent.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionFeedbackSent.jsx) (feedback exists), [SessionReviewedNoFeedback.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionReviewedNoFeedback.jsx) (`reviewed_at` set but no feedback), [SessionFeedbackComposer.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionFeedbackComposer.jsx) (not yet reviewed, and confirmed), or [SessionReviewNotConfirmed.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionReviewNotConfirmed.jsx) (not yet reviewed, and no confirmation to review — see the next entry). [SessionsFeed.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionsFeed.jsx) shows a green "Reviewed" pill on cards whose session has `reviewed_at` set; the coach's confirmation reads of [`useSessionConfirmation.js`](../src/hooks/useSessionConfirmation.js) carry it (`reviewed_at` in `CONFIRMATION_SELECT`, behind `useReviewInbox` and `useConfirmationHistory`). Feedback itself remains one-shot independently — a UNIQUE partial index on `messages(session_id) WHERE session_id IS NOT NULL` guarantees ≤ 1 feedback row per session, and the messages UPDATE trigger pins `body` + `session_id` so feedback is immutable once sent.
- **The feedback being written is kept per session until it is sent, emptied or discarded on purpose — and "Finish without feedback" asks before it discards one.** [`lib/feedbackDraft.js`](../src/lib/feedbackDraft.js) keeps it in `localStorage` under `sl_feedback_draft:<sessionId>` (`feedbackDraftKey`; `readFeedbackDraft`, `writeFeedbackDraft` — blank text removes the key —, `clearFeedbackDraft`; every access in a `try`, so a refused storage stops neither the typing nor the send). [SessionFeedbackComposer.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionFeedbackComposer.jsx) reads it once in its `useState` initializer — one composer per session, because `SessionReview` keys `SessionReviewPage` on the session —, writes it on every change, and forgets it when the send succeeds and when the coach finishes without sending (`finish`). With text that is not blank, "Finish without feedback" opens a `ConfirmDialog` ("Finish without sending?") first; Cancel leaves the text in the box and on the device. `SessionReviewPage` forgets the draft once the session says it is reviewed (`existingFeedback` or `session.reviewed_at`): the review was finished on another device, or the send succeeded after the coach had left the page and the composer could not hear it. The key is not wiped at sign-out: only the session's coach can open its review (RLS), and the composer is the only reader.
- **A review needs a confirmation, and the page offers its feedback step only on a confirmation READ SINCE IT OPENED.** The composer — which also carries "Finish without feedback", the only other caller of `useMarkSessionReviewed` — renders for a confirmed session only. The page is reached without a confirmation in ordinary ways: a session in progress opened from the sheet or from a `session_deviation` notification, an archived session that was never trained, and a `session_completed` notification that went stale because the athlete undid the confirmation. A session reviewed in that last state never came back to the inbox, which reads from `session_confirmations`. The database refuses both writes too (the review-lifecycle migration, 09); this is the client half and it does not depend on it. **The cache is not an answer here**: `['session-confirmation', sessionId]` is served for two minutes and persisted across reloads, so [SessionReview.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionReview.jsx) calls `useSessionConfirmation(sessionId, { refetchOnMount: 'always' })`, takes `openedAt` when it mounts, and derives `confirmationState` from the query's two timestamps: `confirmed` or `none` once `dataUpdatedAt >= openedAt`; `unverified` when the read failed since then (`errorUpdatedAt >= openedAt`) or waits for the network (`isPaused`); `checking` otherwise, which renders nothing. Four rules to keep when editing it:
  - **The successful read is tested first, and nothing un-reads it.** A refetch on window focus that fails later leaves a confirmed session `confirmed`. The composer is the coach's unsent text: gating it on `isSuccess`, `isError` or `isFetching` would unmount it on a flaky network — the draft comes back from `sl_feedback_draft:<sessionId>` when it mounts again, but the coach would see the box vanish under their thumb. Only a read that returns no row takes the composer away.
  - **"Not confirmed" is a statement about the athlete, made for `none` only.** A failed or parked read shows, in the composer's place, the `unverified` variant of [SessionReviewNotConfirmed.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionReviewNotConfirmed.jsx) with a "Check again" button, and the status line at the top of the page stays empty. A confirmation the cache already holds is still shown at the top without waiting (its date and the athlete's notes are content); only its absence waits for the read.
  - **`SessionReview` is a keyed wrapper around `SessionReviewPage`** (`key` = student and session ids). The router keeps a route's element mounted when only its params change, and `refetchOnMount` does not fire on a key change: without one mount per session, the first review's `openedAt` would vouch for whatever the cache holds about the second, or leave it in `checking` for good.
  - **The two read-only cards do not look at the confirmation.** Sessions reviewed before the rule existed have none, and keep saying that they were reviewed. Neither do the deviation actions (adopt, remove, decline) or archive: a deviation is notified while the session is still in progress.

  A test that mocks `useSessionConfirmation` for this page has to give the query's `dataUpdatedAt`, or the page stays in `checking` (`confirmationQuery()` in `SessionReview.test.jsx`). `SessionReview.confirmation.test.jsx` runs the page on the real hook and a real query client, so the reading of those timestamps is React Query's behaviour and not an assumption about it.
- **A review needs a confirmation and closes it — and the database holds both, for a request that carries a user id.** Migration `2026_10_13_09_review_lifecycle.sql` (review P57) ties `session_confirmations` to `sessions.reviewed_at` in three places. *A review needs a confirmation*: the trigger `trg_review_needs_confirmation` (BEFORE UPDATE on `sessions`, function `review_needs_confirmation`) refuses `reviewed_at` going from NULL to a value while the session has no confirmation — `42501`, "session … is not confirmed by its athlete — it cannot be marked as reviewed" — which is what `useMarkSessionReviewed` meets, and what `notify_student_on_session_feedback` meets when it stamps the review, so the feedback message is undone with it; the INSERT policy of `messages` asks `session_is_confirmed(session_id)` of a message that names a session, so the API answers a policy refusal first. *A review closes the confirmation*: the athlete's UPDATE and DELETE policies on `session_confirmations` ask `session_writable(session_id)` — neither archived nor reviewed — so "Undo confirmation" on a reviewed session reaches no row. *When the two cross*: a policy judges the database as its statement saw it when it started, so `block_confirmation_delete_when_reviewed` (BEFORE DELETE) reads the session again `FOR SHARE` and raises `42501` for the undo that lost the race, and `review_needs_confirmation` is `VOLATILE` so that it reads the confirmations as they are once the row of the session is locked. Rules to keep:
  - **"Reviewed" does not mean "confirmed".** Sessions reviewed without a confirmation exist from before the migration, and the owner of the database and the service role are not judged (no user id): removing an account cascades to its confirmations, and a review is taken back by hand (`docs/runbook/migrations/09-review-lifecycle.md`, "Taking a review back" — there is no reopening in the app). The trigger judges the transition from NULL to a value only: such a session is still archived, restored, renamed, reordered and moved with `move_session`.
  - **A reviewed session can still be confirmed, and that confirmation is final.** The INSERT policy of `session_confirmations` does not ask about the review, on purpose: `buildQueue` keeps a session in the athlete's list until it is confirmed or archived. Do not add `session_writable` to it.
  - **A function that marks sessions as reviewed for a coach meets the trigger**, one session or many: the whole statement is refused as soon as one of them has no confirmation. Filter on the confirmation in the statement.
  - **`review_needs_confirmation` stays `VOLATILE` and `trg_review_needs_confirmation` keeps a name that sorts after `trg_pin_draft_session_columns`.** `STABLE` reads the confirmation that has just been taken back; a name that sorts before the pin raises on a session of a draft, where the pin of migration 06 is silent. No test on one connection sees the first: `supabase/tests/review-lifecycle.test.js` has three on several connections.
  - **A confirmation sent twice is insert-or-nothing.** An upsert that updates (`ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, what the app deployed before wave 1 sends) is judged by the UPDATE policy and raises `42501` once the session is reviewed; `confirmSessionFn` sends `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, which is an INSERT only and writes nothing.
- **`SessionReview`'s back button returns to the page that opened it, and every opener has to say which page that is.** The review is reachable from the sessions feed, from a row of the Program Sheet, from the sheet's archived drawer and from the session editor's banner. Each passes `state: { from: <its own pathname + search> }` to the router, and `handleBack` — which is also what "Send feedback" and "Finish without feedback" end on — navigates there, or to `/coach/sessions` when there is none. Only a string that starts with `/coach/` is accepted. The origin is also written into `sl_last_coach_session` as `from`, next to `studentId` and `sessionId`, and read back for the same session only: the Sessions tab restores a review left open with a redirect that carries no router state. A new link into the review that passes no `from` still works, and sends the coach to the feed.
- **The review carries the way into the editor, and archives with a way back.** A 44px pill (`sl-pill sl-pill-btn`) beside "archive" links to `/coach/students/:studentId/s/:sessionId` and clears `sl_last_coach_session`, so the Sessions tab does not bounce back into a review the coach left on purpose. It is labelled "editor", not "edit": for a confirmed session the editor is read-only. "archive" is a 44px button too; once `useArchiveSession` has archived the session, an info toast says "Session archived" (`coachReviewComposerRoster.archive.done`) with Undo (`uiSharedHelpers.undo`), which sends `{ sessionId, archived: false }`. "unarchive" restores with no toast.
- **A coach page says "not found" for a read that answered with nothing, and "couldn't be loaded" for a read that did not answer — never the page itself, and never one for the other.** `useSession` reads with `.maybeSingle()` and resolves `null` for a session that is not there (deleted, or not this account's); `useProgram` does the same for a block. `SessionEditor`, `SessionReview`, `CoachHome` (the athlete route) and `StudentProgrammingSection` branch in this order: data → the page; `isSuccess` with `data == null` → `CoachNotFound` (the way back: the athlete page on the session's block, the page that opened the review or `/coach/sessions`, `/coach/students`, the current block); otherwise `readState` → spinner on `loading`, `CoachReadProblem` with "Try again" (`refetch({ cancelRefetch: false })`) on `offline` and `failed`. Both components are in [`coach/CoachPageState.jsx`](../src/components/coach/CoachPageState.jsx). The order matters: `readState` reads a missing `data` as "not answered", so the `null` check comes first. Testing `isLoading` alone is the trap: a failed read rendered the full editor, empty and writable, a review titled "Session" with a feedback form, a spinner for ever, and "No students yet" to a coach with athletes.
- **A "not found" is never where a tab comes back to.** The editor and the athletes page call `useForgetCoachStudentsPath(notFound)` ([`hooks/useRememberCoachStudentsPath.jsx`](../src/hooks/useRememberCoachStudentsPath.jsx)): it removes `sl_last_coach_students_path` and marks the path in `sl_last_coach_students_path_forgotten`, which `useRememberCoachStudentsPath` skips. The mark is load-bearing: the writer sits in `CoachHome`, above the editor, and React runs a child's effects before its parent's, so a plain removal in the same render would be written back. The mark goes with the next path the writer writes. `SessionReview` writes `sl_last_coach_session` only once its session is known, and removes a saved copy of itself when it learns the session is gone: `SessionsFeed` restores that key on every visit.
- **An athlete's draft is read-only for the coach until it is approved.** `useSession` carries the block's `status` as `program_status`; on `'draft'` the session editor is read-only as a whole (title, date, every field, no "+ Add exercise", no delete), under the banner `coachEditorNavErrors.draft.editor`, and `StudentProgrammingSection` puts `coachEditorNavErrors.draft.sheet` above the sheet. Approve, send back, rename and trash stay in the program menu (`ProgramManageDialog`). A session cached by an older build has no `program_status` and is read as approved until it is read again.
- **A drag in the session editor is one mutation, drawn at once, and leaves every superset one run of two slots or more.** `planSlotDrag` in [`lib/slotOrder.js`](../src/lib/slotOrder.js) decides it: the order renumbered 0 to n-1; the dragged slot joins the superset it is dropped between two members of, stays in its own when it lands beside a member of it, and leaves it otherwise; then a superset of one is cleared, and a group found in two runs gives its later run the id of that run's first slot. Only the rows that change are written, by `useReorderSlots(sessionId)` in `hooks/useSession.js`: one request per row, sent together (no unique constraint on `exercise_slots (session_id, sort_order)`), each `.select('id').single()` so a row not updated is an error; the order is written to `['session', sessionId]` in `onMutate`, put back on an error, and read again once, by the last drag of a run (`mutationScope('slot-order', sessionId)`, `laterWriteQueued`). `SessionEditor` is mounted once per session (`key={sessionId}`) because that scope must not change while the hook lives. The editor draws a superset as the run of adjacent slots that share `superset_group` (`groupSlotsBySuperset`), so a write that only renumbered could split one.
- **A slot whose exercise the coach cannot read comes with `exercise: null`.** RLS hides the library row of another coach (a former coach's exercise on a database without the policy of migration 23). The session editor renders a card that says so in place of `ExerciseSlotRow`, which reads the exercise; the review keeps the slot under `coachEditorNavErrors.exercise.unavailableTitle`, with its sets, swap, skip, videos and notes.
- **`SessionsFeed` treats an arrival with `?student=` as a destination.** The roster's "N to review" chip and the athlete page's "Sessions" action both link to `/coach/sessions?student=<students.id>`. On such an arrival the feed does not restore the review saved in `sl_last_coach_session` (it could be another athlete's), and its default view follows the data: the « To review » inbox when that athlete has something to review, « Done » when nothing is left, so the coach never lands on an empty page. `viewChoice` stays `null` until the coach picks a view, which is what lets the default settle after the confirmations load. A row passes the feed's own URL as `from`, so the athlete filter survives a trip into a review.
- **PLANNED is what the coach prescribed: a set the athlete added is not part of it.** A row with `is_student_added` has no target. [ReviewExercise.jsx](../src/components/coach/ReviewExercise.jsx) builds the « Planned … » line and its rest from the rows with `is_student_added` false only (`plannedLogs`); counted there, an extra set read "SET 7 (BW)". [`reviewTotals`](../src/components/coach/reviewModel.js) counts the sets done of the plan over the prescribed rows and says the extra sets done apart (« 2 more added »); [`setRows`](../src/components/coach/reviewModel.js) draws an extra set as « Set n, added » with what was done, never against a target.
- **The review and the feed date a session by the day it was TRAINED.** The confirmed banner of `SessionReview` says "Trained on {date}" (`coachReviewComposerRoster.review.trainedOn`) and a `SessionsFeed` row sits under the heading of that day — « Today », « Yesterday », the weekday within the week, the date beyond it —, both from `performedDate` ([lib/day.js](../src/lib/day.js) — `performed_on`, then `performed_at`, then `confirmed_at`) through `formatDate(lang, …)`. Never `confirmed_at` alone: for a confirmation sent offline it is the moment the queue was replayed.
- **A feed card says where its session sits as the Programming sheet says it: the phase's name and "Session n/total".** The line under a card's title is `week_label` (left out when the phase has no name) and `coach.roster.sessionOf` from [`useCoachSessionPositions`](../src/hooks/useProgram.js) (left out while that read has no answer), joined with " · ", and no line at all when both are missing. An untitled session is `coach.week.sessionN` with that place, or `coach.editor.titlePlaceholder` while it is not known. Never `week_number` or `day_number`: a phase is a free-text name and a session needs no weekday — "W1 · W1 · D2", a bare "· D" and "Session null" are what they printed.

## Coach Athletes-roster layout

- **Coach roster strip data is folded into one query and comes from the SHARED placement model.** [useCoachDashboardPrograms](../src/hooks/useProgram.js) returns, per student, `{ programName, position, totalSessions, nextSessionTitle, daysSinceLast, weekDays }`. That query is the coach's active blocks, sessions included, and nothing else: a session is done when it carries `sessions.performed_at`, which `sync_session_performed_at` sets exactly while the session has a confirmation (and migration `2026_08_21_session_performed_on.sql` filled in for the confirmations made before it). A second, unbounded read of the confirmations on those blocks said it until wave 3, and past a thousand of them PostgREST cut it without a word — done sessions came back as "next" on the cards. `weekDays` is the 7-slot Mon–Sun array from [`buildDayStrip`](../src/lib/sessionQueue.js) — the SAME function the athlete's own Home strip calls, so the coach's view of a week and the athlete's can never drift (this replaces the old shared `statusOf`). Progress is the athlete's place in the QUEUE, because "W3" said nothing about how far through a block they were once the block stopped running one-week-per-week. Rendering: [`weekFromStrip`](../src/components/student/trainingModel.js) turns the pre-resolved states into the row's `WeekDots` — done, today, the next session — and today never overrides a day already trained.
- **The Athletes roster merges the old Dashboard + Students tabs.** [CoachHome.jsx](../src/components/coach/CoachHome.jsx) landing (`/coach/students`, no `:studentId`) renders the attention-first roster: a search box + one row per athlete in « To follow » (an attention chip) or « Up to date » (avatar, the most urgent status, queue position, next session day, the week's dots) + the collapsible client-errors triage. Cards + chips + ordering come from the pure [`buildRoster`](../src/lib/coachRoster.js) over `useStudents` + `useCoachDashboardPrograms` + `useReviewCounts` (`{ [students.id]: n }`). Attention chips: **to-review** (the sessions confirmed and neither reviewed nor archived, per athlete — `coach_review_counts()`, or `reviewCountsByStudent` over the inbox on a database without it; the function is written with the same predicate, which `supabase/tests/coach-list-rpcs.test.js` holds to `reviewCountsByStudent`), **quiet Nd** (`daysSinceLast >= STALE_AFTER_DAYS`, and never for an athlete who has yet to train), **no active program** (derive from key-absence, *not* a null field, since an active program with nothing scheduled is not a gap). **A read that has not answered is unknown, never empty.** `buildRoster` takes `summary` and `reviewCounts` as null (or undefined) when their read holds no data: a null summary gives no "No program" and no "Quiet" chip and no program name, position or strip, and null counts give no "to review" chip — read as `{}`, a summary on its way or answered by a 500 put a red "No program" on every athlete and sorted them all to the top. `RosterView` asks `readState([summaryRead, countsRead], { online, restoring })`: `loading` draws one placeholder card per athlete (`role="status"`, `aria-busy`, nothing to tap — the order is not known yet, and cards drawn now would re-sort under the coach's thumb); `offline` or `failed` draws a notice with Try again, which calls `refetch({ cancelRefetch: false })` on each read that holds no data, above the cards built from what was read; `ready` is the attention-first roster. A read that holds data, however old, is used even when its refresh failed. `useClientErrors` is not part of it. `/coach` and the legacy `/coach/dashboard` both redirect here. The Athletes tab comes back to the coach's last athlete **for the rest of the launch** — `sl_last_coach_students_path` in `sessionStorage`, written by `CoachHome` and read back by its restore effect (see the coach-persistence section of [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md)) — so a detour through Sessions/Messages/Library returns to the athlete. A cold launch opens on the roster. The restore lands on the **athlete page** only (`athletePageOf` in [`hooks/useRememberCoachStudentsPath.jsx`](../src/hooks/useRememberCoachStudentsPath.jsx): `/coach/students/:studentId`, with its `?program=` when the remembered path was that page), never on the session editor beneath it, and only for an athlete still on the roster. **"‹ All athletes" and a tap on the Athletes tab while it is open both clear that key** (`clearLastCoachStudentsPath`; the tab through the item's `onReselect`): `/coach/students` and `/coach/students/:studentId` keep one `CoachHome` mounted, its restore effect runs again when `:studentId` goes away, and without the clear it bounces the coach straight back into the athlete they just left.
- **The coach's last place in each tab lasts one launch, and is read and written in one module.** [`hooks/useRememberCoachStudentsPath.jsx`](../src/hooks/useRememberCoachStudentsPath.jsx) holds the four keys — `sl_last_coach_students_path`, `sl_last_coach_students_path_forgotten`, `sl_last_coach_session`, `sl_last_coach_sessions_student` — in `sessionStorage`, behind its own functions (`getLastCoachStudentsPath` / `clearLastCoachStudentsPath`, `getLastCoachSession` / `rememberLastCoachSession` / `clearLastCoachSession`, `getLastCoachSessionsStudent` / `rememberLastCoachSessionsStudent`, `clearLastCoachSessionsTab`). `CoachHome`, `SessionsFeed`, `SessionReview` and `layout/navItems` go through them and never touch storage for these keys themselves. In `localStorage` they made every cold launch skip the attention-first roster for an athlete page or a session editor opened days before, and the Sessions tab reopen the last review. The same names in `localStorage`, left by earlier versions, are never read — the first launch after the update opens on the roster too — and are removed at the first access of a launch (`dropLegacyCoachMemory`). `getLastCoachSession` gives `null` for anything but two non-empty string ids, and keeps `from` only when it is a string. The feedback draft (`sl_feedback_draft:<sessionId>`) is not part of this: it stays in `localStorage` on purpose.
- **A tap on the coach tab that is open returns to its root.** The Athletes item of [`layout/navItems`](../src/components/layout/navItems.jsx) carries `onReselect: clearLastCoachStudentsPath`, the Sessions item `onReselect: clearLastCoachSessionsTab` (the review left open and the feed's athlete filter). `BottomNav` and `SideNav` call it from the `NavLink`'s `onClick`, which runs before the link navigates, through `reselectOnClick(event, active, onReselect)`: only when the item's `matches(pathname)` is true, and only for a plain primary click — a click with a modifier or another button opens the tab elsewhere and forgets nothing. The NavLink's own navigation then lands on the roster or the plain feed, and nothing bounces. Messages and Library need no `onReselect`: their root sends the coach nowhere.
- **The Sessions tab's number is the roster's "to review" count, summed.** [`hooks/useToReviewCount.js`](../src/hooks/useToReviewCount.js) adds up the values of `useReviewCounts` — the entry `['all-confirmations', 'review-counts', userId]` the cards' chips read — so the tab and the cards cannot disagree. It runs on every coach page, so it never reads the history: `coach_review_counts()` counts in the database, and without that function the inbox alone is read. It is `undefined` while the counts are not known, and [`layout/ReviewCountBadge`](../src/components/layout/ReviewCountBadge.jsx) draws nothing then and nothing at 0. The navigation asks this hook and nothing else. The count is as fresh as the entry: the invalidations of `['all-confirmations']` by a review, an archive and a feedback send, and the query's own refetches — a new confirmation from an athlete reaches it at the next refetch.
- **A roster card is a link STRETCHED over the card, not an anchor wrapped around it.** The card is a `relative` box; its "open this athlete" `<Link>` covers it with `after:absolute after:inset-0`. That is what allows the "N to review" chip to be a link of its own (to `/coach/sessions?student=<id>`) — an anchor cannot contain an anchor — and the chip needs `relative z-10` to sit above the stretched area, or its tap opens the athlete instead. Any other interactive element added to a card needs the same. `CoachHome.test.jsx` asserts that no `a` contains an `a`.
- **The Sessions feed opens on the inbox, and reads the rest of the history only when it is shown.** [SessionsFeed.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionsFeed.jsx): « To review » is `useReviewInbox`, filtered by athlete on the device; « Done » (every confirmed session that is not archived, reviewed or not) is `useConfirmationHistory({ studentId, enabled: showAll })` and the archived drawer `useConfirmationHistory({ studentId, archived: true, enabled: showArchived })`, each grouped by the day trained and with a « Show more » button while `hasNextPage`, "Loading…" while the next page is on its way, and a line when it could not be read. The default view is decided once the inbox has answered — before that, a `?student=` arrival would read the history for nothing. The empty states and the « N archived sessions » row read `useConfirmationTotals(studentId)`: numbers that are not known — still placeholder data of another athlete (`isPlaceholderData`), or a count that failed — claim no empty feed, and keep the view buttons. An inbox that got no answer is `CoachReadProblem` with Try again, never "All caught up". The athletes of the filter are `useStudents` (the value is `students.id`), not the athletes whose confirmations were read.
- **The Programming tab is the whole block as ONE list; there is no week page and no accordion.** [ProgramSheet.jsx](../src/components/coach/ProgramSheet.jsx) renders every session of the block at once (design/parc/screens/Programme.dc.html) — a section per phase, headed by [PhaseDivider](../src/components/coach/PhaseDivider.jsx) (its name and the places its sessions hold, « Sessions 9 to 16 »), and a row per session (its place in condensed digits, its name, « Done / In progress, weekday, n exercises », a ⋯), and ONE block-level « Archived » drawer linking to SessionReview. **A one-phase block and a six-phase block are the same layout** — the old flat/nested split changed the shape of the page under the coach as soon as they added a second week. Session positions are numbered ACROSS phases, and that number is what names an untitled session. Phase-level actions (add session / duplicate / copy to… / reorder phases / delete) live in the divider's ⋯ menu — including entering phase-reorder mode, which has no standing top-level button. That menu and the row's ⋯ — « Duplicate » and the recommended day, two rows of four 44px cells — are drawn by `PortalMenu`, outside the list. The button that opens the session stretches over the row, between its place and its ⋯; the ⋯ holds the two writes, so no tap on a row writes. **Only what is done folds**: a run of two or more sessions done (confirmed or `performed_at`) that opens a phase is one row, « Sessions 1 to 6, All done », opened by a tap (the mock); every session still to train is always shown — the largest block in production is 19 sessions and the average 8, and the plan is what the page exists to show. Selecting and reordering show every row. **Session delete is deliberately NOT on the sheet rows**: a destructive icon on every row of the main authoring surface is a mis-tap waiting to happen on a phone, so it lives in the session editor, where the coach has already committed to that one session. `WeekTimeline` and `WeekView` are **deleted**; `/coach/student/:id/week/:weekId` and `…/week/:weekId/session/:sessionId` survive only as redirects.
- **A new session is born untitled.** `ProgramSheet` creates sessions without a title (the footer, a phase menu, the first session of an empty block), and `useCreateSession` writes `null` for a missing or empty one. An untitled session is named by its position across the block wherever the sheet shows it, so the name follows every reorder, move and archive; a title written as "Session {n}" was true on the day it was added and on no other. The session editor shows `coach.editor.titlePlaceholder` for it, and so does the archived drawer, where an index would be no place in the block.
- **The sheet of an athlete's draft offers no write.** On `program.status === 'draft'` the sheet draws the block and nothing that writes: a row has no ⋯ (no duplicate, no day), there is no « Add a session », no « Add a phase », no « Choose », no « Reorder », a phase name is text and a divider has no ⋯ menu (`PhaseDivider` with `readOnly`; "Copy to…" goes with it, since the dialog can pick the draft itself as the destination), and the drawer has no restore. A row still opens its session, in the editor's read-only state. An empty draft says `coachSheetUx.draftEmpty` and offers no first session. Migration 20 refuses those writes in the database (`draft_read_only`).
- **A row of the sheet opens the REVIEW once its session is trained, the editor before that.** [SessionRow.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionRow.jsx) takes `confirmed` and `trained`; either one sends the row to `/coach/student/:studentId/session/:sessionId/review` with the sheet's URL as `from`. `ProgramSheet` computes `confirmed` from `useProgramConfirmedSessionIds` **or** `sessions.performed_at` — the confirmation's mirror, which arrives with the sheet's own `['program']` query, so a confirmed row does not open the editor while the confirmations are loading — and `trained` from [`useProgramLoggedSessionIds`](../src/hooks/useSession.js) (`['program-logged-session-ids', programId]`): the sessions that hold a logged set and are neither confirmed nor archived. That query leaves confirmed sessions out on purpose (`performed_at IS NULL`): they hold nearly every logged set of a block, and without the filter the read would approach PostgREST's 1000-row cap on a long one. If it is ever truncated the failure is the safe one — the row opens the editor, which locks the logged sets and links to the review itself. A started, unconfirmed row shows a hollow ring where the ✓ will be.
- **The coach's "Last time" reference reads MEASURED performance, and is keyed on the EXERCISE.** [PreviousSessionPanel.jsx](../src/components/coach/PreviousSessionPanel.jsx) shows, per exercise of the session being written, the sets the athlete actually logged — via the same [`useLastPerformance`](../src/hooks/useLastPerformance.js) that powers the athlete's own hint, so the two surfaces cannot disagree. It previously listed the COACH'S OWN past prescription from "the same weekday one week earlier", which was wrong three ways: a prescription is not a performance to beat; it required `week_number === current - 1` so it rendered nothing at all in a one-week block (the normal shape since the queue refactor); and its matching keyed on `day_number`, meaningless once that became an optional hint.
- **`useLastPerformance` is role-aware and MUST be scoped when a coach calls it.** Pass a STUDENTS-table id as `studentRowId` (same convention as `useStudentRecords`/`useStudentProgressStats`): the hook hands it to `fetchDoneSetHistory`, where both the `set_logs` and the `slot_deviations` read filter through the program join, on every page. Omit it and RLS alone scopes to the signed-in student. Skipping the scope in the coach flow is not a no-op — a coach's RLS returns EVERY athlete's logs, so the hint would silently show the most recent number across the whole roster. `studentRowId` is in the hint's query key, and names the history's cache entry, for the same reason. `src/hooks/useLastPerformance.test.jsx` ("whose history") holds all of it: the filter on every page of both reads, no athlete filter on either read without it, and two athletes opened in turn — same session id, same exercise — each getting their own numbers from their own entries.
- **Opening a session, adding an exercise in the editor, or coming back to the tab must not download the athlete's history.** The hint's key is `['last-performance', studentRowId ?? 'self', sessionId, exerciseIds]`, so each of those starts or refetches a query — and that query only picks its exercises out of the list `fetchDoneSetHistory` already holds (see "Stats"). Putting a `supabase.from('set_logs')` back into the hook's `queryFn` brings the scan-per-session back without failing any assertion on the hint itself; the request counts in `src/hooks/useLastPerformance.test.jsx` ("the history is read once") are what notices.
- **An athlete's own "Last time" is held as fresh for four hours, and a confirmation is what renews it.** `hintStaleTime` in [`lib/queryClient.js`](../src/lib/queryClient.js) gives `['last-performance', 'self', …]` a `staleTime` of `QUIET_MS`: deriving a hint is the read of the whole done-set history once that list is older than `HISTORY_STALE_MS`, and the logging page is returned to twenty times a workout. On the athlete's device the hints of the OTHER sessions change when a session is confirmed or un-confirmed, so `refreshAfter` in [`lib/offlineMutations.js`](../src/lib/offlineMutations.js) calls `flagLastTimeHints` for those two writes: every cached hint but the session's own (a hint reads its own session only for the time of its first set, and a confirmation does not change when the session started; the confirmation of a LATER session is what changes this hint, and it flags it) is invalidated with `refetchType: 'none'` — flagged, not read. A confirmation therefore still costs no history read, which `src/hooks/sharedHistory.test.jsx` pins ("the confirmation reads nothing"); a flagged hint is derived when its session is opened, or on the next return to a page that shows it, and the flag is stored with the entry, so across relaunches too. Three consequences. (1) `refreshAfter` is the `onSettled` DEFAULT of the confirm and undo keys: a hook that sets its own `onSettled` on those keys replaces it, and must flag the hints itself or the next session's hint shows the workout before the one just trained for up to four hours. `src/lib/queryClient.cache.test.js` confirms and undoes through the real hooks and fails when neither path flags. (2) What is not covered is training that was never confirmed: sets ticked in session A do not renew the hint of session B, opened in the previous four hours, until A is confirmed or the four hours are over — do not close that by invalidating on every set tap, which is a history read per tap. (3) A coach's hint (the key names the athlete's `students` row instead of `'self'`) keeps the client's two minutes: it moves when the athlete trains, on another device, and nothing on the coach's device says so.
- **Archiving is the coach's cleanup verb, and it lives on the sheet.** It used to be unreachable from Programming entirely: `useArchiveSession`'s only caller was `SessionReview`, which is reachable only for a session the athlete already CONFIRMED or one already archived — so a planned-but-unwanted session that was never trained could only be DELETED. `ProgramSheet` now archives from a selection bar whose selection **spans phases** (archiving a finished mesocycle is one gesture) and restores in place from the block's "N archived" drawer, instead of sending the coach to a full-page review route for a one-tap action. Archiving is the right verb because the DB refuses to delete a session carrying logged sets (`block_session_delete_with_logged_sets`), so for anything actually trained it is the only answer. Any archive write must invalidate `['student-program-details']` and `['student-progress-stats']` too — archiving pulls the session out of the athlete's queue, and the athlete's stats count sessions by `archived_at`. `useArchiveSession` does it in `onSuccess`, `useArchiveSessions` in `onSettled` (a selection that stopped half-way moved rows too). The drawer marks a row "archived" and nothing else, confirmed or trained.
- **Archiving a selection is SEQUENTIAL and says how far it got; copying one is a single call.** `useArchiveSessions` ([useWeek.js](../src/hooks/useWeek.js)) walks a hand-picked list of ids one request at a time and stops at the first that fails — also one that throws instead of answering. The error it throws carries `error.partial`, the number of rows written before it, and the hook tells the coach ONCE, itself (`meta: { skipErrorToast: true }`): `coachDuplicateRpc.archivePartial` / `restorePartial` ("2 of 5 archived. The rest could not be saved — try again.") when some landed, the usual line of `mutationErrorKey` when none did. It invalidates on `onSettled` rather than `onSuccess` — a partial failure still moved rows, so the caches have to refresh either way — and the sheet keeps its selection after a failure, because it leaves selection mode in `onSuccess` only. `useCopySessions` ([useDuplicate.js](../src/hooks/useDuplicate.js)) is not sequential any more: one `copy_sessions` call copies every entry of the list or none, so it has no count to report and no `error.partial`. (The INVARIANTS ban on fanning ids into `.in(...)` is about UNBOUNDED slot-id lists; a coach's hand-made selection goes into the body of one `rpc()` call.)
- **`CopyDialog` picks the destination in full: athlete → BLOCK → week — and keeps it for as long as it is open.** It used to resolve straight to the destination athlete's ACTIVE program (`useActiveProgram`), which dead-ended with "No weeks" for an athlete who had none and made it impossible to copy into a block being prepared for later. Copies APPEND to the end of the destination week — there is deliberately no insert-at-position picker, because the coach can drag sessions into place on the sheet, so a fourth dropdown would buy nothing a reorder doesn't do better. The three selects live in `CopyForm`, the child `Dialog` mounts only while it is open: the destination is forgotten when the dialog closes — Cancel, the cross, Escape, or the parent once the copy is made — and NOT when Copy is pressed. The parent closes the dialog in the `onSuccess` of its `mutate()`, so a copy that is refused leaves it open with the destination still chosen, to be sent again; clearing at the click left an open dialog with empty selects and a dead button. A choice counts only while it is offered: the copy hooks read the lists again after a refusal, and a block that went to the trash or a phase that was deleted is then neither shown nor sent (`programId` / `weekId` are derived from the lists on every render).
