A closed `CopyDialog` calls no hook — the sheet keeps two mounted.
- **Sessions list by POSITION (`sessions.sort_order`), never by weekday.** This inverts the old rule. `compareSessions` ([lib/day.js](../src/lib/day.js)) ranked by weekday with `sort_order` as a tiebreak, and `compareQueued` then put two dated sessions in date order ahead of that — so the recommended day was the only lever that actually reordered the athlete's queue, and a coach could not say "this one comes first" without also claiming when to train it. Since `2026_08_22_session_order_backfill.sql` every week is renumbered 0..n-1 and position IS the order; `day_number` and `scheduled_date` are hints that sort nothing, and `compareQueued` is deleted. The backfill reproduced the on-screen order exactly (measured: 56 of 253 sessions across 28 of 86 weeks would otherwise have moved on deploy; after it, zero). The coach's control is `useReorderSessions` — an explicit MODE, because on touch a drag handle competes with vertical scroll, reachable beside "+ Session" so it exists in FLAT mode too where there is no week ⋯ menu. It is one call of `reorder_sessions`, which parks and places every row in a single statement: `sessions_week_sort_order_unique` is UNIQUE and NOT deferrable, so the two steps cannot be two requests. The list must include ARCHIVED rows — the constraint spans them, and the function refuses a list that is not every session of the phase (`reorder_ids_mismatch`).
- **The session editor is a page of its own, and DELETE is the app's only delete path.** (design/parc/screens/Editeur.dc.html) Its `LargeTitle` is the session — back to the athlete's sheet, the session's name as the title (a tap renames it), the athlete under it — and the athlete's header stands aside while it is open (`CoachHome` draws none on `s/:sessionId`). The recommended date is a field under the title. Duplicate and "copy to…" are not here: the sheet carries both (a row's ⋯, the selection bar). **Delete stayed** — `useDeleteSession` has exactly one caller and archiving does not replace it for a session created by mistake — behind the round « Session options », the rarest action of a screen whose job is writing exercises, offered only while the session can be deleted (not trained, not a draft). It keeps its confirmation (a Parc `ConfirmSheet`) and its navigate-back-on-success.
- **The session editor locks what the athlete has trained, and how much depends on whether they confirmed.** [SessionEditor.jsx](../src/components/coach/SessionEditor.jsx) derives two states. **Confirmed** (`useSessionConfirmation` returns a row, or `session.performed_at` is set): the whole editor is read-only — the title is text, the date and every field are disabled, and add, remove, reorder, unlink and delete are not rendered; `ExerciseSlotRow` gets `readOnly`. **Logged sets, not confirmed** (a session in progress, or one stopped half-way): only the logged sets are locked. [ExerciseSlotRow.jsx](../src/components/coach/ExerciseSlotRow.jsx) disables and marks each logged row, drops its remove button, disables "Remove exercise" while any row of the slot is logged (student-added extras included, since the delete would cascade to them) and replaces the compact one-row view with the per-set table, where pending sets stay editable and "+ Add set" still works. This second state is deliberately not read-only as a whole: re-planning what is left of a session is what the coach opens it for, and it is what the database allows. In both states a banner says which one it is and links to the review, and "Delete this session" is not rendered — the database refuses a session with logged sets. A notes field is `readOnly`, not `disabled`, so it can still be read in full and copied.
- **A coach's target weight is read, not cast, and text the slot row cannot read is never written.** The weight of [ExerciseSlotRow.jsx](../src/components/coach/ExerciseSlotRow.jsx) — the compact row's and each per-set row's — is a `DecimalInput` read on blur by `parseDecimalInput` under `TARGET_WEIGHT_RULES` (9999.99 at most: `exercise_slots.weight_kg` and `set_logs.target_weight_kg` are `numeric(6,2)`), so "62,5" and "62.5" both write 62.5. Text that is not a number of that range writes nothing, and the field shows the stored value again; that restore is set in the row's local state, because the resync effects only run when the props change. In the compact row the weight is one key of the blur's `onUpdate`: an unreadable weight is left out and the sets, reps, seconds and rest of the same blur still go. **An empty field is still `null` on purpose** — no load, which the athlete sees as "BW": it is how a coach prescribes bodyweight, so it is never treated as an error. A number field read "62,5" as 625 in an English Chromium and as `""` in WebKit, and that `""` was written as `null`: the athlete saw "BW" while the coach still saw "62,5". The library's `volume_weight` (0.1 to 99.99, `numeric(4,2)`) and a goal's `target_weight_kg` (0 to 9999.99) are read the same way on submit, and nothing is sent while they are refused. Tests: `coach/ExerciseSlotRow.test.jsx` ("a target weight typed with a comma or a point"), `coach/ExerciseLibrary.test.jsx` ("the volume weight"), `coach/StudentGoalsSection.test.jsx`.
- **Session editing renders inside the athlete shell.** `SessionEditor` is a child route at `/coach/students/:studentId/s/:sessionId`: `CoachHome` stays mounted while editing, draws no header over it, and hands it the athlete through the outlet. It therefore **must not apply page padding of its own** (`CoachHome` already applies `px-4 pt-3 pb-6 md:px-8`) and it resolves its `weekId` from `session.week_id` rather than the URL.
- **The single-student view is ONE page with collapsible sections — no tabs.** [StudentOverview.jsx](../src/components/coach/StudentOverview.jsx) stacks Programming (open by default), Goals and Stats as collapsible blocks. **There is no identity block above them — on an athlete route the page header *is* the athlete.** [CoachHome.jsx](../src/components/coach/CoachHome.jsx) swaps its own `LargeTitle` when a student resolves: the back link « All athletes » takes the place of the back button, and the name takes the `h1` where « Athletes » sits. The old stack — roster title, then back link, then an avatar/name row, then a coaching-since date, then the pills — put the biggest type on the page the coach had just *left* and pushed the actual subject three rows down at half the size. `StudentHeader.jsx` is **deleted**; don't reintroduce a second identity row. Both headers are the same Parc `LargeTitle`, so moving roster ↔ athlete changes the words and not the box; a long name wraps (`break-words`) rather than being cut. Tabs were removed because every tab was a separate destination that remounted and scroll-reset — losing the coach's place mid-edit. Two rules matter: a collapsed section is **unmounted, not hidden** (Stats runs a heavy aggregate and Goals a profile-id resolve — a closed section must cost nothing), and open/closed persists in `localStorage` under `sl_coach_student_sections`. The athlete header deliberately carries **no week strip**: that data comes from the active-program summary query and would contradict the sheet below whenever a non-active block is selected. Every former tab URL (`programming`, `progress`, `profile`, `goals`, `stats`, `messaging`) redirects to the page.
- **The athlete page carries two actions under its header: Message and Sessions.** `AthleteActions` in [StudentOverview.jsx](../src/components/coach/StudentOverview.jsx) links to `/coach/messages/:profileId` — the thread is keyed on `students.profile_id`, not on the students-row id — and to `/coach/sessions?student=<students.id>`. They are the first row of the page body and not part of `CoachHome`'s header, for two reasons: that header box is shared with the roster and must not change shape, and it stays mounted over the session editor, where a row of actions above the work is what the editor's own header was trimmed to avoid. The student comes from the outlet context.
- **The coach's place must survive a trip into the session editor.** Two separate mechanisms, both required: `AppShell`'s `useScrollRestoration` remembers each route's scroll offset and re-applies it across a few animation frames (the target route is `React.lazy`, so on the first frame the container is still the Suspense fallback and has no height to scroll); that is now the WHOLE mechanism for the program sheet, since the sheet no longer collapses anything and has no open-week state to remember (`sl_coach_open_week` is gone). A first visit to a route still starts at the top — only a revisit restores. Don't reintroduce a blanket `scrollTo(0)` on pathname change.
- **Coach roster owns the student lookup.** [CoachHome.jsx](../src/components/coach/CoachHome.jsx) resolves the `:studentId` from `useStudents()` and exposes the resolved student to the sub-tabs via `<Outlet context={{ student }} />`. Each section reads it with `useOutletContext()` — including `StudentGoalsSection`/`StudentStatsSection` nested inside `StudentProgressSection`, since outlet context reaches any descendant. `/coach/students/:id` redirects to `…/programming` (the index sub-route). The athlete route's back link is **« All athletes »**, back to the roster. The legacy `/coach/student/:id/goals` URL redirects to `…/students/:id/goals`.

## Training is a QUEUE; the calendar is a consequence

- **A block is an ordered list of sessions, not a schedule.** The next session is the first unconfirmed, non-archived one in program order (weeks by `week_number`, sessions by `compareSessions`) — computed by [`buildQueue`](../src/lib/sessionQueue.js), which also returns `position`/`total` ("Session 7 of 24") — `position` is the place of the Next card (`upcoming[0]`) among the non-archived sessions, not `completed + 1`: with sessions 1, 2, 3 and 5 confirmed it is 4; `total` once nothing is left, 0 for an empty block — `lastPerformedAt`, `daysSinceLast` and `doneLast7`. **A date never jumps the queue**: a session the coach dated today but placed in a later week does NOT become "next" while earlier work is open. That is the whole point — it is what stops a missed session from cascading into a re-dating exercise. `flattenSessions` sorts two DATED sessions chronologically before falling back to weekday order, because `compareSessions` ranks by weekday alone and would otherwise put the 13th (a Monday) ahead of the 10th (a Friday).
- **`sessions.day_number` is a RECOMMENDED weekday (1 = Mon … 7 = Sun) and is NULLABLE** (2026_08_21). It is advice, never a commitment: order comes from `sort_order`, and nothing is "missed" for being trained on another day. New sessions are created with `day_number: null` — auto-assigning the next free weekday made every session look due on a date nobody chose, and capped a week at 7 because day 8 fell outside every strip. The student draft builder used to write this column as an ORDINAL (a 5th session silently became "Friday"); it now writes null. **A NULL only survives if the write path passes it through**: `save_draft_tree` predates the nullable column and coalesced to `1`, so every student-authored session came back recommending Monday even though the authoring UI has no weekday control at all — fixed in `2026_08_22_draft_day_number_optional.sql`. Since migration `2026_10_16_21_rpe_half_steps_day_check.sql` (review B12) the database holds it: `sessions_day_number_weekday` refuses anything but NULL or 1 to 7 (`23514`). That migration cleared every value outside 1 to 7 and every weekday of a block an athlete wrote (`programs.created_by` set — drafts and approved blocks, trained sessions included), whose builder has no weekday control: those were positions or the coalesced Monday. `save_draft_tree` writes a number outside 1 to 7 as NULL rather than refuse the whole tree — what a phone that cached its draft before that migration still sends. The coach's editor writes 1 to 7 or NULL (`nextFreeDayNumber` of `lib/day.js` stops at 7), and the four copy functions copy the value as it is.
- **There is no "missed" state anywhere.** `statusOf`, `deriveWeekStats` and `preferSession` are deleted from `lib/day.js`. Strip placement is [`buildDayStrip`](../src/lib/sessionQueue.js), **shared by the student's Home and the coach's roster (`weekFromStrip`)** so the two can never tell different stories about the same week. Its states, in the order they win a contested day: `performed` (trained on that date — the record, which nothing may overwrite) > `planned` (a coach-set recommended date for open work) > `archived` (pulled by the coach; kept visible, not blanked to rest), plus `suggested` for an undated queue head projected onto its recommended weekday. Weekday projection is gated on the CURRENT week (`weekdayFallback`) — projecting a recommendation onto a navigated week would invent a plan. Coach-side triage replaces "missed Wed" with **"quiet Nd"** (`STALE_AFTER_DAYS = 10` in [coachRoster.js](../src/lib/coachRoster.js)); an athlete who has never trained shows no chip, because "quiet forever" is not a finding.
- **`scheduled_date` is still a bare `YYYY-MM-DD`** — always parse with `parseISODate` (local time), never `Date.parse`/`toISOString`, which shift across timezones. Day-cell `aria-label`s include the day-of-month (`Monday 13 — Push Day`) because the button's label masks its inner text from AT; the range header is `aria-live="polite"` so week navigation is announced.
- **The student has ONE training surface, and it is the list.** [StudentTraining.jsx](../src/components/student/StudentTraining.jsx) replaced a Home page and a Sessions page that both listed sessions and both offered "start the next one" — two doors onto one question. `/student/sessions` redirects to `/student`, `StudentHome.jsx`/`StudentSessions.jsx` are deleted, and the student tab bar is **3 tabs** (Training, Stats, Messages), not 4. In the Parc design (`design/parc/screens/Entrainement.dc.html`) the page states the athlete's place ONCE, under its title (« Force tirage, séance 7 sur 24 »), with the week the athlete is in as seven dots (`WeekDots`, from `buildDayStrip` through `weekOf` of [trainingModel.js](../src/components/student/trainingModel.js): done, a session planned today, the day of the next one — no "missed") and one line beside them (the sessions done this week, else when the last one was, else that there never was one). **There is no Mon–Sun grid with week arrows**: that IS the calendar week, the framing the block was rewritten to stop imposing — the month calendar in Stats carries "when did I train" as a retrospective. **The next session is a panel, open**, with when it is planned, its place, its exercises and their prescriptions (`lib/prescriptionText`), and « Commencer la séance »; the sessions after it follow under « Ensuite » in program order, three of them and then « Voir les n autres séances ». **Finished sessions order by real training date, newest first** — they used to sort by recommended weekday, so a session trained on the 18th sat above one trained on the 21st. Undated legacy rows sink below dated ones rather than jumping to the top. From 1024px the next session and the lists stand side by side.
- **Everything already trained sits in closed rows under « Historique »** — one for the active block's own finished sessions (« 6 séances faites, Dans ce bloc, la dernière hier »), one per past block (« Base tirage, Bloc terminé, 18 séances ») — leaving only the next session and the work still ahead in plain sight. A month-long block reaches ~20 finished sessions and a year of coaching hundreds, every one of them standing between the athlete and the single question the page answers. `HistoryRow` takes a **`renderBody` function, not children**, so a closed row builds nothing and the cost stays one row however long the history gets. Open state is deliberately NOT persisted. The archived-sessions toggle is a quiet text button under the lists rather than a control inside a row the athlete would have to open first. A finished session of a past block is read-only (`SessionView` renders a past block read-only) and its row stops LOGGING, never LOOKING: it leads to the session when it is confirmed, and also when it was trained and never confirmed — `holdsLoggedSet`, from `isLoggedSet` (`lib/loggedSets`) over the session's `set_logs` (L32); a past-block session never trained has nothing to show and its row leads nowhere.
- **The training list is built from two reads, both of them kept on the device, and from nothing else.** [`useStudentProgramDetails`](../src/hooks/useStudentProgramDetails.js) reads `studentProgramDetailsKey(userId)` — `['student-program-details', userId, 'active']`, the one variant of that root `shouldPersistQuery` stores — and `useMyConfirmedSessionIds` reads `['my-confirmed-session-ids', userId]`. A list that reads any other key works on a network and is empty at the next launch without one. The entry is `{ weeks, pastPrograms }`: the tree of the block being trained, and the other blocks as `{ id, name, sort_order, session_count, archived_count }` — two requests in one `queryFn`, failing together, so a block the coach switched off never shows in both places. Before them the `queryFn` reads the athlete's `students` row with `.maybeSingle()`: an account its owner has not linked to a coach yet has none, and those zero rows are the answer `{ weeks: [], pastPrograms: [] }` — stored like any other — not the `PGRST116` error `.single()` makes of them, which the page would show as "couldn't be loaded" over a "Try again" that can never succeed. The header request selects nothing of a session but `archived_at`, and the tree of the block being trained (`ACTIVE_TREE`) selects the targets of each set and nothing the athlete logged; a column added to either grows what is re-serialised to IndexedDB on every change of the cache. `select` reads a bare array (the entry of a build that cached the list of weeks) as a block with no past blocks known. `src/hooks/useStudentProgramDetails.test.jsx` holds the key against `shouldPersistQuery`, and `src/components/student/StudentTraining.offline.test.jsx` saves the cache the way the app does, restores it into a client without a network and renders the page on it.
- **The sessions of a past block are a read of their own, made when its drawer opens, and never stored.** `useStudentPastProgram(userId, programId)` — key `studentPastProgramKey`, `['student-program-details', userId, 'past', programId]` — is mounted by `PastBlockSessions`, the body of the drawer, so a closed drawer costs no request (the same `renderBody` rule as above). The row of a closed drawer is made of the header alone: its name, and `session_count` minus `archived_count` unless the archived toggle is on; the toggle's own count adds the `archived_count` of every past block to the archived sessions of the active one. Both keys sit under the root every writer of a program invalidates (`['student-program-details']`). Its tree (`PAST_TREE`, built by the same `programTree(setColumns)` as the active one) selects `LOGGED_SET_COLUMNS` on `set_logs` besides the targets: it is how a card knows `hasLoggedSets`. Since the read is not stored, the columns cost nothing on the device; they never move to `ACTIVE_TREE`.
- **"No program assigned yet" is said by a read that answered, and by nothing else.** `readState` in [`lib/readState.js`](../src/lib/readState.js) names what the reads of a part of the page amount to — `ready` (each holds data), `loading` (one is on its way, the cache is still being read back from IndexedDB — `useIsRestoring` — or the account is not known), `offline` (one waits for the network, `isPaused`, or `navigator.onLine` is false), `failed` (`isError` on a network, or a read that answered with nothing — `isSuccess` with `data == null`, a `maybeSingle` read of a row that is not there: waiting will not bring it, so it is not `loading`) — and the page renders the list only on `ready`. Data always wins: a refresh that failed leaves `isError` next to the last answer, and the answer is shown. The list needs BOTH reads: the block without the confirmations puts the athlete back on its first session, so a missing `my-confirmed-session-ids` is a missing list, not an empty set. `offline` and `failed` render [`Unavailable`](../src/components/student/Unavailable.jsx) — what happened, and a "Try again" that calls `refetch({ cancelRefetch: false })` on both reads — under the page's greeting; an open past-block drawer does the same for its own read. Testing `isLoading` alone is the trap: a read that is paused, errored or not yet restored is not loading and has no data, and the empty state of the page is a claim about the athlete's program. The opposite trap is a `queryFn` that throws on an answer: "no row" from the server (an athlete without a coach link, a block put in the trash) is returned as an empty value, never thrown, or the page reports a failure that no retry can clear.
- **The session page shows a session only when it holds one, and offers no confirmation of a session it does not hold.** [`SessionView.jsx`](../src/components/student/SessionView.jsx) is made of two reads: `useSession`, and `useSetLogs` once the session is known to have exercises (a session without an exercise waits for no sets). `readState` over those — the same function as the training list, with `useOnlineStatus()` and `useIsRestoring()` — decides what is rendered: the spinner on `loading`, the page on `ready`, and on `offline` or `failed` the top bar (the session's title when the row is there, `sessionUnavailable.title` otherwise) over `Unavailable` with the strings of the `sessionUnavailable` namespace and a "Try again" that calls `refetch({ cancelRefetch: false })` on the reads. Nothing else of the page is rendered then: no progress line, no exercise, no "Confirm session". Testing `isLoading` alone is the trap, as on the list: a read that waits for the network or failed is not loading and has no data, and rendered as an empty session it was a page titled "Session" with a live "Confirm session" (live item N1). The session row without its sets is not a session either: exercises with no set under them, and a confirmation whose training date `performedOnFromLogs` cannot read. Data wins here too — the workout the device holds is shown offline and after a refresh that failed. When the network returns and the page is not `ready`, an effect asks again by itself, once, on the offline → online step. What is NOT waited for: the confirmation, the notes, the swaps, the videos and "Last time". A session that was read ahead and never opened has none of them on the device, and it has to be trainable and confirmable in the gym; an unknown confirmation is shown as "not confirmed", and the confirm is insert-or-nothing.
- **When the network returns and the list has nothing to show, it asks again by itself — once.** An effect in `StudentTraining` compares `useOnlineStatus()` with its previous value and calls the same `retry` on the offline → online step when the list is not `ready`. It is a step, not a state: a read that keeps failing while the device is online stops at its message and its button. A client whose `onlineManager` follows `navigator.onLine` resumes a paused read on its own, and the effect's `refetch` joins it; a client that believed it was online at launch has an errored read that nothing else picks up.
- **The next session is read ahead while the list is on screen.** `readAheadSession` hands `sessionQueryOptions(nextId)` (exported by [`useSession.js`](../src/hooks/useSession.js)) to `queryClient.fetchQuery`, then `setLogsQueryOptions(nextId, session.exercise_slots)` (exported by [`useSetLogs.js`](../src/hooks/useSetLogs.js)) to `prefetchQuery` — the two reads `SessionView` holds its spinner for, under the keys and in the shapes its hooks mount, both roots stored on the device. Without it a session that was never opened on the device had nothing to show in the gym. Four rules. (1) The sets are asked for with the slots of the session ROW just cached, not the list's: `useSetLogs` builds its key from the row, and the list may be one coach edit behind it. (2) The effect runs for the next session only, when the list is `ready` — confirmations included, or the first session of the block would be read — and again each time the list is read again (`dataUpdatedAt`), when the network returns and when the queue of writes empties; `fetchQuery` answers from the cache while the copy is fresh, so this is at most one read of the session per stale time. (3) It does not run offline, nor while a mutation is pending or paused (`useIsMutating`): the answer of a read sent before a queued set reached the database would put the ticked sets back as they were. (4) `sessionQueryOptions` and `setLogsQueryOptions` carry no `staleTime`, `gcTime` or `refetchOn…`: those are set per root in `lib/queryClient.js`, and it is that file's `gcTime` which keeps an entry nobody observes — a session read ahead and not opened yet — in memory and therefore on the device. The tests of the page build clients that keep everything for ever (`gcTime: Infinity`) and cannot see this; the last one of `StudentTraining.offline.test.jsx`, "on the client of the app", renders the page on the app's own `queryClient` and asks both entries for their `gcTime` after the read-ahead: at least `PERSIST_MAX_AGE_MS`. What is NOT read ahead: the session's notes, swaps, videos, confirmation and "Last time" hints — the page renders without them, and shows them once it has been opened on a network.
- **A week survives only as an optional PHASE, and a phase has a NAME, never a number.** `weeks.week_number` is still the ordinal the DB sorts on and `adopt_swap`/`adopt_skip` predicate on, but it is never shown: a number is what made a week feel like a fixed-length container, which is the idea the queue refactor removed. An unnamed phase still **draws its divider** — half the phases in production are unnamed, and hiding those rules would silently merge them into the phase above — it simply carries no heading, and its "name this phase" invitation drops out of `sl-label` so a stack of them doesn't shout over the sessions. An empty block's CTA adds a SESSION and creates phase 1 implicitly.

- **Dragging a session across a divider MOVES it between phases, and that is ONE write.** [`planSessionDrag`](../src/lib/sessionMove.js) decides reorder-vs-move purely and returns the FULL ordered id list of every week touched, archived rows appended — `UNIQUE (week_id, sort_order)` spans the whole week, so a payload that omits archived rows renumbers straight into a collision. A cross-phase drag then goes through the `move_session` RPC (`2026_08_22_move_session.sql`, **SECURITY INVOKER** so it rides the coach's existing `Coaches manage sessions` policy rather than adding trust surface), which re-homes the row and renumbers both weeks in one statement, parking every involved id at `100000 + n` first. Split across two round trips, a failure between them leaves a session holding a position that collides in its new week. Reordering *within* a phase stays on `useReorderSessions`.
- **`move_session` still parks at `100000 + n`; `reorder_sessions` does not.** A unique key that is not deferrable is checked row by row, inside one `UPDATE` too: parked at a constant, a session can meet one that an interrupted reorder left at that number, and `move_session` then fails with `sessions_week_sort_order_unique` on such a phase — or goes through, as the ids fall. `reorder_sessions` and `reorder_weeks` park above every number the parent holds (`GREATEST(max, n) + place`) and cannot. A phase left parked is put right by one call of `reorder_sessions` with its sessions in the order they are shown (`docs/runbook/migrations/11-activate-reorder-rpcs.md`, "Sessions or phases left parked").
- **The order of a block is written one drag at a time, and only the last drag of a run reads the server again.** `useReorderSessions`, `useMoveSession` and `useReorderWeeks` ([hooks/useWeek.js](../src/hooks/useWeek.js)) each take the block's id and run under ONE TanStack mutation scope per block (`block-order-<program id>`, from `mutationScope` in [lib/mutationScope.js](../src/lib/mutationScope.js)); `ProgramSheet` hands `program.id` to all three, because its single `handleDragEnd` can end in any of them. Mutations of one scope are sent one after the other, in the order they were made — but each still runs its `onMutate` at once, so the order of a second drag is on screen while the first is on its way, and every drag sends the FULL list of ids as the sheet shows it. All three draw their order in `onMutate`, the move included: a session still shown in the phase it left would have the next drag send lists from before the move — a reorder the database refuses (`reorder_ids_mismatch`), or a second move renumbered from lists `move_session` does not check. Four things follow. (1) **A hook never changes scope while one of its writes waits: `ProgramSheet` is mounted once per block and `ProgramSwitcher` once per athlete** — `key={selectedProgram.id}` and `key={studentId}` in [StudentProgrammingSection.jsx](../src/components/coach/StudentProgrammingSection.jsx). `scope` is an option of `useMutation`, and TanStack Query hands the options of a hook that renders again to the mutation that hook still has pending (`MutationObserver.setOptions`): rendered with another id, the hook re-points its waiting write at a queue it was never put in, where it is not given its turn — the write is not sent until the next focus or reconnection, and not at all after a reload. A hook that unmounts leaves its writes in the cache as they were made, and they go out in their turn. So the scope is per block, not per phase; a component that calls one of these hooks is keyed on the id it hands them; a hook called without an id has no scope and sends at once; and a callback that has to outlive the sheet goes in the hook's options, never in `mutate(vars, { … })`, whose callbacks are dropped with the component. (2) `onSettled` asks `laterWriteQueued(qc, scope)` before it invalidates. A refetch started by the first of two drags answers with the order BEFORE the second, puts that back on screen — the row just dropped jumps back — and a third drag made at that moment sends a list that undoes the second. (3) `onError` asks the same before it puts its snapshot back (`restoreBlockOrder`): a snapshot is the WHOLE block from before its drag, so it would take the order of a later drag off the screen. The refetch of the last drag settles what a failed one left there. (4) The three hooks settle through one `refreshBlockOrder` (`['program']`, `['week']`, `['student-program-details']`, the coach dashboard), so whichever is last makes up for the refetches the others left out: a mutation that joins the scope must settle through it too. `laterWriteQueued` counts the mutation that asks as pending, which is what it is inside `onError` and `onSettled` — called from `onSuccess` of a `mutate()` call, after the mutation is done, the same test is wrong by one.

## The date a session was actually trained

- **`session_confirmations.performed_on` (date) is the truth; `sessions.performed_at` (timestamptz) is its mirror** (2026_08_21). `confirmed_at` is the server clock at INSERT, which for an offline student is the REPLAY moment, not the training one — so it is a fallback, never the primary. The client computes `performed_on` at CONFIRM time via [`performedOnFromLogs`](../src/lib/day.js) from the session's `set_logs.logged_at` (minted when each set was ticked, so it survives an offline queue intact) and it rides in the serialized mutation variables. It takes the EARLIEST logged set: a session running past midnight belongs to the day it began, matching the migration's `MIN` backfill. Resolved in LOCAL time via `isoDate`.
- **The mirror is maintained by its own trigger, `sync_session_performed_at`** — deliberately NOT folded into `notify_coach_on_session_confirm`, which is the notification/Web-Push path (AFTER INSERT only, with an EXCEPTION-wrapped pg_net call). The mirror needs the DELETE branch (undoing a confirmation un-performs the session, so `performed_at` goes back to NULL) and an UPDATE branch. The app's own confirm does not reach the UPDATE branch — it is insert-or-nothing, see "Offline-safe student writes" — but a `performed_on` corrected in SQL does, and so does an installed client that still sends the confirm as an updating upsert. SECURITY DEFINER, because students have no UPDATE grant on `sessions`. `performed_on` is client-supplied, so the trigger clamps a value more than a day in the FUTURE back to `confirmed_at`; past dates are never clamped — confirming days after training is the entire point.
- **`sessions.performed_at` holds a DATE stored as midnight UTC, and is read as a date — never through `new Date()`.** What `sync_session_performed_at` writes is `performed_on::timestamptz`, `'2026-09-28T00:00:00+00:00'` for a session trained on Monday 28 September, and the optimistic write of `useSessionConfirmation` (`performedAtOf`) writes the same string. Read as an instant, that midnight is Sunday evening anywhere west of Greenwich: the session sat on Sunday of the strip, in the week before on the Stats page and its calendar, and "trained today" read "yesterday". Europe and CI (UTC) never see it. Every reader takes the date part, at local midnight: `performedDate` in [`lib/day.js`](../src/lib/day.js) (and through it `buildDayStrip`, the done list of `StudentTraining`, the coach roster), `buildQueue` (`lastPerformedAt` is that Date, and `daysSince` is given the Date, never a `'YYYY-MM-DD'` string, which `new Date()` reads as UTC), `trainedOn` in [`useStudentProgressStats`](../src/hooks/useStudentProgressStats.js) (`calendarDay`), and `useStudentHistoricalSessions`. `confirmed_at` and `logged_at` are real instants and are read in the device's zone, where the athlete trained. Two cases are read by their UTC date part and accepted: a `performed_at` the trigger set from `confirmed_at` (a `performed_on` more than a day in the future, clamped), which is a day early for an evening west of Greenwich; and a confirmation whose `performed_on` is itself wrong in the future, which `trainedOn` takes as it is where the mirror was clamped — the app always sends `performedOnFromLogs`. The zone tests run through `inTimeZone(zone, fn)` of `src/test/inTimeZone.js` (sets `process.env.TZ`, checks that `Intl` follows it, keeps it across the awaits of a hook test) in Los Angeles, Kiritimati and Paris; a fixture of `performed_at` is written as the database writes it, `'<day>T00:00:00+00:00'`.
- **Rows written before the column existed degrade quietly, never by inventing a date.** `buildQueue` ignores a confirmed session with no `performed_at` when computing staleness; `buildDayStrip` falls back to the day it was PLANNED for; `trainedOn` in [`useStudentProgressStats`](../src/hooks/useStudentProgressStats.js) takes the confirmation's `performed_on` → `performed_at`, read as a date → the earliest `logged_at` of the session's done sets (`performedOnFromLogs`, asked only with sets that carry a time — it answers "today" for none) → the confirmation's `confirmed_at` → `scheduled_date` for a LEGACY row only, archived AND reviewed. An open session with none of these is counted in `backlogSessions` rather than parked on a made-up day; an archived one with none of these and no done set was never trained, and is in no figure (next section).

## Stats

- **One definition of a performed session, behind every figure of the Stats page.** `isPerformed` in [`useStudentProgressStats`](../src/hooks/useStudentProgressStats.js): confirmed, or archived with a training date (`trainedOn`) or at least one done set. Archiving is no proof of training — it is also how a coach clears a session nobody will train, and `duplicate_week` / `duplicate_program` can hand over archived sessions — so an archived session that was never trained is in NO figure: not `totalSessions` nor `totalSessionsConfirmed`, not `totalSets`, no week bucket, no progression point, not the calendar, as `buildQueue` leaves it out of the athlete's list. One that was trained keeps every figure it earned, and goes on the calendar on its training date only, never on its plan date. A session trained and NOT confirmed is dated by its done sets and counts everywhere a date counts — its week (sets, planned and performed volume), its progression point, its day on the calendar — but it is not performed: not in `totalSessionsConfirmed`, not in a week's `sessions_confirmed`, `completed: false` on the calendar. The table test "one state per row, every figure" in `src/hooks/useStudentProgressStats.test.jsx` holds each state of a set and of a session against every figure; a new state or a new figure joins it.
- **Time-based stats bucket by the REAL calendar week the student trained in** (2026_08_21), not by the ordinal training week a session was authored under — a block spread over ten days used to land in a bucket corresponding to no actual seven-day span. `weeklyVolume[]` rows are `{ bucket_start, bucket_end, … }` (Mon–Sun of the day `trainedOn` gives, a `'YYYY-MM-DD'` that is the same in every zone — the date columns read as dates, the instants in the device's zone: previous section) rendered as date ranges by `formatBucketRange`; `weeksActive` counts calendar weeks trained; `exerciseProgress.byExercise[]` is **one point per SESSION** on a date axis (which retired the `program_id:week_id` composite key that existed only to stop week 1 of two blocks colliding). **A session's PLANNED volume travels with it into the bucket where it was performed**, so planned and performed are always compared over the same days; work not yet done has no date and lands in `backlogSessions` instead of dragging down a week it was never attempted in. `sessionCalendar` plots trained sessions on their REAL date and open ones on the recommended date, so a session trained without any coach-set date finally appears at all; an archived session is on it only where it was trained, and `completed` is `isPerformed`.
- **The weekly panel draws consecutive weeks, a week nothing was trained in as a row of zeros.** `weeklyVolume` holds a bucket only for a week a session was trained in; [`WeeklyVolumePanel`](../src/components/student/WeeklyVolumePanel.jsx) takes it whole and picks its rows with `weeklyWindow(weeks, { now, current })`: `WEEKS_SHOWN` (6, as the Parc mock draws them) consecutive Mon–Sun weeks, stepped by calendar day with `addDays` (a change of the clocks moves nothing), a missing week built in the hook's bucket shape with every figure at 0, never starting before the oldest week trained. The window ends at the week of today when `current` is true — `scopeReachesToday(scope, programs)` of [`lib/statsPrefs.js`](../src/lib/statsPrefs.js): `'all'`, `'active'` and the active block — so a layoff shows as empty weeks; it ends at the newest week trained for a past block, whose six weeks would otherwise all be empty weeks of today. The bars are scaled to the weeks drawn (`computeMaxWeeklyTotal`). A week's sets (`setsAdherence`, "3/3 · 100%") are drawn whenever it had prescribed sets, volume or not — a week of planks and holds has no volume, since `computeSessionVolume` skips a slot held for a time; "No training logged yet." (`statsPanels.noTraining`) is said only when `weeklyWindow` gives no week at all, and one line (`statsPanels.adherenceNote`) says that a week's sets are those of the sessions trained in it. Do not go back to `weeklyVolume.slice(-4)` in a page: the four rows were the last four weeks TRAINED, months apart after a layoff, and a week without volume replaced every row with "No volume assigned yet.".
- **The progression chart is a time axis, with a few dates under it.** In [`ExerciseProgressChart`](../src/components/student/ExerciseProgressChart.jsx), `pointPositions(points, { left, right })` places a point by its training day, counted from the digits of its `date` (`Date.UTC`, so the night the clocks change does not stretch a day); one point, or the points of one day, are centred; a point with no `date` makes the axis fall back to spacing by order. The first and the last point sit 10 units inside the plot (`INSET`) so their bar stays in it, and a bar is at most 18 wide and 0.6 of the gap to the nearest training day. The dates under the axis are `thinTicks(ticks, { max: 6 })`: for k from 6 down to 2, the point nearest each of k even marks along the axis, and the first k whose labels — `tickWidth` (a monospace estimate), centred on the point and held inside the chart by `tickCentre` — do not overlap; the first and the last point are always labelled, a single spot once. The program prefix (`BLO·7/8`) is part of a label's width. One label per point drew some 24 on top of each other after three months.
- **Stats are performance-aware: charts show PERFORMED work, not just the prescription** (2026_07_13). [`useStudentProgressStats`](../src/hooks/useStudentProgressStats.js) builds a `perfBySlot` index from the flat `set_logs` read — DONE rows only (`done = true`), filtered through the program join and read to its end with `fetchAllRows`. `performedReps(l, slot)` = a done set's `actual_reps ?? target_reps` (`??`, so a real 0 is respected; skipped/undone contribute 0), and 0 on a slot held for a time — `exercise_slots.duration_seconds` set, the database allows reps or a duration and never both — whatever was typed in its actual reps or in a set added to it (its done sets still count as sets done); `performedWeight` = `actual_weight_kg ?? target_weight_kg` with bodyweight (null/0) → 1kg. `weeklyVolume[].pull`/`push` are now PERFORMED (prescribed kept as `*_planned`), plus `sets_done`/`sets_prescribed` for adherence and `extra_sets_done` beside them; `exerciseProgress.byExercise[].tonnage` is PERFORMED with `plannedTonnage` as the reference. The dashboard renders performed volume over a faint planned ghost track + per-week adherence %, and the exercise chart draws performed tonnage (solid) with a dashed planned line when they diverge. This is what makes the off-plan actuals + deviations features finally visible in the student's motivation loop. Coach `StudentStatsSection` reuses the same hook + chart, so it inherits this for free.
- **The lifetime volume of the Profile page is the tonnage of the Stats page, over every done set of every block that is not in the trash.** [`useStudentLifetimeStats`](../src/hooks/useStudentLifetimeStats.js) (`setTonnage`): reps = `actual_reps ?? target_reps`, 0 on a slot held for a time (`exercise_slots.duration_seconds`), times load = `actual_weight_kg ?? target_weight_kg`, 1 kg for no load or a load of 0. It reads no `set_logs.weight_kg` and joins no exercise: `volume_weight` is a multiplier, which the old total multiplied by the target reps (5 × 5 at 100 kg made 25), and an inner join to the library dropped the sets of an exercise the athlete can no longer read. The two counts and the volume read filter `programs.deleted_at IS NULL` through their embed, as the Stats page, the training list and the history do. One difference with the Stats page is kept: on a SWAPPED slot the Stats page takes the actual load alone, which needs the swaps; here a swapped set with no actual load counts its target. "Sessions" counts confirmations and "Sets done" every done set, added ones included.
- **The flat `set_logs` list in `useStudentProgressStats` holds DONE sets and nothing else.** Every figure derived from it — `totalSetsDone`, `avgRpe`, `sets_done`, performed volume and tonnage — counts done sets only, so the pending, failed and skipped rows are not downloaded (they were most of the rows and none of the result). Anything that needs the prescribed sets reads them from the program tree (`exercise_slots.sets`, the embedded `set_logs` targets), which is complete. A new figure about failed or skipped sets needs its own read; do not drop the `done` filter to get it — that puts the athlete's whole prescription back into a read that is paged.
- **"Sets done" counts the PRESCRIBED sets done; the sets the athlete added are counted apart.** `totalSetsDone` goes by the rule of a week's `sets_done` — done and not `is_student_added`, out of the prescribed `totalSets` — and `totalExtraSetsDone` (`extra_sets_done` per week) holds the added ones. Both are summed over the sessions of the tree, so a row the scope did not ask for adds nothing. Added sets are real work: they stay in the volume, the tonnage and `avgRpe`. Counted with the prescribed ones, the card read "5 done, of 3 prescribed" beside a week that said 3/3. The "Sets done" card of [`StudentDashboard`](../src/components/student/StudentDashboard.jsx) and of [`StudentStatsSection`](../src/components/coach/StudentStatsSection.jsx) adds "· +N extra" (`statsCompletionRules.extraSets`) after "of N prescribed" when there are any, and nothing otherwise.
- **The `'all'` scope leaves the athlete's drafts out.** A draft (`programs.status = 'draft'`) is no block they train, and its sessions and sets lowered the completion for work nobody had asked of them yet. The tree is read with `.neq('status', 'draft')` in that scope, `applyProgramScope` puts the same filter on the three flat lists (`status` is in their `programs!inner(…)` embed), and the hook skips a program row that says it is a draft all the same. `'active'` cannot be a draft (`programs_draft_not_active`), and a draft picked by its id is still shown; neither asks. Blocks approved and not yet started stay in `'all'`.
- **A Stats page computes ONE scope per visit, and holds its stats query back until it knows which.** Every scope is a cache entry of its own (`['student-progress-stats', <owner>, <scope>]`) and a full read — a tree and three lists — so a page that starts on the default and switches a moment later reads the whole history under `'all'` for nothing. [`resolveStatsScope`](../src/lib/statsPrefs.js) decides from what the FIRST render can see: the URL param, else the saved scope read from localStorage during render. `'all'` and `'active'` are known at once; a program id is known when the programs list is there — kept if the list has it, `'all'` if it does not — and until then `resolved` is false and `scope` is the id that was asked for. Both surfaces ([`StudentDashboard`](../src/components/student/StudentDashboard.jsx), [`StudentStatsSection`](../src/components/coach/StudentStatsSection.jsx)) pass `{ enabled: scopeResolved }` to `useStudentProgressStats`. Three things must stay as they are. **The saved scope is read on every render, never held in state or a memo**: picking "All" clears it and the next render has to see that. **"Scope not known" counts as loading** (`!scopeResolved || isLoading`): a query that is held back is not loading for React Query (`isLoading` false, `data` undefined), and without that the page falls through to its empty state — "No program assigned yet" — until the list arrives. **A programs list that failed or is paused is not waited for** (`listUnavailable`): the page computes `'all'`, as it does for an id it cannot find, and forgets nothing of what was saved; waiting would leave a saved block on its spinner for as long as the device is offline. **A saved block lets go once another block is active.** A block is saved with the block active when it was picked (`saveStatsScope` writes `activeBlock`, `NO_ACTIVE_BLOCK` when none was); `resolveStatsScope({ …, savedActiveBlock })` answers `{ scope: 'all', resolved: true, reset: true }` for a SAVED block when a block is active now that is neither that one nor the saved block itself — `reset` is only in the answer when it is true. A block named by the URL is kept, as are `'all'` and `'active'`, a block whose active block is still the active one, and every scope while no block is active. The selector offers the active block by its id, so without the marker a block picked as "the one I am training" stayed on screen after the next one was made active, and the athlete's new training never showed. A block saved before the marker existed is kept as it was — nothing says whether it was picked as the current block or as a past one — and is given the block active now. **The effect that writes the URL waits for the scope to be known** (`scopeResolved`, no scope in the URL): it calls `settleSavedScope` (forgets the saved scope on a reset, stamps an unmarked block) and writes the scope the page COMPUTES, never the saved one. Written at mount, as it was, the saved block reached the URL before the list could say it was replaced, and the URL wins over what was saved. The stats query does not wait for the effect, the query key does not change when it runs, and a reset sends no read: the replaced block is never computed (`StudentDashboard.requests.test.jsx`).
- **The Stats page sends six reads, and the athlete's row id once.** In order: `students` (the row id), the shallow programs list, the program tree, then confirmations, done sets and swaps TOGETHER (`Promise.all` in `useStudentProgressStats` — the three filter through the program join and none needs the rows of another; the tree stays first because it decides whether they are sent at all). The history overlay ([`useStudentHistoricalSessions`](../src/hooks/useStudentHistoricalSessions.js), two more reads) is drawn in the `'active'` scope only and takes `{ enabled }` so that it is read there only: eight reads in that scope. Changing the scope adds four (the tree and the three lists of the new scope). [`src/components/student/StudentDashboard.requests.test.jsx`](../src/components/student/StudentDashboard.requests.test.jsx) mounts the page on its real hooks over `fakePostgrest` and counts; a new read on this page changes those numbers on purpose or not at all. Awaiting the three lists one after the other again fails `src/hooks/useStudentProgressStats.test.jsx` ("the three flat lists go out together").
- **An athlete's `students.id` is one cache entry, `['my-student-id', userId]`, shared through `myStudentIdQueryOptions`.** [`useStudents.js`](../src/hooks/useStudents.js) exports the read as query options. A component observes it with `useMyStudentId()`; a `queryFn` that needs the id before its own reads takes it with `queryClient.ensureQueryData(myStudentIdQueryOptions(user.id))` — the cached value, the read already in flight, or a new one — and never reads `students` itself. `useStudentProgressStats` (athlete flow) and `useStudentHistoricalSessions` do; the Stats page mounts both next to `useMyStudentId` and used to send the same request three times. The entry has `staleTime: Infinity`: nothing in the app re-creates the row an account trains under (`students` has no INSERT or DELETE policy, `transfer_student` updates the row in place). It is not in `PERSISTED_QUERY_ROOTS`, so a reload reads it again, and a failed read is not kept. An owner who deletes and re-creates a link by SQL changes the id under a session that is open: that session keeps the old id until it reloads.
- **Changing the Stats scope keeps the figures that were on screen — of the same owner only.** `useStudentProgressStats` passes `placeholderData`: while a scope that was never read loads, `data` is the previous scope's, `isPlaceholderData` is true and `isLoading` stays false. Both surfaces keep the page as it is — the selector, which already shows the new choice, stays usable — and wrap the figures in an element with `aria-busy="true"` and `opacity-50` until the new ones arrive. It is NOT React Query's `keepPreviousData`: the coach's section stays mounted from one athlete to the next, and would show athlete A's figures under athlete B's name while B loads. The function returns the previous data only when `previousQuery.queryKey[1]` — the owner, a `students.id` in the coach flow and the user id in the athlete's — is the owner being asked for. A component that reads `data` for anything it must not show stale (the history overlay is one: it is not drawn over outgoing figures) checks `isPlaceholderData`.
- **Of two sets that tie for a best, the EARLIER one holds the record.** `buildRecords` keeps the first set that reaches a value, so the sets must reach it oldest first: `readDoneSetHistory` sorts the done sets by `logged_at` (then `id`), and `useStudentRecords` reduces them in the order it receives them. Pages arrive in id order, which is arbitrary; without the sort a record matched last week could take the date of the record and light the "New PR" badge for a lift that set no record.
- **"Last time" is the session trained last, by `logged_at`, and does not depend on the order its rows arrive in.** `useLastPerformance` hands the athlete's whole history to `buildLastPerformance` (every done set, all exercises — a prior swap sits under another `exercise_id`) with the open session's `exerciseIds`, which are the only exercises that get an entry. Per exercise, the sessions are ranked by the latest `logged_at` of their sets of it, compared as instants (the stamps come as `Z`, `.000Z` and `+00:00`, which do not sort as text); a session with no stamp comes after every session with one, and `scheduledDate` then `sessionId` only break ties, so the answer is the same on every load. Two kinds of session are left out: the open session itself, and a session that STARTED AFTER it — whose first logged set, of any exercise, is later than the open session's first. `sessions.scheduled_date` takes no part: it is advice, a copy of a block carries none, and an athlete trains a session before its date; a rule that dropped the sessions dated on or after the open one (or after today) hid a session trained ahead of its date, and one that ranked by the date first put an old dated block above last week's undated copy. An open session with no logged set in the history cuts nothing, nor does a session with no stamp. The hook's third parameter (`_currentScheduledDate`) is not read and stays for its two callers. The old newest-first `.order('logged_at')` existed only so that the cut at 1000 rows kept the recent part; there is no cut any more.
- **The "Last time" hint keeps its place while it is read, so the sets do not move when it arrives.** It reads the athlete's whole history and arrives after the sets (1.55 s against 0.77 s on a gym's 4G in the live review): it pushed every row down by its height under a finger on its way to set 1. [`SessionView.jsx`](../src/components/student/SessionView.jsx) passes `lastPerformanceLoading` to the exercise shown ([`SessionExercise.jsx`](../src/components/student/SessionExercise.jsx)) — true while the `useLastPerformance` read is pending and in flight, or pending while the swaps it is keyed on are being read (`useSlotDeviations` not yet fetched and fetching). The panel of the set in progress then keeps its last line, under its rule, with an `aria-hidden` « … » in place of « 7 days ago, 5 × 5 at 17.5 kg. ». A read paused without a network, or that failed, keeps no place — it would be kept for good. An exercise without history gives its place back once the answer is there. Tests: `src/components/student/SessionExercise.test.jsx`, `src/components/student/SessionView.test.jsx`.

- **Personal records + bodyweight** (2026_07_14). [`useStudentRecords`](../src/hooks/useStudentRecords.js) reduces the athlete's done sets — taken from the shared history (`fetchDoneSetHistory`, next bullet), not read by the hook; a coach names the athlete with a `students.id`, which is what `programs.student_id` references, NOT `profiles.id`, and an athlete reading their own passes nothing — into one record per exercise via [`lib/records.js`](../src/lib/records.js): best Epley e1RM (`w × (1 + reps/30)`, but a **single rep returns the weight exactly** — a 1-rep set IS a 1RM), best single set, best reps (bodyweight PR), and a `recent` flag for a PR set in the last 14 days. `recent` is said of the number the card shows, and only of it: `bestE1rmAt` for a record that has an e1RM (the rounded `bestE1rm` that [PersonalRecords](../src/components/student/PersonalRecords.jsx) tests to pick the e1RM view), `bestRepsAt` only for a record with none. The most reps in a set move with any set at any load — a light back-off of 12, an extra set with reps and no weight — and lit "New PR" beside an e1RM that had not changed; do not put `bestRepsAt` back in the flag of a weighted record. Effective load = `actual_* ?? target_*`. **Records are SWAP-AWARE:** the history reads the athlete's `kind='swap'` `slot_deviations` alongside the sets (through the same program join, never an unbounded slot-id `.in()`) and hands a swapped set over under the SUBSTITUTE exercise — with **only the logged actuals**, because the slot's pinned `target_*` are the coach's ORIGINAL exercise's numbers and are foreign to the substitute, so they are null on such a set. The consequence is deliberate and must not be "fixed" to match Stats: a swap with no logged actual sets **no** record here, whereas [`useStudentProgressStats`](../src/hooks/useStudentProgressStats.js) still ESTIMATES volume from the prescribed reps (the student did that rep scheme, just a different movement). A PR is a MEASURED fact — crediting the substitute from the coach's prescription would fabricate one. [`useLastPerformance`](../src/hooks/useLastPerformance.js) follows the same rule. `bodyweight_logs` (one row per student per day, upsert on the LOCAL `(student_id, logged_on)` key — use `lib/day.isoDate`/`parseISODate`, never `toISOString`; student owns it keyed on `profiles.id`, coach reads via the students-join) records the bodyweight series. Both surfaced on [StudentProfile](../src/components/student/StudentProfile.jsx) via `BodyweightCard` + `PersonalRecords`; bodyweight logging is online-only (new-row insert can't queue offline), and `BodyweightCard` reads the weight with `parseDecimalInput` between 1 and 499.99 — `bodyweight_logs.weight_kg` is a `numeric(5,2)` with `CHECK (weight_kg > 0 AND weight_kg < 500)`, so 500 itself is refused before the request. **The `×bodyweight` relative-strength figure shipped on top of this series** (2026_07_23) once `exercise_library.load_mode` gave the library a way to tell ADDED load (weighted calisthenics) from FULL load (barbell): [`buildRecords`](../src/lib/records.js) takes a `bodyweightAt(dateIso)` resolver and emits `relStrength` + `bwAtBest`, rendered as a ×BW pill on [PersonalRecords](../src/components/student/PersonalRecords.jsx). Four rules hold, and none of them may be quietly relaxed. **The est-1RM headline — and PR selection and sort — stay the ADDED/logged-load e1RM**, bodyweight-independent and byte-identical to before the feature, so weight change can never manufacture or reshuffle a PR. **`relStrength` is the PEAK across all qualifying sets** of (system-load e1RM ÷ bodyweight at that set) — a true max, NOT a figure derived from the added-load PR set, because the PR set and the strongest RELATIVE single needn't be the same set (and a bodyweight-only set on an `added` movement, system load = bodyweight, still contributes). **An unclassified `load_mode` (NULL) or an unresolvable bodyweight degrades gracefully to `relStrength = null` and no pill** — never `0×` or NaN; that's why `bodyweightAt` defaults to `() => null`, which keeps every caller that passes no series on the pre-feature output. **Tonnage and volume stay bodyweight-blind by design** — ×BW and tonnage legitimately tell different stories about the same added-load lift, so do NOT "fix" one to match the other. Schema, RLS, and the `useLogBodyweight` → `['student-records']` invalidation rule are in the relative-strength entry under `set_logs` above.
- **An athlete's done-set history is read ONCE, by `fetchDoneSetHistory`, and whatever reduces the whole history derives from that read.** [`lib/doneSetHistory.js`](../src/lib/doneSetHistory.js) holds it. `readDoneSetHistory(studentRowId)` sends the done `set_logs` and the `kind='swap'` `slot_deviations` together, each to its end with `fetchAllRows`, and returns flat rows, oldest first (`logged_at`, then `id`), with the swap already applied: `exerciseId` is the exercise PERFORMED (the substitute on a swapped slot), `exercise` its `{ id, name, type, load_mode }` — or null when the reader may not see that library row —, `swapped` says which, and `target_*` are null on a swapped set, because the slot's pinned targets are the original exercise's numbers. `fetchDoneSetHistory(queryClient, { studentRowId, userId })` keeps that list in the query cache under `['student-records', 'history', <athlete>]`: the STUDENTS-table id in the coach flow, the signed-in user's id when an athlete reads their own. [`useStudentRecords`](../src/hooks/useStudentRecords.js) and [`useLastPerformance`](../src/hooks/useLastPerformance.js) call it from their `queryFn` and reduce; neither reads `set_logs` or `slot_deviations` itself. Each used to scan that five-level join on its own — "Last time" once per session opened and once more per exercise added in the session editor, 152 kB a time in production. Six rules keep it one read:
  1. **The key's root is `'student-records'` on purpose.** `refreshAfter` in `lib/offlineMutations.js` (the `onSettled` default of the confirm and undo keys, through `CONFIRMATION_READS`) and `useLogBodyweight` invalidate `['student-records']`, and that prefix is the only thing that tells the history an athlete's done sets changed. A new write path that changes what an athlete has done invalidates `['student-records']`, never a narrower key; and the history cannot move under another root without those sites learning it. `src/hooks/sharedHistory.test.jsx` drives the real `useConfirmSession` on a client that carries those defaults, and fails if a confirmation stops renewing the history.
  2. **It is read with `queryClient.fetchQuery`, never observed with `useQuery`.** With no observer an invalidation only flags the entry and the next derivation pays for the read; callers that arrive while a read is in flight share it. An observer would download the whole list again on every confirmation and every return to the tab. The price of having none: an invalidation that lands while a read is in flight restarts nothing, and the read's own success clears the flag — so the `queryFn` in `fetchDoneSetHistory` compares the entry's `isInvalidated` before and after its read and, when it went up in between, reads once more before anyone gets the list. Keep that comparison if the `queryFn` is rewritten; without it a session confirmed during the read is missing from a list held as fresh for ten minutes. It sees one invalidation per read: a second one during the repeat, or one during a read that began on an entry already flagged, waits for `HISTORY_STALE_MS`.
  3. **Between two invalidations a read is trusted for `HISTORY_STALE_MS` (10 minutes), and dropped from memory after the same time.** Sets ticked on this device since the last read are missing from it until the session is confirmed or the ten minutes have passed. "Last time" reads the open session only for the time of its first set, which cuts the sessions started after it — a session being trained has none — so this delays only what the NEXT session's hint and the records list show; a coach sees a workout the athlete finished on another device at most ten minutes late. Do not close that window by invalidating on every set tap: that is one full scan per tap, which is what this module removed.
  4. **`'student-records'` stays out of `PERSISTED_QUERY_ROOTS`.** The list runs to hundreds of kB and the persister re-serialises the whole cache on every event. What is persisted for offline use is the small object `useLastPerformance` reduces it to, under `'last-performance'`.
  5. **The athlete scope lives here.** With a `studentRowId` both reads carry `.eq('exercise_slots.sessions.weeks.programs.student_id', …)` on every page; without one NO filter is sent and RLS scopes the rows to the signed-in athlete. A coach caller MUST pass it (see the `useLastPerformance` bullet under "Coach Athletes-roster layout"). Both hooks name the athlete the same way — if one resolved a `students` row and the other did not, one athlete would get two entries and two reads.
  6. **It passes `retry: false`.** The hook that derives from it retries; inheriting the client's default too would multiply the attempts of a read that is several requests long. A failed read is not kept.

  A new figure over an athlete's whole done-set history — a coach-side records list, an e1RM trend — calls `fetchDoneSetHistory`; it does not add a scan. `useStudentProgressStats` is deliberately not a consumer: it is scoped to a block, needs the prescribed side, and ESTIMATES a swapped set from the prescription where this list holds measured numbers only.
- **A swap is a swap even when its substitute cannot be shown.** `readDoneSetHistory` decides on `slot_deviations.substitute_exercise_id`, not on the embedded library row: a set on a swapped slot belongs to the substitute's id whether or not the reader may see that exercise (one from a former coach's library comes back with no row). "Last time" works on the id alone; a record needs a name, so `useStudentRecords` sets none for a set whose `exercise` is null. Handing such a set back to the exercise prescribed would credit work — possibly a record — to a lift that was not done. For the same reason the library embed on `set_logs` is a plain one, not `!inner`: a hidden exercise must not take the set out of the history.

## Stats persistence

- **Stats page selections are persisted in URL + localStorage.** Program scope lives in the URL (`?scope=` on `/student/stats`, `?statsScope=` on `/coach/students/:id/stats`) so a refresh holds — but a tab switch drops the search params, so [lib/statsPrefs.js](../src/lib/statsPrefs.js) mirrors `{ scope, activeBlock, exerciseId }` to localStorage too (`activeBlock` — the block active when a block was picked; see "A Stats page computes ONE scope per visit" under "Stats"). The scope is decided during render by `resolveStatsScope` — the URL param wins, else the saved scope, else `'all'` — so the first render already computes the saved scope (see "A Stats page computes ONE scope per visit" under "Stats"); once the scope is known, an effect writes it into the URL and settles the saved one (`settleSavedScope`) — a saved block is forgotten when another block has been made active since it was picked. Picking a scope writes both through `saveStatsScope`; picking "All" clears both. The exercise selection inside [ExerciseProgressChart.jsx](../src/components/student/ExerciseProgressChart.jsx) reads/writes its own `localStorage` slot via the `storageKey` prop. Keys are namespaced per surface — `sl_stats_prefs:self` for the student, `sl_stats_prefs:coach:<studentId>` for each coach view — so a coach hopping between students never drags one student's selection onto another. Stale ids fall back gracefully: `resolveStatsScope` answers `'all'` for a program id the programs list does not have, and `ExerciseProgressChart` falls back through its `exercises.some(...)` check.

## Offline-safe student writes

- **Offline-safe student writes are wired through one allow-list.** Persistence + replay are handled by [`lib/queryClient.js`](../src/lib/queryClient.js) (the client, the IDB store, `persistOptions` — the one object `App.jsx` hands to `PersistQueryClientProvider` — and `shouldPersistQuery`, whose allow-list is `PERSISTED_QUERY_ROOTS` in that file — twelve roots, pinned by `src/lib/queryClient.cache.test.js` so that adding one is a decision; of `student-program-details` only the `'active'` variant, the `'all'` history tree would bloat every persist write), [`lib/persistence.js`](../src/lib/persistence.js) (which writes are stored, how, and what a stored blob gives back at the next launch — the bullets after the draft ones) and [`lib/offlineMutations.js`](../src/lib/offlineMutations.js) (mutationFns + `registerOfflineMutationDefaults`). **Persisted query data must be plain JSON** — a Set/Map silently dehydrates to `{}`; that's why `useMyConfirmedSessionIds` caches an ARRAY and derives its Set via `select`. `student-program-details` and `my-confirmed-session-ids` are load-bearing for offline cold start: they are the two reads the training list is built from, and it shows neither half without the other — the block without the confirmations would send the athlete back to the first session (the bullet "**The training list is built from two reads**" under "Training is a QUEUE"). Any new student-side mutation that must survive offline → reload → resume needs three things: a stable `mutationKey` from `MUTATION_KEYS`, its mutationFn registered in `MUTATION_FNS`, and `networkMode: 'online'` (pauses while offline, fires on reconnect). Registering the key is also what gives the write its retry policy and its session gate — see the bullets after the confirm one. **Replay ordering is scope-based:** the registered defaults carry `scope: { id: 'offline-writes' }`, so the writes whose hook sets no scope — the confirmation, a note, a deviation — replay strictly FIFO; the set-log hooks set a per-row scope (`useSetLogs.rowScope`), so two writes to the same row stay in order (done → un-done cannot land reversed) while different rows never head-of-line-block each other. React Query stores a mutation's `scope` with it (`dehydrateMutation`), so a set-log write restored after a reload KEEPS its per-row scope: after a relaunch the sets of different rows go out in parallel, and the confirmation can reach the database before the last of them — one more reason the session lock is `reviewed_at`. Anything that needs "the queue has drained" therefore looks at the mutations still pending, the way `refreshAfter` does; there is no "last one in order" to wait for. Replay-idempotency is the caller's job: a queued write replayed against a row that already exists must not trip a UNIQUE constraint, so nothing in the queue is a bare INSERT. `useSaveSlotComment` and `useSaveSlotDeviation` upsert on `exercise_slot_id` and the last writer wins; `useConfirmSession` is insert-or-nothing on `session_id` and the first writer wins (next bullet). `AppShell` calls `resumeMutationsAndReconcileDrafts` on every `online` event, which replays nothing while no session can be read; the user-facing surface is [`components/ui/OfflineBanner.jsx`](../src/components/ui/OfflineBanner.jsx), backed by [`hooks/useOnlineStatus.js`](../src/hooks/useOnlineStatus.js). **Both of those read their state, they never mirror it** — `useOnlineStatus` is a `useSyncExternalStore` over the `online`/`offline` events, and the banner's count is `useIsMutating({ predicate: isQueuedWrite })` — `isQueuedWrite` of `lib/persistence.js`, the test that also decides what the persister stores, so "N changes waiting to sync" is exactly what the device keeps. A `useState` + `mutationCache.subscribe` mirror is the trap here: React Query notifies cache listeners *synchronously*, and `useMutation` builds its observer during render, so the mirror fired `setState` while a sibling was still rendering ("Cannot update a component (OfflineBanner) while rendering a different component") — benign in dev, a tear under concurrent rendering. Video uploads are deliberately **out of scope** — `VideoUploadButton` is gated on `useOnlineStatus()` and flips to a "Connect to record video" hint when offline (Supabase Storage has no resumable-upload story we can lean on). Coach writes are not queued. Another account, or a sign-out on this device, wipes both the in-memory client and the persisted IDB blob — `ClearOnUserChange` in `App.jsx`, see "**The persisted blob says whose it is**" below; a session that merely went away wipes nothing.
- **A confirm is insert-or-nothing: the first confirmation of a session is the one that stays.** `confirmSessionFn` in [`lib/offlineMutations.js`](../src/lib/offlineMutations.js) upserts on `session_id` with `ignoreDuplicates: true` — PostgREST runs it as `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (session_id) DO NOTHING` — and reads the answer with `.maybeSingle()`. A second confirm is not exotic: it is the replay of a confirm whose first attempt reached the server but whose answer was lost, or a second device whose persisted cache still shows the session as unconfirmed. It writes nothing, so the athlete's note, `performed_on` and the `sessions.performed_at` mirror stay those of the first confirm, and the statement never reaches the UPDATE policy on `session_confirmations`. Both halves are load-bearing. Without `ignoreDuplicates` the same call is `DO UPDATE` on every column of the payload: the replay replaces the note with its own (usually NULL) and moves the training date. With `.single()` in place of `.maybeSingle()` the empty answer of a duplicate becomes a `PGRST116` error — a toast and a rolled-back screen for a session that is confirmed. The mutation therefore resolves the inserted row, or `null` for a duplicate: nothing may read its result as "the confirmation", and the reads of the confirmation are refreshed when the write settles — by `refreshAfter`, the `onSettled` default of the key, so after a relaunch too. A refusal of the INSERT itself (session archived, block no longer active) still fails the mutation. The only way to change a confirmation is undo, then confirm again — a DELETE, then a fresh INSERT, replayed in that order by the `offline-writes` FIFO scope. The cases are in `src/lib/offlineMutations.test.js`, which sends the request through the real supabase-js query builder and answers `fetch` by PostgREST's rules: a mocked `.upsert()` chain cannot tell `DO NOTHING` from `DO UPDATE`.
- **A queue replayed after the coach has switched blocks is saved for 72 hours, and refused after.** Every queued write of an athlete — the five on a set, the note, the swap or skip, confirm and unconfirm — goes through `program_active_for_session`, which since migration `2026_10_13_17_replay_grace_window.sql` answers yes for 72 hours after a block is switched off. Inside that time a replay succeeds exactly as it would have on the active block: the UPDATE returns its row, the confirmation is inserted and notifies the coach, `sessions.performed_at` follows `performed_on`. After it, and for a block in the trash, a replay fails as before — an UPDATE reaches no row (PGRST116), an INSERT is refused (`42501`) — and is not sent again. The window does not depend on when the set was tapped: the database has no proof of that time, so it is counted from the switch.
- **React Query is offline when the device is — the state is read from `navigator.onLine`, not waited for.** `onlineManager` starts at `true` and only moves on a window `online` / `offline` event, and no event fires at launch: left alone, an app opened without signal believes it is online, replays the queue restored from IndexedDB at once, and every write fails and is dropped from the next persist. `syncOnlineManager` in [`lib/queryClient.js`](../src/lib/queryClient.js) sets the manager from `navigator.onLine` when the module is evaluated — before anything can run — and again on `online`, `offline`, `pageshow` and `visibilitychange`. The last two are not decoration: iOS resumes a suspended PWA without firing `online`, and a manager stuck on "offline" holds back every read and every write. Do not seed it later (in an effect, in `App.jsx`): the persister's `onSuccess` runs before the first effect of anything below it. `navigator.onLine === false` is trusted, `true` is not — a request can still fail, which is the retry policy's business. The cases are in `src/lib/queryClient.test.js`, which evaluates the module again for each "launch".
- **A read without a network waits; it is not attempted.** Queries run with `networkMode: 'online'` (`lib/queryClient.js`). Offline, a query is `fetchStatus: 'paused'` at once — `isPaused: true`, `isLoading: false`, and no `error` — and React Query runs it on the manager's offline → online step. Data restored from IndexedDB is rendered in every mode; this setting only decides what happens to the request. Two consequences for a page. It must render "not loading, no data, no error" as *waiting for the network*, not as *empty*: testing `isLoading` alone shows the empty state the moment the page mounts offline. And a test client that claims to mirror the app's defaults says `networkMode: 'online'`. `'offlineFirst'` is the trap here: it always makes one attempt, nothing can answer it (the service worker caches no API response), and postgrest-js re-sends a failed GET after 1 s, 2 s and 4 s — so every read of an offline launch spins for 7 s, twice with `retry: 1`, and ends as an error instead of a paused read.
- **A queued write is not sent without a session, and is not dropped for lack of one.** supabase-js puts the anonymous key on a request when `auth.getSession()` gives it no session — and that is also its answer while an expired token cannot be refreshed, the usual state of a phone coming back to a bad network. The database refuses the anonymous write *with a code* (`PGRST116` on an update, `42501` on an insert), which looks like any refusal and is final. So every function of `MUTATION_FNS` ([`lib/offlineMutations.js`](../src/lib/offlineMutations.js)) first reads the session and throws `AuthNotReadyError` instead of sending; `mutationRetry` ([`lib/networkErrors.js`](../src/lib/networkErrors.js)) retries that error without limit, at most 15 s apart, and React Query turns the wait into a paused mutation as soon as the device is offline or the page hidden. Three things hang on it. (1) The gate is active on the clients `registerOfflineMutationDefaults` ran on — it is that call which makes `AuthNotReadyError` a wait; on a bare `QueryClient` it would be a permanent failure, so there the write is sent as before. A test that registers the defaults on its client must therefore give its mock of `lib/supabase` an `auth.getSession`; without one the write fails at once with a `TypeError`, on purpose. (2) `resumeMutationsAndReconcileDrafts` replays nothing and re-reads no draft while there is no session, and `useAuth` calls `replayQueuedWrites` when one appears: a `SIGNED_IN` where none was known, and every `TOKEN_REFRESHED`. Whoever rewrites the auth listener keeps that call, and keeps it un-awaited (the callback runs inside supabase-js's auth lock). (3) A waiting write sleeps between two attempts and `queryClient.clear()` cannot stop it, so before each attempt the function checks that its mutation is still in the client's cache (`mutationOf`, by identity of the variables object) and otherwise never settles: no request with the next account's session, and no `onError` rollback writing the previous account's rows into the emptied cache. Clearing the client is therefore enough to drop the queue — nothing else has to cancel anything.
- **A queued write that got no answer is sent again; one the database refused is not; and only the queue retries.** `mutationRetry` / `mutationRetryDelay` are set per mutation key by `registerOfflineMutationDefaults`, so a hook that names a `MUTATION_KEYS` key gets them live as well. *No answer* (`isTransientError`) is: `fetch` failed or the body was not JSON — postgrest-js hands both back as an object with an **empty** `code`; a gateway answered in PostgREST's place — an object with no `code`; or the database said "not now" — `PGRST000`–`PGRST003`, classes `08` and `53`, `57P01`–`57P03`, `57014`, `55P03`, `40001`, `40P01` (the schema-cache reload that ends every migration is one of them). Such a write is retried five times, 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 s apart, and a retry that comes due offline pauses the mutation instead of failing it. Any other code is a refusal and fails once. Waiting for a session does not count against the five (`rankTransientFailure`).
